| R41 | f16=IEEE754 binary16 (1/5/10 bias15); f32→f16 = round-to-nearest-even, overflow→inf, subnormals+NaN per IEEE. bf16=top 16 bits of f32 (1/8/7 bias127); f32→bf16 = RNE via bias=((u32>>16)&1)+0x7FFF then (u32+bias)>>16 (NOT truncation), NaN kept as NaN (0x7FC0 not inf); bf16→f32 = pure left-shift u32(bf16)<<16. matches our tensor/half.go, bit-exact vs numpy.float16 + torch.bfloat16 | IEEE 754-2019 §4.3; numpy.half docs; PyTorch torch/headeronly/util/BFloat16.h round_to_nearest_even; Eigen BFloat16.h (PAPER/spec = tier2 final) | high |
| R42 | mixed-precision training 3 techniques: (1) fp32 MASTER weights — optimizer updates fp32 master, fp16 copy for fwd/bwd, updates <2^-24 lost in fp16 but accumulate in master; (2) LOSS SCALING — ×S before backward, unscale ×1/S before step; (3) DYNAMIC scaler (torch/Apex defaults) init 2^16=65536, growth ×2 after 2000 clean steps, backoff ×0.5 on inf/nan AND SKIP the step (not clamp); (4) fp32 ACCUMULATION (already §V10). matches nn.MixedPrecision + nn.LossScaler + Tape.BackwardScaled | Micikevicius et al. 2018 arXiv:1710.03740 §3.1-3.3 + PyTorch torch/amp/grad_scaler.py (PAPER = tier2 final) | high |

Row-major C[M,N]=A·B via col-major C^T=B^T·A^T: cublasSgemm(h,OP_N,OP_N,N,M,K,&α,B,N,A,K,&β,C,N) — operands B THEN A, dims N,M,K, ld N,K,N. GPU-detect: cudaGetDeviceCount(&n)==cudaSuccess && n>0. matches cuda_bridge.c | docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cublas + leimao.github.io cuBLAS-Transpose (API convention = definitional, no paper §V16) | high |

DescriptorSetLayout 3 storage bindings @0,1,2 stage COMPUTE + pool + update; dims M,K,N via push constants. ShaderModule(SPIR-V)→ComputePipeline. Cmd: begin→bindPipeline→bindDescriptors→pushConstants→dispatch(ceil(N/16),ceil(M/16),1) for 16×16 local→end→submit→queueWaitIdle→read. GLSL C[y=row][x=col], GID.x=col→N, GID.y=row→M. matches vk_bridge.c + matmul.comp | registry.khronos.org/vulkan spec §dispatch/§descriptor-sets + docs.vulkan.org/tutorial Compute_Shader (API convention = definitional, no paper §V16) | high |
| R45 | op-level NPU dispatch from Go = impractical (2026). ANE: CoreML whole-model only (.mlpackage/ML Program), NO per-op API, NO gradient API, FP16 inference-only. Intel NPU: OpenVINO graph/whole-model, inference-only. DirectML: HAS per-op (CompileOperator/DML_GEMM) + training grads (DML_*_GRAD) BUT native C++/nano-COM on D3D12, no Go binding → heavy interop. ∴ NPU = MODEL-level target (export→CoreML/ONNX/OpenVINO/DirectML runner), NOT L1b op backend. T44 = documented honest non-goal, backend/npu.Available()=false | developer.apple.com/documentation/coreml/mlmodel + learn.microsoft.com directml + docs.openvino.ai npu-device (vendor docs = definitional, no paper §V16) | high |
| R46 | backend auto-select = ordered preference, first REGISTERED wins, CPU/reference always guaranteed fallback. ONNX Runtime = ordered execution-provider list tried per-op, CPU EP final fallback. PyTorch idiom = cuda if avail else mps else cpu (lib/user picks, no global auto-default). ggml/llama.cpp = backend registry w/ per-device score, GPU preferred, CPU always fallback. NPU inference-only → not a training tier. Default order cuda>metal>vulkan>cpu defensible + on-host benchmark (Apple M2 Pro/MoltenVK): vulkan matmul 1.47× (512) & 3.38× (1024) vs cpu-SIMD → GPU-first empirically justified. matches backend.Default/preference (§T46) | onnxruntime.ai execution-providers + docs.pytorch.org mps + deepwiki ggml/llama.cpp backends (API/design convention = definitional, no paper §V16) | high |
| R47 | tag-free cgo accel feasibility: (1) Metal/MPS/Foundation = macOS SYSTEM frameworks (/System/Library/Frameworks), always present → `//go:build darwin && cgo` linking -framework Metal always compiles/links, NO SDK, NO tag needed (cgo=default on native macOS). (2) CUDA cuBLAS = CUDA-Toolkit-only (libcublas+headers not on generic Linux) → link-time -lcublas breaks generic build → opt-in tag required. (3) Vulkan loader libvulkan.so.1 DESIGNED for runtime dlopen (dlsym vkGetInstanceProcAddr → resolve all fns); "apps not required to link directly to loader" = official graceful-fail pattern (volk meta-loader). ∴ CUDA/Vulkan tag-free ONLY via dlopen, not build-link. matches register_darwin.go (Metal tag-free) + register_cuda/vulkan.go (tag-gated) | developer.apple.com/documentation/metalperformanceshaders + docs.nvidia.com cuda-installation-guide-linux + KhronosGroup/Vulkan-Loader LoaderApplicationInterface.md (docs = definitional, no paper §V16) | high |
| R48 | DPO (Rafailov et al. 2023 Eq.7): L=−E log σ(β·((logπθ_w−logπref_w)−(logπθ_l−logπref_l))), σ=logistic, β temp (default 0.1), π_ref FROZEN (no grad), logπ(y\|x)=Σ per-token logprobs. implicit reward r̂=β log(πθ/πref); loss=−log σ(r̂_w−r̂_l). GRADIENT ∇θL=−β·E[σ(r̂_l−r̂_w)·(∇logπθ_w−∇logπθ_l)] → weight=σ(reversed margin)=σ(−Δ), max when model wrongly prefers rejected. −log σ(z)=softplus(−z)=log(1+e⁻ᶻ) stable. matches OpDPO kernel+VJP+nn.DPO. CONFIRMED vs paper §4/Eq.7 + HF TRL DPOTrainer (loss_type sigmoid, beta 0.1) | Rafailov et al. 2023 arXiv:2305.18290 §4 Eq.7 + huggingface.co/docs/trl/dpo_trainer (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R49 | PPO clipped surrogate (Schulman et al. 2017 Eq.7): L^CLIP=E[min(r·Â, clip(r,1−ε,1+ε)·Â)], r=exp(logπθ−logπ_old), ε default 0.2, MAXIMIZED → loss=−L^CLIP=−mean min(surr1,surr2). π_old & Â DETACHED (rollout consts, no grad) → grads only thru logπθ. CLIP = trust region: min takes pessimistic bound; when surr2 selected & r clamped outside [1−ε,1+ε] → clip flat → grad 0 (kills incentive to leave region). ∂/∂logπ=Â·r when surr1 chosen or (surr2 & r in-range), else 0. value+entropy = SEPARATE additive terms → clipped policy loss standalone. matches OpPPOClip kernel+VJP+nn.PPOClipLoss. CONFIRMED vs paper §3 Eq.6-7 + SB3/CleanRL ppo.py | Schulman et al. 2017 arXiv:1707.06347 §3 Eq.6-7 + SB3/CleanRL (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R50 | GAE (Schulman et al. 2016, arXiv:1506.02438): δ_t=r_t+γV(s_{t+1})−V(s_t) (Eq.11-12); Â_t^{GAE(γ,λ)}=Σ_{l≥0}(γλ)^l δ_{t+l} (Eq.16, exp-γλ-weighted sum of future TD residuals). backward recursion Â_t=δ_t+γλ·nonterminal·Â_{t+1} (T−1→0, accumulator reset at episode ends via nonterminal=1−done masking bootstrap+accum) = the infinite sum. λ=0→Â=δ_t (Eq.17, TD low-var/high-bias); λ=1→Â=Σγ^l r_{t+l}−V (Eq.18, MC high-var/low-bias). returns_t=Â_t+V(s_t)=value-fn target. defaults γ=0.99,λ=0.95. matches rl.GAE. CONFIRMED vs paper Eq.11-18 + CleanRL/SB3 ppo.py | Schulman et al. 2016 arXiv:1506.02438 Eq.11-18 + CleanRL/SB3 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R51 | knowledge distillation (Hinton et al. 2015, arXiv:1503.02531): soft targets p=softmax(z_teacher/T), q=softmax(z_student/T), SAME T>1 on both, teacher FROZEN. soft loss = KL(p‖q)=Σ p(log p−log q) (=CE H(p,q) up to teacher const). CRITICAL: soft-target grads scale 1/T² → multiply soft loss by T² for magnitude parity w/ hard loss. gradient dC/dz_s=(1/T)(q−p), w/ T² prefactor → T·(q−p). total = α·L_soft(T) + (1−α)·L_hard(CE, T=1), SEPARATE additive terms → soft loss standalone. matches OpDistill kernel(T²·KL, stable softmax)+VJP(T·(q−p)/N, teacher nil)+nn.DistillLoss. CONFIRMED vs paper §2-3 + PyTorch KD tutorial (sum(p·(log p−log q))·T²) | Hinton Vinyals Dean 2015 arXiv:1503.02531 §2-3 + pytorch.org KD tutorial (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R52 | label smoothing (Szegedy et al. 2016 §7 arXiv:1512.00567; Vaswani 2017 §5.4 ε=0.1): smoothed target q'(k)=(1−ε)δ(k,y)+ε·u(k), u=1/K UNIFORM over ALL K → q'(y)=(1−ε)+ε/K, q'(k≠y)=ε/K (NOT ε/(K−1)). loss = −Σ q'(k)log softmax(z)_k = lse(z)−(1−ε)z_y−(ε/K)Σ_k z_k, mean/batch. gradient ∂/∂z_k = softmax(z)_k − q'(k). ε=0 = exact hard CE. matches OpCrossEntropy label_smoothing attr (kernel+VJP) + nn.CrossEntropySmooth. CONFIRMED vs Szegedy §7 + Vaswani §5.4 + PyTorch CrossEntropyLoss (uniform-over-K) | Szegedy et al. 2016 arXiv:1512.00567 §7 + Vaswani 2017 §5.4 + PyTorch docs (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R53 | speculative decoding (Leviathan et al. 2023 arXiv:2211.17192 Alg.1/Thm.1; Chen et al. 2023 arXiv:2302.01318 Alg.2): draft x≈q, accept w/ prob min(1,p(x)/q(x)); on reject resample from residual p'(x)=max(0,p−q)/Σmax(0,p−q). LOSSLESS: output ≈ EXACTLY target p ∀ q (Thm.1), via identity min(q,p)+max(0,p−q)=p. E[accept]=Σ min(p,q)=1−TV(p,q). loop: draft K tokens, target scores K+1 in 1 parallel pass, accept L→R to first reject (emit 1 residual) else all-accept→bonus token from target[K]; 1..K+1 tokens/step all ≈p. NOTE Chen swaps p/q notation, algo identical. matches nlp.SpeculativeSample+SpeculativeRun. CONFIRMED vs both papers | Leviathan 2023 arXiv:2211.17192 + Chen 2023 arXiv:2302.01318 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R54 | beam search (Sutskever et al. 2014 seq2seq; length penalty Wu et al. 2016 GNMT arXiv:1609.08144 eq.14): maintain B best partial hyps, expand each ×V, score=Σ_t log p(y_t\|y_<t) (cumulative log-softmax), prune frontier to top-B. B=1 = greedy (argmax); unbounded B (≥V^L, no prune) = exact top sequences by total log-prob. EOS-hyp completed/removed; stop when B complete or max len. LENGTH PENALTY lp(Y)=(5+\|Y\|)^α/(5+1)^α=((5+n)/6)^α, final score=Σlogp/lp(Y), α≈0.6-0.7, α=0→lp=1 raw sum (HF variant: score/len^penalty). matches nlp.BeamSearch. CONFIRMED vs Sutskever 2014 + Wu 2016 eq.14 + HF generate | Sutskever et al. 2014 + Wu et al. 2016 arXiv:1609.08144 eq.14 + HF (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R55 | inverted dropout (Srivastava/Hinton et al. 2014 JMLR 15; PyTorch nn.Dropout): TRAIN — each unit retained w.p.(1−p), dropped (0) w.p. p via Bernoulli(1−p) mask r, kept units scaled 1/(1−p): y_i=(r_i/(1−p))·x_i. TEST — IDENTITY (disabled, no scaling). E[y_i]=(1−p)(1/(1−p))x_i+p·0=x_i (expectation preserved → no test rescale). BACKWARD — elementwise multiply by fixed mask ⇒ ∂L/∂x_i=(r_i/(1−p))·∂L/∂y_i (VJP re-applies same mask). NOTE paper's classic form uses retain-prob p + test×p; inverted/PyTorch uses drop-prob p + train 1/(1−p) — same, relabeled. matches nn.Dropout (OpMul by mask). CONFIRMED vs JMLR + PyTorch docs | Srivastava et al. 2014 JMLR 15:1929-1958 + PyTorch nn.Dropout (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R56 | gradient accumulation (PyTorch/HF Accelerate convention): split batch N into K microbatches (size m, N=Km), fwd+bwd each WITHOUT optimizer step, ADD grads, then ONE step + zero. EXACT EQUIVALENCE for MEAN-reduction loss: full-batch mean grad = (1/N)Σ∇ℓ = (1/K)Σ_k[(1/m)Σ_{i∈k}∇ℓ] = AVERAGE of K per-microbatch mean grads (grad of mean = mean of grads, linearity). 1/K scaling: accumulated sum ÷K (or loss÷K before bwd) or grads K× too large. changes only memory/compute scheduling, NOT the update math. CAVEAT: token-level/variable-length SUM-reduction losses need normalize by total tokens, not per-batch mean (like BN running-stats exception). matches nn.GradAccumulator (avg accumulated grads). CONFIRMED vs HF Accelerate (empirical 2.04==2.04) + PyTorch amp_examples (definitional convention = linearity identity, no paper §V16) | huggingface.co/docs/accelerate gradient_accumulation + docs.pytorch.org amp_examples | high |
| R57 | logit penalties: (1) REPETITION (CTRL Keskar 2019 §4.1): p_i=softmax(x_i/(T·I(i∈g))), I=θ if seen else 1 → seen-token logit ÷θ; θ≈1.2, θ=1=none. HF SIGN-AWARE refinement: score=where(score<0, score*θ, score/θ) → +logit÷θ, −logit×θ (÷ a negative logit would wrongly raise its prob). (2) OpenAI FREQUENCY: mu_j−=c_j·α_freq (scales w/ count c_j). PRESENCE: mu_j−=[c_j>0]·α_presence (flat one-off). range [−2,2], 0=none. (3) unseen tokens (i∉g / c_j=0) UNCHANGED. matches nlp.ApplyPenalties. CONFIRMED vs CTRL §4.1 + HF RepetitionPenaltyLogitsProcessor + OpenAI advanced-usage docs | Keskar et al. 2019 arXiv:1909.05858 §4.1 + HF logits_process.py + OpenAI API docs (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R58 | IPO (Azar et al. 2023 arXiv:2310.12036 Eq.17; TRL loss_type="ipo"): same log-ratio diff as DPO h=(logπθ_w−logπref_w)−(logπθ_l−logπref_l)=pi_logratios−ref_logratios (=DPO Δ pre-β). loss=mean (h−1/(2β))² — SQUARED/MSE toward finite target margin 1/(2β), NOT DPO's logistic −log σ. gradient ∂L/∂h=2(h−1/(2β)); ∂L/∂πθ_w=+2(h−1/(2β))/N, ∂L/∂πθ_l=−…, ref frozen. MOTIVATION: bounded loss regularizes to FINITE margin, avoids DPO margin→∞/overfit on deterministic prefs. canonical Eq.17 = unnormalized. matches OpIPO kernel+VJP+nn.IPO. | R59 | KTO (Ethayarajh et al. 2024 arXiv:2402.01306; TRL KTOTrainer) — UNPAIRED binary feedback (label desirable/undesirable, no pairs). reward r=β·(logπθ−logπref), π_ref frozen. reference point z_ref = β·KL_batch (β-scaled batch KL over mismatched y'), DETACHED (no backprop, clamp≥0). desirable: loss=λ_D·(1−σ(β(logratio−kl)))=λ_D·σ(z_ref−r) (reward↑); undesirable: loss=λ_U·σ(r−z_ref) (reward↓). σ logistic, defaults β=0.1, λ_D=λ_U=1. mean/batch, grads only into policy (ref/z_ref/labels frozen). value v=σ(±(r−z_ref)) = Kahneman-Tversky prospect-value. matches OpKTO(pl,rl,labels; β,z_ref,λ_D,λ_U)+VJP+nn.KTO. | R60 | ALiBi (Press Smith Lewis 2021 arXiv:2108.12409 §3) — Attention with Linear Biases, positional method for length extrapolation. STATIC non-learned bias m_h·(j−i) added to q·k/√d BEFORE softmax (causal j≤i → bias≤0, distant keys penalized most). NO positional embeddings, NO learnable params. per-head SLOPES geometric: n power-of-2 → head h (1-idx) slope 2^(−8h/n) = base^h, base=2^(−8/n); n=8 → 2^-1..2^-8. non-power-of-2 → slopes of nearest pow2 below n, then interleave (every other) slopes of 2·pow2. bias constant → NO grad to bias; backward = standard softmax-attn backward on BIASED scores (Q/K/V grads flow thru softmax(score+bias)). matches backend.ALiBiSlopes + OpMHA "alibi" attr (kernel+VJP re-add bias). LOAD-BALANCE aux loss L=α·N·Σ_i f_i·P_i: f_i=fraction dispatched to i (hard top-1 argmax count, eq.5), P_i=(1/T)Σ_t softmax(h(x_t))_i mean prob (eq.6), α=0.01. MINIMIZED uniform (f_i=P_i=1/N → N·Σ=1), imbalance ↑. f_i DETACHED non-diff → grad ONLY thru P_i. matches nn.TopKGating + OpMoEBalance+nn.MoEBalanceLoss. | R62 | sliding-window attention (Mistral Jiang et al. 2023 arXiv:2310.06825 §2; Sparse Transformer Child 2019; Longformer Beltagy 2020) — local attention for efficient long-context. window W + causal: query at abs pos i attends ONLY keys j∈[max(0,i−W+1), i] (W most recent incl. self); older + future masked → contribute 0. within window = standard scaled-dot softmax over the ≤W keys. cost O(W)/token not O(seq). RECEPTIVE FIELD grows w/ depth: after k layers info moves ≤k·W tokens (Mistral W=4096, 32 layers ≈131K span). W≥seq = full causal (no-op). matches OpMHA "window" int attr (kernel+VJP jmin=max(0,iₐᵦₛ−W+1)). | R63 | min-p sampling (Nguyen et al. 2024 arXiv:2407.01082; HF MinPLogitsWarper, llama.cpp, vLLM): truncation on the prob dist (post temperature/softmax). threshold = min_p·P_max (P_max = max token prob); KEEP tokens with prob ≥ threshold, mask others (→−inf/0), renormalize. top-1 token ALWAYS survives (P_max ≥ min_p·P_max ∀ min_p≤1 = HF min_tokens_to_keep=1 floor); min_p=0 disables. typical 0.05-0.1. ADAPTIVE: confident (high P_max) → high threshold → aggressive truncation; flat (low P_max) → low threshold → more kept (scales nucleus w/ confidence, unlike top-p fixed cumulative mass). composable w/ temperature+top-k. matches Sampler.MinP (WithMinP). | R64 | RoPE Position Interpolation (Chen et al. 2023 arXiv:2306.15595) — context-window extension. RoPE angle = m·θ_i, θ_i=base^(−2i/d). LINEAR PI: extend trained len L→L'>L by DOWN-scaling position m→m·L/L'=m/s, s=L'/L≥1 → angle (m/s)·θ_i (effective pos p/s). s=1 recovers plain RoPE. simple linear rescale, short fine-tune (≈1000 steps), phases stay in trained range. CONTRAST NTK-aware (bloc97/YaRN): scales BASE (base'≈base·s^(d/(d−2))) NOT position — distinct. BACKWARD: RoPE orthogonal → VJP = transpose rotation w/ SAME scaled angle. matches OpRoPE "pos_scale" attr (kernel+VJP pos=p/s). | R65 | Lion (EvoLved Sign Momentum, Chen et al. 2023 arXiv:2302.06675 Alg.2; lucidrains/lion-pytorch) — memory-efficient optimizer, symbolic-regression discovered. UPDATE (per step, decoupled WD): c_t = β1·m_{t−1} + (1−β1)·g_t (interp for update); θ_t = θ_{t−1} − lr·(sign(c_t) + λ·θ_{t−1}); m_t = β2·m_{t−1} + (1−β2)·g_t (momentum EMA updated AFTER, w/ β2). ONE momentum buffer (½ Adam state, no v/second-moment). sign() → every coord moves ±lr (magnitude-independent) → use lr ≈3-10× smaller than Adam, λ ≈3-10× larger. defaults β1=0.9, β2=0.99, λ=0. decoupled (AdamW-style) weight decay. sign(0)=0. matches nn.Lion + NewLion(params, lr, WithLionBetas/WithLionWeightDecay). | R66 | YaRN (Peng et al. 2023 arXiv:2309.00071 §3.1-3.3) — "NTK-by-parts" RoPE context extension, superset of linear PI (§R64). θ_i=base^(−2i/hd) i=0..hd/2−1, wavelength λ_i=2π/θ_i, r(i)=L/λ_i full rotations over trained ctx L. find_correction_dim(r)=hd·ln(L/(2π·r))/(2·ln base) inverts r→pair-index. range low=⌊fcd(β_fast=32)⌋, high=⌈fcd(β_slow=1)⌉, clamp [0,hd/2−1], +0.001 if equal. ramp γ_i=clamp((i−low)/(high−low),0,1). blend inv_freq_i=(1−γ_i)·θ_i+γ_i·(θ_i/s), s=scale: γ=0 (small i, high-freq, λ≪L) EXTRAPOLATE θ_i unchanged; γ=1 (large i, low-freq, λ≥L) INTERPOLATE θ_i/s; ramp between. (HF form: inv_freq=interp·(1−mask)+extrap·mask, mask=1−γ ⇒ mask=1 extrapolate — sign-consistent.) attn temperature m_scale=0.1·ln(s)+1 for s>1 (§3.3), mult q,k / logits. backward = orthogonal rotation w/ same per-dim angle. matches backend.RoPEFreqs (kernel+VJP) + YaRNAttnScale + OpRoPE yarn_* attrs. Go SIMD: avo AVX2/512 .s ≈3× scalar; Go1.26 GOEXPERIMENT=simd/archsimd (Float64x8/AVX-512, amd64-only, inlinable, >4× w/ inline, API unstable). memory-bound (softmax/LN/attn) under bandwidth roof → fuse+contiguous+arena (Williams Roofline). | R69 | GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization, Shao et al. 2024 DeepSeekMath arXiv:2402.03300 §4.1 eq3-4; DeepSeek-R1) — critic-free policy-gradient RL. (1) group advantage: for G sampled outputs/prompt w/ scalar rewards r_1..r_G, Â_i=(r_i−mean(r))/(std(r)+eps), std=POPULATION (ddof=0, verl compute_grpo_outcome_advantage), eps=1e-4 (TRL); broadcast identically to every token of o_i (OUTCOME supervision); replaces value net. (2) J=(1/G)Σ_i(1/\|o_i\|)Σ_t{min(ρ_{i,t}·Â_i,clip(ρ_{i,t},1−ε,1+ε)·Â_i)−β·D_KL}, ρ=π_θ/π_θold token-ratio, PPO-style clip on ratio, Â_i const across tokens; loss=−J. (3) KL = Schulman k3 UNBIASED: D_KL=exp(Δ)−Δ−1, Δ=logπ_ref−logπ_θ per token (NOT naive log-ratio); SUBTRACTED as explicit −β·D_KL penalty INSIDE objective (not folded into reward, unlike PPO-RLHF). (4) ε=0.2, β=0.04 (DeepSeekMath G=64; R1-style ≈0.001; TRL default 0). grad→π_θ only (∂kl/∂logπθ=1−exp(Δ)). matches nn.GroupAdvantage+OpGRPO+nn.GRPOLoss. | R70 | DoRA (Weight-Decomposed Low-Rank Adaptation, Liu et al. 2024 arXiv:2402.09353 eq5, ICML'24 Oral; PEFT dora.py; NVlabs/DoRA) — refines LoRA by decomposing weight into magnitude+direction. W'=m·V/‖V‖_c, V=W0+ΔW=W0+(α/r)·BA; ‖·‖_c = per-COLUMN/per-output-neuron L2 norm (PEFT torch.linalg.norm(weight,dim=1) on [out,in] = over in-axis → length-out vector); m[out] trainable magnitude. INIT m=‖W0‖_c ⇒ B=0⇒V=W0⇒W'=W0 EXACTLY (no-op start). forward (PEFT): mag_norm_scale=(m/‖V‖_c); result=(scale−1)·base+scale·lora. reduces to LoRA when m frozen at ‖V‖_c (scale=1). BACKWARD: paper §4.3 + PEFT DETACH ‖V‖_c (treat as const, ≈24% mem save, negligible acc loss) → detached grad dV=m·g/n, dm=Σg·V/n. OUR CHOICE: exact through-norm grad dV=(m/n)(g−V·s/n²) dm=s/n (s=Σg·V) → passes §V2 finite-diff (DoRA def=eq5 forward; detach is optional opt, forward identical). matches nn.DoRALinear + OpDoRAWeight. CONFIRMED unanimous 4-agent vs paper eq5/§4.3 + PEFT peft/tuners/lora/dora.py + NVlabs/DoRA | Liu et al. 2024 arXiv:2402.09353 eq5 + PEFT dora.py (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R71 | SimPO (Meng 2024 arXiv:2405.14734 eq5-7; princeton-nlp/SimPO) + ORPO (Hong 2024 arXiv:2403.07691 eq6-7; TRL orpo_trainer) — reference-free preference losses (research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous). BOTH consume LENGTH-AVERAGED chosen/rejected logps (avglogp=Σlogp/\|y\|), NO reference model. SimPO: reward r=β·avglogp; z=β(avgW−avgL)−γ (γ subtracted INSIDE σ); loss=−logσ(z)=softplus(−z); β∈[2,2.5] γ∈[0.5,1.4] (γ/β≈0.3-0.5); grad ∂/∂avgW=−β·σ(−z), ∂/∂avgL=+β·σ(−z). ORPO: monolithic SFT+odds-ratio, NO separate SFT phase; P=exp(avglogp), odds=P/(1−P); logOR=(avgW−avgL)−(log(1−Pw)−log(1−Pl)), log(1−P)=log1p(−exp(avg)); L=L_SFT+λ·L_OR, L_SFT=−avgW (NLL chosen), L_OR=−logσ(logOR); λ default 0.1 (TRL exposes as `beta`, loss=nll−β·logσ(logOR)); grad ∂/∂avgW=−1−λσ(−logOR)/(1−Pw), ∂/∂avgL=λσ(−logOR)/(1−Pl). matches nn.SimPO/OpSimPO + nn.ORPO/OpORPO. CONFIRMED unanimous vs papers + princeton-nlp/SimPO simpo_trainer.py + TRL cpo/orpo_trainer.py | Meng 2024 arXiv:2405.14734 + Hong 2024 arXiv:2403.07691 + TRL (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R72 | FlashAttention-2 (Dao 2023 arXiv:2307.08691 Alg.1 fwd + Alg.2 bwd; Triton 06-fused-attention) — exact online-softmax tiled attention, O(N) extra mem (research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous). FWD per query row, streaming K/V blocks j: S_j=(Qᵢ·K_jᵀ)/√dk; m⁽ʲ⁾=max(m⁽ʲ⁻¹⁾,rowmax S_j); P=exp(S_j−m⁽ʲ⁾); ℓ⁽ʲ⁾=e^{m⁽ʲ⁻¹⁾−m⁽ʲ⁾}·ℓ⁽ʲ⁻¹⁾+rowsum(P); O⁽ʲ⁾=e^{m⁽ʲ⁻¹⁾−m⁽ʲ⁾}·O⁽ʲ⁻¹⁾+P·V_j; init m=−∞,ℓ=0,O=0; FINAL Oᵢ=O⁽ˡᵃˢᵗ⁾/ℓ⁽ˡᵃˢᵗ⁾ divide ONCE post-loop (FA-2 tweak vs FA-1 per-block rescale). scale 1/√dk (paper folds into Q). causal: skip K-blocks wholly past row i, mask within diagonal block only. EXACT — \"returns correct O=softmax(QKᵀ)V with no approximation\", differs from naive only by FP reassociation (f64 1e-12 valid, bit-exact NOT guaranteed). BWD (Alg.2, = standard softmax-attn bwd): D_i=rowsum(dO∘O)=Σ_j P_ij dP_ij; dV_j=Pᵀ dO, dP=dO Vᵀ, dS_ij=P_ij(dP_ij−D_i), dQ=dS·K/√dk, dK=dSᵀ·Q/√dk. matches OpFlashAttn (reuses mhaVJP). CONFIRMED vs paper Alg.1/2 + tridao flash-attn + Triton tutorial | Dao 2023 arXiv:2307.08691 Alg.1/2 + Triton 06-fused-attention (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R73 | KV-cache eviction (StreamingLLM Xiao 2023 arXiv:2309.17453 §3.1-3.2 + H2O Zhang 2023 arXiv:2306.14048 §4.1 Alg.1; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous). STREAMINGLLM: cache = first S "attention sink" tokens (default S=4, kept PERMANENTLY) + rolling most-recent R; evict middle; size S+R. sink cause: softmax sums to 1 → excess attention dumped onto always-visible initial tokens regardless of content; ≈4 sinks restore window-attn perplexity. CRITICAL: RoPE positions = position-WITHIN-cache (contiguous 0..S+R−1) NOT original text index (cache[0,1,2,3,6,7,8] decode tok9→pos[0..7]); key_position_ids=arange(kv_seq_len). state: sink_count S + window R. H2O: fixed budget k; per-key eviction score = ACCUMULATED attention = col-sum of post-softmax matrix score(j)=Σ_i A[i,j] over query rows; greedy keep union(recent-r, top heavy-hitters by score), evict lowest-score non-recent when over budget (dynamic-submodular Alg.1). state: budget k + recent r + per-token accumulated-attention accumulator (update each step). matches nlp.StreamingKeep/H2OScores/H2OKeep/GatherRows/EvictStreaming. CONFIRMED vs papers + mit-han-lab/streaming-llm kv_cache.py StartRecentKVCache + FMInference/H2O H2OKVCache_LayerWise | Xiao 2023 arXiv:2309.17453 §3 + Zhang 2023 arXiv:2306.14048 §4.1 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R74 | MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention, DeepSeek-V2 Liu 2024 arXiv:2405.04434 §2.1.1-2.1.3 eqs9-19; HF modeling_deepseek.py; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous). Low-rank KV compression: c^KV=W_DKV·h (dim dc, SOLE cached latent, dc≪n_h·d_h); k^C=W_UK·c^KV, v^C=W_UV·c^KV (per-head content). DECOUPLED RoPE key k^R=RoPE(W_KR·h) SHARED single-head dim dR, broadcast/concat to every head's key. Query: c^Q=W_DQ·h, q^C=W_UQ·c^Q, q^R=RoPE(W_QR·c^Q) per-head. per head q=[q^C;q^R] k=[k^C;k^R]; score=(q^C·k^C+q^R·k^R)/√(dh+dR), o=Σ_j softmax_j·v^C_j, u=W_O·[o_1..o_nh]. dims (DeepSeek-V2): d_h=128, dc=512(=4d_h), dR=64(=d_h/2), d'_c=1536, n_h=128 → cache 576·l ≈93.3% cut vs MHA 2n_h·d_h·l. WEIGHT-ABSORB (W_UK→W_UQ, W_UV→W_O) = inference-only fusion avoiding K,V materialization; NAIVE un-absorbed fwd IDENTICAL result → implement/test first. decoupled-RoPE rationale: RoPE matrix between W_Q and W_UK non-commutative → W_UK can't be absorbed, so separate shared RoPE dim added. matches nn.MLA + OpMLA (RoPE internal). | R75 | NF4 (4-bit NormalFloat) quantization + QLoRA (Dettmers et al. 2024 arXiv:2305.14314 §3; bitsandbytes functional.py; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous). NF4 CODEBOOK (16 exact values, idx7=0.0 exact): -1.0,-0.6961928009986877,-0.5250730514526367,-0.39491748809814453,-0.28444138169288635,-0.18477343022823334,-0.09105003625154495,0.0,0.07958029955625534,0.16093020141124725,0.24611230194568634,0.33791524171829224,0.44070982933044434,0.5626170039176941,0.7229568362236023,1.0 (bitsandbytes create_normal_map offset=0.9677083 use_extra_value=True). construction: N(0,1) quantiles via norm.ppf, asymmetric 8neg+9pos share 0.0, normalized by absmax→[-1,1]; q_i=½(Q_X(i/17)+Q_X((i+1)/17)); info-theoretically OPTIMAL for zero-centered normal (equal prob mass/bin). BLOCKWISE quant (default blocksize=64): absmax=max\|w\|, normalize w/absmax∈[-1,1], store nearest-code 4-bit idx + fp32 absmax. dequant w_hat=absmax·code[idx]. DOUBLE-QUANT optional (compress_statistics: 8-bit absmax, 2nd blocksize=256, +offset); naive single-quant = correct baseline. QLoRA = NF4-frozen base + LoRA adapter (only adapter trains). ROUND-TRIP: dequant(quant(w)) snaps w→absmax·nearest-code (argmin), IDEMPOTENT (re-quant → same idx/absmax). matches nn.QuantizeNF4/DequantizeNF4/nf4Codes + nn.QLoRALinear. CONFIRMED vs paper §3 + bitsandbytes functional.py quantize_4bit/dequantize_4bit | Dettmers et al. 2024 arXiv:2305.14314 §3 + bitsandbytes functional.py (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R76 | Mamba selective-scan (S6) (Gu & Dao 2023 arXiv:2312.00752 §3.2 Alg.2; state-spaces/mamba selective_scan_ref; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous). Linear-time O(L·D·N) input-dependent SSM, attention alternative. params: Δ=softplus(proj+Δ_bias)>0 [L,D]; A=−exp(A_log) [D,N] real neg (stable Ā∈(0,1)); B,C input-dep [L,N] SHARED over D channels; x[−1]=0. ZOH discretize: Ā=exp(Δ⊗A)=exp(einsum bdl,dn→bdln); B̄=SIMPLIFIED Δ·B (NOT exact (Ā−I)A⁻¹B), ref folds u: B̄u=Δ·B·u. RECURRENCE h[t,d,n]=Ā·h[t−1]+B̄u; y[t,d]=Σ_n C[t,n]·h[t,d,n] + D_skip[d]·u[t,d] (D_skip optional). BACKWARD reverse scan (dx[L]=0): dx[t]=Cᵀdy[t]+Ā[t+1]dx[t+1]; dC=Σ_d h·dy; du=D·dy+Σ_n B̄·dx; dĀ=dx·h[t−1]; dΔ,dA,dB via Ā=exp(ΔA),B̄=ΔB chains — = exact grad, matches finite-diff. z-gate (out·SiLU(z)) + conv1d + in/out-proj are OUTSIDE the core scan (full Mamba block = follow-up). matches OpSSM + reverse-scan VJP. NO draft model: the drafter COPIES a continuation from a reference — in PLD the reference is the running sequence (prompt+output): take the last n-gram suffix, find an EARLIER occurrence, propose the tokens that FOLLOWED it (up to max draft len). ETA (§4.4): η=min(ε, √ε·exp(−H)), α=√ε, H=−Σp·ln p = Shannon entropy in NATS (natural log) over the (pre-truncation) distribution; allowed A={x: p(x)>η}. | R95 | Dr. GRPO ("GRPO Done Right", Liu et al. 2025 "Understanding R1-Zero-Like Training: A Critical Perspective" arXiv:2503.20783 §3.1; = sail-sg/understand-r1-zero; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent). Paper: "simply remove the 1/\|oᵢ\| and std normalization terms" from GRPO. (1) ADVANTAGE: Âᵢ=rᵢ−mean(r), NO /(std+ε) (vs GRPO §R71) → removes QUESTION-LEVEL DIFFICULTY BIAS (÷std rescales every group to unit variance, amplifying low-variance near-all-correct/wrong prompts). (2) LOSS NORM: drop per-response 1/\|oᵢ\|, use a CONSTANT normalizer 1/(G·L) (masked_sum, constant_normalizer=max_len) → removes RESPONSE-LEVEL LENGTH BIAS (favoring longer wrong / shorter right). Keeps critic-free group sampling + PPO clipped-ratio surrogate + optional KL (paper β=0). Result: unbiased, prevents runaway length inflation, token-efficient. GoAI: DrGRPOAdvantage = std-free advantage; GRPOLoss already uses a GLOBAL token mean (constant, not per-\|oᵢ\|) so it carries no length bias → DrGRPOAdvantage+GRPOLoss(β=0) = Dr. GRPO. matches nn.DrGRPOAdvantage | Liu et al. 2025 arXiv:2503.20783 §3.1 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R96 | NEFTune noisy-embedding fine-tuning (Jain et al. 2023 "NEFTune: Noisy Embeddings Improve Instruction Finetuning" arXiv:2310.05914 Alg.1 NeurIPS; = HF integrations/neftune.py neftune_post_forward_hook; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent vs paper + HF + neelsjain/NEFTune). After the token-embedding lookup produces X_emb [L,d] (L=seq len, d=embed dim), add scaled uniform noise: X_emb ← X_emb + (α/√(L·d))·ε, ε≈Uniform(−1,1) i.i.d. per entry. mag=α/√(L·d) (HF: dims=L·d, mag_norm=α/√dims, uniform_(−mag_norm,+mag_norm)). α = tuned noise scale (paper sweeps {5,10,15}). TRAINING ONLY (guarded by module.training), OFF at inference/generation. Applied to the embedding-layer OUTPUT (transformer input), NOT later hidden states. Noise is a per-step additive constant (sampled independent of θ) → gradients flow normally to the embeddings. matches nn.NEFTune | Jain et al. 2023 arXiv:2310.05914 Alg.1 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R97 | StreamingLLM / attention sinks (Xiao et al. 2023 "Efficient Streaming Language Models with Attention Sinks" arXiv:2309.17453 ICLR'24 §3; = mit-han-lab/streaming-llm; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 4-agent vs paper + ref impl). BOUNDED KV-cache = first `start_size` sink tokens (default 4) + rolling `recent_size` window; overflow evicts the MIDDLE: cat[k(0:start), k(seqlen−recent:seqlen)] (kv_cache.py StartRecentKVCache). RoPE detail (critical for rotary models): keys cached BEFORE rotary; at attention time key_position_ids=arange(kv_seq_len) = position WITHIN the post-eviction cache, NOT absolute text pos → relative distances stay in trained range (pos_shift/modify_llama.py). ATTENTION-SINK phenomenon: initial tokens absorb excess attention (softmax must sum to 1) regardless of relevance; keeping ≈4 sinks stabilizes PPL over millions of tokens, evicting them collapses it (§3 Fig.4). Constant-memory streaming to 4M+ tokens beyond pretrain length; does NOT extend usable context (evicted middle unrecoverable). matches nlp.StreamCache/StreamStep/StreamGenerate | Xiao et al. 2023 arXiv:2309.17453 §3 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R98 | Lookahead optimizer (Zhang, Lucas, Ba & Hinton 2019 "Lookahead Optimizer: k steps forward, 1 step back" arXiv:1907.08610 NeurIPS Alg.1; = michaelrzhang/lookahead; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent). META-optimizer wrapping any base ("fast") optimizer. Two weight sets: fast θ (base opt updates), slow φ; φ₀=θ₀. Per outer step: θ←φ (sync), run base opt k inner steps on θ, then φ←φ+α(θ_{t,k}−φ)=(1−α)φ+α·θ (Polyak EMA ONCE per k steps, NOT per-step), then θ←φ (reset). k=sync period (paper default 5), α=interpolation (paper default 0.5; ref code 0.8). Inference uses SLOW weights φ (generalize better). Optimizer-agnostic (SGD/Adam/… unchanged), negligible overhead; reduces variance, robust to LR. matches nn.Lookahead | Zhang et al. 2019 arXiv:1907.08610 Alg.1 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R99 | Q6_K k-quant format (ggml/llama.cpp; ggml-common.h block_q6_K + ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q6_K/quantize_row_q6_K; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent vs source, NOT /deep-research). 256-element SUPER-BLOCK (QK_K=256) of 16 sub-blocks×16, ≈6.56 bits/w; used STANDALONE and as the high-precision tensors of the Q4_K_M/Q5_K_M mixes → gates loading most modern downloadable GGUF models. block_q6_K layout (210 B): ql[128] (low 4 bits of the 256 quants), qh[64] (high 2 bits, 4 packed/byte), scales[16] int8 (per-sub-block), then f16 d (super-block scale). DEFINITIONAL source, no paper | ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q6_K + ggml-common.h block_q6_K (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R100 | Q4_K k-quant format (ggml/llama.cpp; ggml-common.h block_q4_K + ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q4_K/get_scale_min_k4; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent vs source, zero disagreements, NOT /deep-research). The DOMINANT modern GGUF weight format: 256-element SUPER-BLOCK of 8 sub-blocks×32, ≈4.5 bits/w, the bulk tensors of the Q4_K_M/Q4_K_S mixes (paired with Q6_K §R99 for the few high-precision tensors). Unlike SYMMETRIC Q6_K (scale only), Q4_K is ASYMMETRIC AFFINE: each sub-block carries a 6-bit scale AND a 6-bit min → y = (d·sc6)·nibble − (dmin·min6), nibble∈[0,15]. block_q4_K layout (144 B): f16 d (super-block scale-of-scales), f16 dmin (super-block scale-of-mins), scales[12] (8 six-bit scales + 8 six-bit mins bit-packed), qs[128] (256 4-bit quants). get_scale_min_k4(j,q)→(sc,min): j<4 → sc=q[j]&63, min=q[j+4]&63; j≥4 → sc=(q[j+4]&0xF)\|((q[j−4]>>6)<<4), min=(q[j+4]>>4)\|((q[j]>>6)<<4). DEQUANT: for pair=0..3 (is=2·pair): q=qs[pair·32:+32]; low nibble → sub-block is+0 (y=d·sc6·(q&0xF)−dmin·min6), high nibble → sub-block is+1 (y=d·sc6·(q>>4)−dmin·min6), 32 each. matches format/gguf dequantQ4_K (READ = ggml-exact, golden-decoded vs spec formula) + getScaleMinK4 + putScaleMinK4 (bit-exact inverse of the 6-bit splice) + quantizeQ4_K (VALID inverse encoder: per-sub-block scale=(hi−lo)/15, offset min=−min(lo,0)≥0 clamped non-positive like ggml — so floor≤0, Q4_K targets zero-crossing weights; scale-of-scale d=max(scale)/63, min-of-min dmin=max(min)/63; NOT ggml's iterative make_qkx2_quants → round-trips within k-quant error ≈R/15 (R=super-block range), not bit-identical). DEFINITIONAL source, no paper | ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q4_K + get_scale_min_k4 + ggml-common.h block_q4_K (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |

| R102 | Q5_K k-quant format (ggml/llama.cpp; ggml-common.h block_q5_K + ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q5_K; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent vs master, zero disagreements, NOT /deep-research). ≈5.5 bits/w, the bulk tensors of the Q5_K_M/Q5_K_S mixes (2nd-most-common download after Q4_K_M; paired with Q6_K §R99). IS Q4_K's ASYMMETRIC AFFINE (§R100) — same 6-bit scale AND 6-bit min per sub-block, IDENTICAL get_scale_min_k4 packing — but 5-bit: each quant q5∈[0,31] gains a high bit from a qh plane → y=(d·sc6)·q5−(dmin·min6), q5=nibble\|(highbit<<4). block_q5_K layout (176 B, qh BEFORE qs): f16 d, f16 dmin, scales[12] (scales+mins), qh[32] (1 high bit/quant), qs[128] (256 low nibbles). DEQUANT (per pair p=0..3, is=2p): u1=1<<2p, u2=2<<2p; low nibble sub-block is+0 → y=d1·((qs[l]&0xF)+(qh[l]&u1?16:0))−m1; high nibble is+1 → y=d2·((qs[l]>>4)+(qh[l]&u2?16:0))−m2; 32 each; qs+=32 per pair. So qh[l] bit 2p = 5th bit of the low-nibble quant, bit 2p+1 = 5th bit of the high-nibble quant. matches format/gguf dequantQ5_K (READ = ggml-exact, GOLDEN high-bit decode vs spec formula) + quantizeQ5_K (VALID inverse encoder: 5-bit grid scale=(hi−lo)/31, min=−min(lo,0) clamped non-positive, scale-of-scale d=max(scale)/63, min-of-min dmin=max(min)/63, 5th bit split into qh; NOT ggml's iterative make_qkx2_quants → round-trips within k-quant error, not bit-identical). NOTE: per-element error = 5-bit grid ≈R/62 PLUS the 6-bit min (offset) quant ≈amax/126 (fuzz surfaced this — bound needs an amax term, not just R). DEFINITIONAL source, no paper | ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q5_K + ggml-common.h block_q5_K (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R103 | Q3_K k-quant format (ggml/llama.cpp; ggml-common.h block_q3_K + ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q3_K; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent vs master, NOT /deep-research). ≈3.44 bits/w, the bulk tensors of the Q3_K_M/Q3_K_L/Q3_K_S mixes (run large models on limited hardware). SYMMETRIC (single super-block scale d, NO min, unlike Q4_K/Q5_K): each 3-bit quant q3∈[0,7] → y=d·(sc6−32)·(q3−4), q3=(2 low bits from qs)\|(1 high bit from hmask)<<2; the per-sub-block scale sc6−32∈[−32,31] is SIGNED. block_q3_K layout (110 B, d LAST): hmask[32] (high bit/quant), qs[64] (2 low bits/quant), scales[12] (16 six-bit scales, aux/kmask splice), f16 d. SCALE UNPACK (verbatim aux[4], kmask1=0x03030303, kmask2=0x0f0f0f0f): tmp=aux[2]; aux[2]=((aux[0]>>4)&k2)\|(((tmp>>4)&k1)<<4); aux[3]=((aux[1]>>4)&k2)\|(((tmp>>6)&k1)<<4); aux[0]=(aux[0]&k2)\|(((tmp>>0)&k1)<<4); aux[1]=(aux[1]&k2)\|(((tmp>>2)&k1)<<4). DEQUANT: for nb∈{0,1} (n=0,128), shift=2j (j=0..3), m<<=1 PER j (bits 0..7 over the super-block, NOT per 128-block — research corrected this), two 16-groups per j (q[l],q[l+16]); y=d·(sc6[is]−32)·((q[l]>>shift)&3 − (hmask[l]&m ? 0 : 4)) — INVERTED hmask (bit SET→sub 0, CLEAR→sub 4); is++ per group, q+=32 per 128-block. matches format/gguf dequantQ3_K (READ = ggml-exact, GOLDEN decode + high-bit vs spec formula) + q3kUnpackScales/packScalesQ3_K (bit-exact inverse of the aux splice) + quantizeQ3_K (VALID inverse encoder: symmetric signed sub-scale max(posmax/3,negmax/4) so neither [−4,3] extreme clips, non-negative sub-scales, scale-of-scale d=maxScale/31; NOT ggml's iterative make_q3_quants → round-trips within k-quant error ≈A/6, not bit-identical). DEFINITIONAL source, no paper | ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q3_K + ggml-common.h block_q3_K (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R104 | Q2_K k-quant format (ggml/llama.cpp; ggml-common.h block_q2_K + ggml-quants.c dequantize_row_q2_K; research-lite CONFIRMED unanimous 3-agent vs master, zero discrepancies, NOT /deep-research). ≈2.63 bits/w, the SMALLEST mainstream quant (Q2_K/Q2_K_S mixes → fit very large 70B–405B models on tight hardware). Error is GRID-dominated (R/6, R=range); the 4-bit min-quant cross-sub-block term (≈amax/30) is smaller. DEFINITIONAL source, no paper. | R105 | AdEMAMix optimizer (Pagliardini, Ablin & Grangier 2024 arXiv:2409.03137 "The AdEMAMix Optimizer: Better, Faster, Older"; apple/ml-ademamix pytorch/ademamix.py; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Eq(AdEMAMix)+Apple ref code, NOT /deep-research). AdamW with a SECOND, much slower momentum EMA mixed in → old gradients stay relevant far longer than one fast EMA allows (a single EMA cannot be both responsive AND long-memoried; two can). THREE state buffers: fast m1 (β1≈0.9), SLOW m2 (β3≈0.9999), second moment ν (β2≈0.999). EMAs: m1=β1·m1+(1−β1)g; m2=β3·m2+(1−β3)g; ν=β2·ν+(1−β2)g². UPDATE θ−=lr·[(m1/(1−β1ᵗ) + α·m2)/(√(ν/(1−β2ᵗ))+ε) + λ·θ] — α multiplies the SLOW m2; ONLY m1 and ν are bias-corrected, m2 is NOT (it intentionally warms up from zero); decoupled AdamW weight decay λ. Denominator √ν/√(1−β2ᵗ)+ε ≡ √(ν/(1−β2ᵗ))+ε (identical, bc2>0). defaults β1=0.9,β2=0.999,β3=0.9999,ε=1e-8; α: Apple code default 2.0, paper LLM experiments α∈[4,10] (5–8 typical) — we default α=5. WARMUP schedulers (training-from-scratch stability, NOT the core update): α linear α(t)=min(t·α/Tα,α) start 0; β3 half-life interp f(β)=ln0.5/ln(β+ε)−1, β3(t)=f⁻¹((1−a)f(β1)+a·f(β3)), a=t/Tβ3 (β_start=β1) — LEFT TO CALLER (this impl is the constant-α, constant-β3 core, ≡ fully-warmed-up regime). matches nn.AdEMAMix + NewAdEMAMix + WithAdEMAMix{Betas,Alpha,Eps,WeightDecay}. §V16 tier-1: TestAdEMAMixAlgorithmParity (independent straight-line recurrence @1e-12) + TestAdEMAMixSlowMomentumPersists (m2 keeps driving updates 50 zero-grad steps after fast EMA decayed) + DecoupledWeightDecay + ConvergesQuadratic (5e-3 tol: slow-EMA lag near optimum is the paper's property, not a bug). CORRECTIONS to initial assumptions: code default α=2.0 (not 5–8); β3-warmup starts specifically from β1 | Pagliardini/Ablin/Grangier 2024 arXiv:2409.03137 + apple/ml-ademamix pytorch/ademamix.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R106 | Cautious optimizer / C-AdamW (Liang, Chen, Zhang, Ding, Zhai, Liu et al. 2024 arXiv:2411.16085 "Cautious Optimizers: Improving Training with One Line of Code"; github.com/kyleliang919/C-Optim c_adamw.py; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent vs paper Alg.2 + reference code, NOT /deep-research). A one-line MASK on any base optimizer's step: zero every coordinate whose proposed update would move the parameter AGAINST the current gradient's descent direction, so the optimizer never fights the fresh gradient → provably faster convergence (≈1.4–1.5× on LLM pretrain), optimizer-agnostic. MASK φ=𝟙(u_t⊙g_t>0) STRICT >0, where u_t=m̂/(√v̂+ε) is the adaptive step SUBTRACTED from θ (θ←θ−lr·φ⊙u_t·rescale); keeps coords where the update agrees in sign with the current gradient g_t (NOT −u_t; momentum tracks g so aligned = descent). WEIGHT DECAY λ·θ is OUTSIDE the mask — decoupled AdamW, params·=(1−lr·λ) applied separately, never cautious-gated. RESCALE (paper-vs-code DISCREPANCY, research-flagged): PAPER Alg.2 L11 = d/(‖φ‖₀+1); official CODE = mask/mask.mean().clamp(min=1e-3) ≡ d/max(sum φ,1e-3·d), NO +1. We implement the CODE form (byte-faithful reference authority per §V16 when paper≠code) → all-aligned reduces to AdamW EXACTLY (scale=1, vs paper's d/(d+1)≈1); the 1e-3·d floor caps amplification when nearly all coords masked. Reduces to base AdamW when φ=all-ones; key property = freeze anti-descent coords (m̂·g_t≤0). matches nn.CautiousMask (reusable primitive: in-place zero+rescale, returns kept) + nn.CautiousAdamW + NewCautiousAdamW + WithCautious{Betas,Eps,WeightDecay}. float64 state (§V10). §V16 tier-1: nn/cautious_test.go — CautiousMask (kept/zeroed/rescale exact), CautiousAdamWAlgorithmParity (independent recurrence @1e-12), FreezesMisalignedStep (small anti-grad after built-up momentum → coord moves by wd only; large anti-grad flips m̂ so realigns — documented), ConvergesQuadratic, ExampleCautiousAdamW. DESIGN NOTE (autonomy rule): chose code-faithful rescale over paper's +1, documented here | Liang et al. 2024 arXiv:2411.16085 Alg.2 + github.com/kyleliang919/C-Optim c_adamw.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R107 | LR schedules: WSD (Warmup-Stable-Decay) + inverse-sqrt/Noam (research-lite CONFIRMED WSD 3-agent unanimous vs paper; Noam definitional). (A) WSD (Hu/Tu/Han et al. 2024 "MiniCPM" arXiv:2404.06395 §4.2 Eq.1) — modern cosine-alternative for LLM pretrain. THREE phases over step s, peak η, warmup W, decay-start S, half-life H: WSD(s)=(s/W)·η for s<W [LINEAR warmup, s/W NOT (s+1)/W → reaches η at s=W]; =η for W≤s<S [STABLE constant plateau]; =η·0.5^((s−S)/H) for s≥S [EXPONENTIAL decay, halves every H steps]. DEFAULT decay = exponential (paper ablation beats linear/cosine), MiniCPM half-life≈5000 steps≈20B tok, peak η=0.01. Decay DURATION≈10% of total ("10% Steps are Enough", 2.5% falls short) = a training-config choice, NOT a param of f. NO explicit η_min floor (exponential → ≈0; clamp externally if wanted). KEY vs cosine: long constant stable phase → no preset total T, CONTINUE/BRANCH from any stable checkpoint, only final ≈10% decayed. Boundaries continuous (s=W: warmup=stable=η; s=S: stable=decay=η). CORRECTION vs initial assumption: warmup is s/W not (s+1)/W (differs from nn.WarmupCosine's (s+1)/W convention — documented). (B) InverseSqrt/Noam (Vaswani et al. 2017 "Attention Is All You Need" §5.3) — lr=scale·dModel^(−1/2)·min(sₙ^(−1/2), sₙ·W^(−3/2)), sₙ=step+1 (1-indexed); linear rise then 1/√step decay, PEAK at sₙ=W value scale·(dModel·W)^(−1/2), never reaches 0 (slow tail). matches nn.WSD + nn.InverseSqrt (stateless fns, sibling to nn.WarmupCosine). §V16 tier-1: nn/schedule_test.go — WSD exact @1e-12 at all phase boundaries+interior + flat-plateau + continuity; InverseSqrt vs independent formula @1e-12 + closed-form peak + monotonicity + scale-linearity; Example{WSD,InverseSqrt} | Hu et al. 2024 arXiv:2404.06395 §4.2 Eq.1 + Vaswani 2017 §5.3 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R108 | Quantized KV cache Q8_0 (ggml/llama.cpp type_k/type_v=GGML_TYPE_Q8_0; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent vs ggml-quants.c + KVQuant arXiv:2401.18079, NOT /deep-research). Stores K/V cache in 8-bit per-block Q8_0 instead of f32 → ≈3.76× memory cut (34 B/32-block=1.0625 B/elem vs 4), the dominant long-context inference cost. FAITHFUL approach: STANDARD per-block Q8_0 (blocks of 32 along the contiguous row, symmetric, d=amax/127 f16 + 32 int8, NO per-channel/per-token grouping beyond the 32-block) applied IDENTICALLY to BOTH K and V — exactly the weight Q8_0 layout (§R94, already tier-2 verified). 8-bit is the NEAR-LOSSLESS "safe/simple" tier: the per-channel-Key/per-token-Value asymmetry of KVQuant (Key outlier channels) only matters SUB-4-bit (KVQuant PPL LLaMA-7B: 4-bit +<0.02, 3-bit +0.07, 2-bit +0.33; 8-bit strictly better, measured ≈+0.004 ppl q8_0/q8_0) → per-channel NOT required at 8-bit. Layout 34 B/block (2-byte f16 scale + 32 int8), static_assert-matched to weights. matches nlp.QuantKVCache (Append quantizes each row via gguf.Quantize(Q8_0); Keys/Values dequantize to f32 [t,dim]; Bytes footprint). NO new numerical algorithm — applies verified Q8_0 (§R94) to the cache tensors → acceptance by §V15 round-trip+fuzz + attention parity vs f32. §V15: TestQuantKVCacheRoundTripFuzz (200 iters random dim∈32..256/scale/nTok → cache==direct gguf round-trip bit-exact AND within amax/254+f16 bound). §V16 tier-1: TestQuantKVCacheAttentionParity (OpMHA over Q8_0 cache == over f32 K/V within 2e-2, near-lossless) + memory-ratio + error cases + Example. K/V both Q8_0 (some llama.cpp configs pair q8_0-K/q4_0-V for lower bits — not needed at 8-bit) | ggml-quants.c block_q8_0 + llama.cpp #5932 + KVQuant Hooper 2024 arXiv:2401.18079 (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R109 | Gradient checkpointing / activation rematerialization (Chen, Xu, Zhang, Guestrin 2016 "Training Deep Nets with Sublinear Memory Cost" arXiv:1604.06174; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §4.1/§4.3/§5.1 + torch.utils.checkpoint, NOT /deep-research). MECHANISM: a checkpointed segment does NOT store its intermediate activations on the FORWARD (keeps only inputs+final outputs); on BACKWARD its forward is RE-RUN from the saved inputs to regenerate the activations, which feed the local VJPs, then are freed. CORRECTNESS: computes MATHEMATICALLY IDENTICAL gradients to standard backprop — trades one extra forward per segment for memory, NO approximation; recomputation must be DETERMINISTIC (same inputs→same activations) → with deterministic ops the grads are BIT-FOR-BIT identical. MEMORY SCHEDULE: checkpoint every √n of n layers → activation memory O(n)→O(√n) for ≈one extra forward (≈30% runtime; paper: 1000-layer ResNet 48G→7G). CAVEAT: non-determinism (dropout RNG, device moves, fwd/bwd divergence) → silently WRONG grads unless RNG stashed/restored (torch preserve_rng_state); for deterministic ops NO caveat. matches autograd.Checkpoint (first forward on the tape's non-recording ctx → intermediates GC'd; records ONE checkpoint node; backward re-runs fn on a private recording sub-tape, seeds it with the parent output cotangents, runs its reverse pass, propagates input grads) + CheckpointFunc (must be deterministic). Additive tape change: node gains optional ckpt field, backward split into seed+runBackward with a checkpoint branch — existing op-VJP path unchanged. §V16 tier-1: autograd/checkpoint_test.go — TestCheckpointGradientsIdentical (checkpointed dX/dW1/dW2 == non-checkpointed BIT-IDENTICAL via != over a relu-MLP segment + FEWER tape nodes proving intermediates not retained) + TestCheckpointStacked (composed segments, grads bit-identical) + Errors + ExampleCheckpoint (Σx²→2x). NOTE: dropout inside a checkpointed segment breaks determinism (documented on CheckpointFunc) — RNG-stashing = follow-up if stochastic segments needed | Chen et al. 2016 arXiv:1604.06174 §4.1/§4.3/§5.1 + torch.utils.checkpoint (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R110 | Sharpness-Aware Minimization SAM (Foret, Kleiner, Mobahi, Neyshabur 2021 "Sharpness-Aware Minimization for Efficiently Improving Generalization" arXiv:2010.01412 ICLR'21; davda54/sam + google-research/sam; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.2/Eq.3 + ref code, NOT /deep-research). A training METHODOLOGY (wraps any base optimizer) that seeks FLAT minima (better generalization) via a two-step per-update. PERTURBATION (Eq.2, general p-norm): ε̂=ρ·sign(g)·\|g\|^(q−1)/‖g‖_q^(q−1), 1/p+1/q=1; DEFAULT p=2 → ε̂=ρ·g/(‖g‖₂+ε) with GLOBAL L2 norm over ALL params concatenated (NOT per-layer). PROCEDURE: (1) g=∇L(w); (2) ascend w_adv=w+ε̂ (climbs the gradient); (3) g_adv=∇L(w_adv); (4) RESTORE w, then base-opt step using g_adv (Eq.3 ∇L_SAM≈∇L(w+ε̂)) — the first g is used ONLY for ε̂ then DISCARDED, descent uses g_adv. davda54 ref: first_step scale=ρ/(grad_norm+1e-12), e_w=g·scale, save+add; second_step restore p, base.step() with perturbed-point grads. ≈2× compute (two fwd/bwd per step). default ρ=0.05, ε=1e-12. ASAM (Kwon 2021 arXiv:2102.11600) = ADAPTIVE variant (elementwise \|w\|² rescale of e_w) — NOT vanilla, follow-up. matches nn.SAM + NewSAM(params,base,opts) + WithSAM{Rho,Eps} + SAMOption. CONTRACT: the grads closure must return a SNAPSHOT (like tape.Grad) since base-opt applies g_adv AFTER restore (documented on Step). §V16 tier-1: nn/sam_test.go — AlgorithmParity (independent two-step recurrence @1e-12) + UsesPerturbedGradient (SAM moves strictly farther than plain SGD: g_adv=g·(1+ρ/‖g‖)) + RestoresWeights (perturbation fully undone) + ConvergesQuadratic (within O(ρ) of the min = flat-region behavior) + ExampleSAM (w=1→0.895). Vanilla p=2 SAM | Foret et al. 2021 arXiv:2010.01412 Eq.2/Eq.3 + davda54/sam (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R111 | Regex/FSM-guided constrained decoding (Willard & Louf 2023 "Efficient Guided Generation for Large Language Models" arXiv:2307.09702, the Outlines method; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §3 Alg.2/3/4 + Outlines RegexGuide, NOT /deep-research). Guarantees the LLM output matches a regex BY CONSTRUCTION. METHOD (Def.1 + Alg.2): compile regex→FSM (Q,Σ,δ,q0,F); each step MASK next-token logits so only tokens keeping the FSM in a live (non-dead) state are allowed, then ADVANCE the state by consuming the chosen token's characters. TOKEN VALIDITY (Alg.3): a token is allowed iff walking ALL its characters through the FSM from the current state hits no dead transition (δ=∅ → reject). EFFICIENCY (Alg.3/Alg.4, §3 — NOT §4 which is the CFG/pushdown extension): PRECOMPUTE an index σ:Q→P(V) (state→valid tokens + resulting state) by stepping each token through the FSM → O(1) per-step mask vs O(\|V\|) regex tests. TERMINATION: EOS allowed / generation may stop ONLY when the state ∈ F (accepting). GUARANTEE: every produced string ∈ L(regex). matches nlp.RegexGuide — FSM = the stdlib regexp/syntax NFA (pure Go, no cgo): a state = ε-closed set of NFA program counters, interned to int ids; state→token transitions memoized lazily (= the σ index, O(1) amortized); Start/Accepting/Allowed/Advance/MaskLogits. Empty-width assertions (^,$,\b) treated permissively as ε (documented; common structured-output patterns don't use them); pattern matches the ENTIRE output (accept = full match, since the prog is anchored at prog.Start and accept states have no trailing wildcard). §V16 tier-1: nlp/guided_test.go — Masking (digit/non-digit/multi-char validity) + MaskLogitsAndTermination (EOS only at accept, −Inf mask) + AlwaysMatchesFuzz (THE guarantee: 8 patterns × 300 random guided generations → every accept-terminated output fully matches the regex via Go regexp) + StateInterning + ExampleRegexGuide. Follow-ups: CFG/GBNF grammars (Alg.4 pushdown, §4), byte-level tokens (partial UTF-8), sampler wiring | Willard & Louf 2023 arXiv:2307.09702 §3 Alg.2/3/4 + Outlines RegexGuide (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R112 | Weight-space averaging: SWA + weight-EMA (research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs arXiv:1803.05407 + torch.optim.swa_utils, NOT /deep-research). Both average parameters in weight space → flatter/wider optimum, better generalization than the raw last-iterate. (A) SWA (Stochastic Weight Averaging, Izmailov/Podoprikhin/Garipov/Vetrov/Wilson 2018 arXiv:1803.05407): EQUAL-weight running average of parameter snapshots captured periodically (end of each cyclical/high-constant-LR cycle). Online update w_SWA ← w_SWA + (w − w_SWA)/(n+1); n+=1 = arithmetic mean of snapshots (torch get_swa_avg_fn `averaged + (param−averaged)/(num_averaged+1)`). (B) weight-EMA (Polyak & Juditsky 1992; ubiquitous in diffusion + LLM eval): EXPONENTIAL average w_EMA ← decay·w_EMA + (1−decay)·w every step, init to current weights, decay≈0.999–0.9999 (torch get_ema_avg_fn `decay*averaged+(1−decay)*param`, default 0.999). SWA's post-hoc BatchNorm update_bn recompute is ONLY for BatchNorm running stats → NOT needed for LayerNorm/RMSNorm transformers (no running stats) — documented. matches nn.SWA (Update folds a snapshot, Weights() = mean, N()) + nn.EMA (Update, Weights(), Decay); both keep a float64 master copy (§V10), read live Params on Update, never mutate the training params. NO gradients (pure averaging) → §V16 tier-1 = exact-formula parity. §V16 tier-1: nn/weightavg_test.go — SWAEqualWeightAverage (== arithmetic mean @1e-12) + SWAMatchesTrueMean (200 random snapshots) + EMARecurrence (independent recurrence @1e-12, init=weights) + MultiParamNoMutation (shapes preserved, snapshot independent of later edits) + Example{SWA,EMA} | Izmailov et al. 2018 arXiv:1803.05407 + torch.optim.swa_utils (get_swa_avg_fn/get_ema_avg_fn) (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R113 | z-loss / log-Z softmax regularizer (Chowdhery et al. 2022 "PaLM" arXiv:2204.02311 §5 Training Instability; origin Mesh-TensorFlow softmax z-loss Shazeer 2018; ST-MoE router z-loss Zoph et al. 2022 arXiv:2202.08906 Eq.5; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs both papers, NOT /deep-research). A standard LLM-pretraining STABILIZER: adds L_z = z_coeff·(log Z)² per token, Z=Σ exp(logit)=softmax normalizer so log Z=logsumexp(logits), mean over tokens, ADDED to cross-entropy (L_tot=L_CE+z_coeff·(logsumexp)²). Penalising log Z toward 0 keeps the softmax normalizer from drifting → prevents logit explosion, markedly improves bf16/fp16 numerical stability. DEFAULT z_coeff=1e-4 (PaLM). GRADIENT: d/d(logit_j)(logsumexp)²=2·logsumexp·softmax_j → per-token grad 2·z_coeff·log Z·softmax(logits), ÷tokens for the mean. Distinct from label smoothing (targets uniform-mix) and weight decay (param L2). Used in PaLM, ST-MoE, Baichuan, OLMo 2, Chameleon. ST-MoE ROUTER z-loss = SAME form on router logits, coeff 1e-3 (§V16-noted follow-up = standalone op on arbitrary logits). matches nn.CrossEntropyZLoss + backend.CrossEntropyAttrs.ZLoss — FUSED into the existing OpCrossEntropy (reuses its stable lse + softmax machinery, T82-pattern): kernel adds ZLoss·lse² per row (§ref/crossentropy.go), VJP adds 2·ZLoss·lse·softmax to the CE gradient (§autograd/vjp_linalg.go). ZLoss<0 rejected. §V16 tier-1: nn/zloss_test.go — Value (CE+z-loss == independent recompute @1e-12; coeff 0 == plain CE; positive penalty) + Gradcheck (§V2 finite-diff vs tape grad incl. the z-loss VJP term) + Validation + ExampleCrossEntropyZLoss. Ref-only kernel (cpu/metal fall back §I4) | Chowdhery et al. 2022 arXiv:2204.02311 §5 + Zoph et al. 2022 arXiv:2202.08906 Eq.5 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R114 | RetNet retention (Sun/Dong/Huang/Wang et al. 2023 "Retentive Network: A Successor to Transformer for LLMs" arXiv:2307.08621; microsoft/torchscale multiscale_retention.py; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.5/6/7/8 + torchscale, NOT /deep-research). Attention alternative: replaces softmax with a γ-DECAY mask → three EQUIVALENT forms (parallel train / recurrent O(1) decode / chunkwise). PARALLEL (Eq.5): Retention=(QKᵀ⊙D)V, D_nm=γ^(n−m) if n≥m else 0 (γ⁰=1 diag, 0 above); NO softmax. RECURRENT (Eq.6): state S_n∈R^{d×d}, S_n=γ·S_{n−1}+K_nᵀV_n (outer product, S_0=0), out=Q_n·S_n → O(d²) memory, no [L,L] matrix. DUALITY: parallel≡recurrent EXACTLY (S_n,ij=Σ_{m≤n}γ^(n−m)K_mi V_mj ⇒ out_nj=Σ_{m≤n}γ^(n−m)(Σ_i Q_ni K_mi)V_mj = parallel). CHUNKWISE (Eq.7): inner-chunk parallel + cross-chunk γ-recurrent carry (3rd form, follow-up). MSR (Eq.8, follow-up): per-head γ=1−2^(−5−arange(h)); MSR=(swish(XW_G)⊙GroupNorm_h(concat heads))W_O — GN+swish gate BLOCK-level. CRITICAL: torchscale's scale-invariant retention-score GroupNorm (qk/abs().sum().clamp) is NOT in core Eq.5 → OMITTED so the pure forms are exactly dual (research-confirmed safe). matches backend.OpRetention (fused ref kernel, parallel Eq.5) + RetentionAttrs{Gamma} + autograd VJP (dQ/dK/dV analytic, softmax-free attention grad) + nn.Retention (parallel, differentiable) + nn.RetentionRecurrent (Eq.6 O(1) inference) + nn.RetentionDecays (Eq.8 γ). §V16 tier-1: nn/retention_test.go — Duality (parallel==recurrent @1e-10 over γ∈{0,0.5,0.9,0.99,1}×4 shapes = THE property) + Golden (== independent recompute @1e-12) + Gradcheck (§V2 Q/K/V finite-diff @1e-4) + Decays + Errors + ExampleRetention. Ref-only kernel (GPU + MSR block + xPos = follow-ups; parallel form is matmul-shaped so a GPU kernel mirrors OpMHA) | Sun et al. 2023 arXiv:2307.08621 Eq.5/6/8 + microsoft/torchscale (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R115 | LU decomposition with partial pivoting + derived determinant/solve/inverse (Golub & Van Loan "Matrix Computations" §3.2 LU/GEPP + §3.4 det; LAPACK dgetf2/dgetrs/dgetri; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs LAPACK reference + G&VL, NOT /deep-research). Definitional numerical algorithm (no paper; canonical textbook/LAPACK = §V16 tier-2). LU/GEPP right-looking Doolittle: factor P·A=L·U (L unit-lower, U upper); per column k pivot p=argmax_{i≥k}\|A[i,k]\| (max-abs, stability), swap rows k,p (record permutation + swap count), m=A[i,k]/A[k,k] stored in L[i,k], A[i,j]−=m·A[k,j] for i,j>k. DET=sign·Π_k U[k,k], sign=(−1)^(#swaps); singular (det=0) iff any U[k,k]=0. SOLVE A·x=b: permute Pb, forward-subst L·y=Pb (unit-L, y[i]=Pb[i]−Σ_{j<i}L[i,j]y[j]), back-subst U·x=y (x[i]=(y[i]−Σ_{j>i}U[i,j]x[j])/U[i,i]). INVERSE: one LU + solve A·X=I column-by-column (explicit inverse discouraged vs solving but correct when the inverse itself is needed). Partial pivoting (not complete) is the standard for general dense matrices (bounds growth factor). matches linalg.LU/Factor/Det/Solve/Inverse + free Solve/Inverse/Det. §V16 tier-1: linalg_test.go — SolveResidual (A·x==b @1e-9) + InverseIdentity (A·A⁻¹==I) + Det (hand 2×2 ad−bc, triangular ∏diag, det(I)=1, det(A)·det(A⁻¹)=1) + MultipleRHS + Singular/shape errors | Golub & Van Loan §3.2/§3.4 + LAPACK dgetf2/dgetrs/dgetri (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R116 | QR decomposition via Householder reflections + least-squares (Golub & Van Loan §5.1-5.2 Householder/QR + §5.3.3 least-squares; LAPACK dgeqrf/dorgqr/dgels; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs LAPACK dlarfg/dgeqrf/dgels + G&VL, NOT /deep-research). Definitional (canonical textbook/LAPACK = §V16 tier-2). For m×n A (m≥n): reduced A=Q·R, Q∈R^{m×n} orthonormal cols (QᵀQ=I), R∈R^{n×n} upper. HOUSEHOLDER reflector H=I−β·v·vᵀ maps x→α·e₁: α=−sign(x₀)·‖x‖₂ (sign OPPOSITE x₀ so v₀=x₀−α=x₀+sign(x₀)‖x‖ avoids cancellation), v=x−α·e₁, β=2/(vᵀv), R[k,k]=α. RIGHT-LOOKING: per col k build reflector from A[k:m,k], apply H_k to trailing A[k:m,k:n] (A_sub−β·v·(vᵀA_sub)), store v below diag. Q=H_0·…·H_{n−1} (reflectors applied in reverse onto I's first n cols). LEAST-SQUARES min‖Ax−b‖ (m≥n): c=Qᵀb (reflectors forward), back-solve R·x=c[0:n], residual=‖c[n:m]‖ — stabler than normal eqns AᵀAx=Aᵀb. Householder QR backward-stable (unlike classical Gram-Schmidt). matches linalg.QR + Lstsq. §V16 tier-1: qr_test.go — QRReconstruct (Q·R==A @1e-9 + QᵀQ==I + R upper, over m×n=1..10×1..8) + LstsqSquare (A·x==b) + LstsqOverdetermined (normal eq AᵀAx==Aᵀb @1e-8) + LineFit (exact y=2x+1) + errors | Golub & Van Loan §5.1-5.3 + LAPACK dgeqrf/dgels (REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R117 | SVD via one-sided Jacobi (Hestenes) (Golub & Van Loan §8.6.3, Demmel "Applied Numerical Linear Algebra" §5.4.3, Hestenes J.SIAM 6:1958; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs G&VL/Demmel/Hestenes, NOT /deep-research). Definitional numerical algorithm (canonical textbook = §V16 tier-2). Reduced A=U·Σ·Vᵀ, p=min(m,n): U [m×p] orthonormal cols, Σ=diag(σ₁≥…≥σ_p≥0), V [n×p] orthonormal. METHOD: orthogonalize A's COLUMNS by right-applied Jacobi rotations A←A·J (accumulate V←V·J from I). Per pair i<j: α=‖aᵢ‖², β=‖aⱼ‖², γ=aᵢᵀaⱼ; if γ≈0 skip; else ζ=(β−α)/(2γ), t=sign(ζ)/(\|ζ\|+√(1+ζ²)) (smaller root \|θ\|≤π/4), c=1/√(1+t²) (NOT 1/(1+t²) — a common typo), s=c·t; aᵢ'=c·aᵢ−s·aⱼ, aⱼ'=s·aᵢ+c·aⱼ (exactly zeros aᵢ'ᵀaⱼ'). SWEEP cyclic-by-row until every \|γ\|/√(αβ)<tol (quadratic convergence). EXTRACT σⱼ=‖aⱼ‖ (final col norms), uⱼ=aⱼ/σⱼ, V=accumulated; sort σ DESCENDING, permute U/V cols; σⱼ=0→arbitrary uⱼ. m<n handled by SVD(Aᵀ) then swap U↔V. Backward-stable, HIGH RELATIVE ACCURACY for small σ (avoids κ²(A) of forming AᵀA). matches linalg.SVD. §V16 tier-1: svd_test.go — Reconstruct (U·Σ·Vᵀ==A + UᵀU=I + VᵀV=I + σ descending≥0, over m×n tall/square/wide 1..10) + Diagonal (σ=sorted \|diag\|) + rank-1 (σ=[5,0]) + errors | Golub & Van Loan §8.6.3 + Demmel §5.4.3 + Hestenes 1958 (REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R118 | SVD-derived matrix quantities + matrix norms (numpy.linalg pinv/matrix_rank/cond/norm; Golub & Van Loan §2.3 norms + §5.5.2 pseudoinverse; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs numpy docs + G&VL, NOT /deep-research). Definitional (numpy/textbook = §V16 tier-2), all derived from the §R117-verified SVD. PINV (Moore-Penrose A⁺): A⁺=V·Σ⁺·Uᵀ, Σ⁺=diag(1/σᵢ if σᵢ>cutoff else 0), cutoff=rcond·σ_max, numpy default rcond=1e-15. Satisfies the 4 MP props (AA⁺A=A, A⁺AA⁺=A⁺, (AA⁺)ᵀ=AA⁺, (A⁺A)ᵀ=A⁺A); full-rank tall → A⁺=(AᵀA)⁻¹Aᵀ (=least-squares). RANK=count(σ>tol), tol=σ_max·max(m,n)·eps (numpy default, eps≈2.22e-16 f64). COND₂=σ_max/σ_min, +∞ if σ_min=0. NORMS: Frobenius=√Σaᵢⱼ²; induced-1=max_j Σ_i\|aᵢⱼ\| (max COL sum); induced-∞=max_i Σ_j\|aᵢⱼ\| (max ROW sum); induced-2=σ_max. matches linalg.Pinv/Rank/Cond/Norm2/NormFro/Norm1/NormInf. §V16 tier-1: derived_test.go — PinvMoorePenrose (4 props @1e-8 tall/square/wide + A⁺b==Lstsq) + PinvEqualsInverse + Rank (I/rank-1/zero/singular) + Cond (I=1, diag=2.5, singular=+Inf) + Norms (hand-computed Fro/1/∞/2) | numpy.linalg docs + Golub & Van Loan §2.3/§5.5.2 (REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R119 | Symmetric eigendecomposition (eigh) via cyclic Jacobi (numpy.linalg.eigh / LAPACK dsyev / gonum EigenSym; Golub & Van Loan §8.5.1-8.5.2 Jacobi eigenvalue method; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs numpy/LAPACK/gonum + G&VL, NOT /deep-research). Definitional (numpy/textbook = §V16 tier-2). For real SYMMETRIC A: A=V·diag(w)·Vᵀ, w real eigenvalues ASCENDING (w₀≤…≤wₙ₋₁), V orthogonal (VᵀV=I) COLUMNS = orthonormal eigenvectors, A·V[:,k]=w[k]·V[:,k]. CYCLIC (two-sided) JACOBI: A←JᵀAJ zeroing off-diag pairs (p,q), V←V·J from I; τ=(A_qq−A_pp)/(2A_pq), t=sign(τ)/(\|τ\|+√(1+τ²)), c=1/√(1+t²), s=t·c (SAME (c,s,t) as the one-sided Jacobi SVD §R117); quadratic convergence; unconditionally stable for symmetric input, high relative accuracy for small eigenvalues. numpy eigh reads ONE triangle (does NOT symmetrize); distinct eigenvalues → eigenvectors unique up to sign. matches linalg.Eigh (wraps the tested internal/linalg.SymEig — used by PCA §T25 sklearn-golden + GaLore — reversing SymEig's DESCENDING to numpy's ASCENDING + laying eigenvectors as V columns). §V16 tier-1: eigh_test.go — Reconstruct (A·V=V·diag(w) eigenvalue-eq + A=VΛVᵀ + VᵀV=I + w ascending, n=1..8) + Diagonal + Known2x2 ([[2,1],[1,2]]→w=1,3) + non-symmetric/non-square errors | numpy.linalg.eigh + LAPACK dsyev + Golub & Van Loan §8.5 (REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R120 | Cholesky decomposition + SPD solve (numpy.linalg.cholesky / LAPACK dpotrf+dpotrs / gonum mat.Cholesky; Golub & Van Loan §4.2.1-4.2.5 Cholesky-Banachiewicz; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous zero-contradiction vs numpy/LAPACK + G&VL, NOT /deep-research). Definitional (numpy/textbook = §V16 tier-2). For real SYMMETRIC POSITIVE-DEFINITE (SPD) A: A=L·Lᵀ, L LOWER-triangular with L[i,i]>0 (numpy default UPLO='L'). CHOLESKY-BANACHIEWICZ recurrence: for j=0..n−1: L[j,j]=√(A[j,j]−Σ_{k<j}L[j,k]²); for i=j+1..n−1: L[i,j]=(A[i,j]−Σ_{k<j}L[i,k]·L[j,k])/L[j,j]. NO pivoting (SPD needs none; numerically stable). SPD TEST: factor succeeds ⟺ A SPD; the √ argument becomes ≤0 EXACTLY when A not positive-definite (LAPACK dpotrf returns INFO=j at the failing leading minor of order j) — the standard definitional SPD check. numpy.linalg.cholesky assumes A symmetric (reads one triangle, no symmetrize), raises LinAlgError on non-SPD. SPD SOLVE A·x=b (dpotrs): forward-sub L·y=b (y[i]=(b[i]−Σ_{k<i}L[i,k]y[k])/L[i,i]) then back-sub Lᵀ·x=y (x[i]=(y[i]−Σ_{k>i}L[k,i]x[k])/L[i,i]). ≈n³/3 flops = ≈2× faster than LU (2n³/3); standard direct solver for SPD systems (normal equations, covariance, Gaussian processes). matches linalg.Cholesky/CholSolve (validates square+symmetric rel-tol, input never mutated). §V16 tier-1: cholesky_test.go — Reconstruct (A=L·Lᵀ + L lower-tri + L[i,i]>0, n=1..8 on A=B·Bᵀ+nI) + CholSolve (A·x=b residual + ==LU Solve + multi-RHS [n,k]) + NonSPD ([[1,2],[2,1]] indefinite + negative diag rejected) + non-symmetric/non-square errors + ExampleCholesky | numpy.linalg.cholesky + LAPACK dpotrf/dpotrs + Golub & Van Loan §4.2 (REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R121 | CPO — Contrastive Preference Optimization (Xu et al. 2024 arXiv:2401.08417 eq3-5; TRL CPOTrainer; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Eq.2/3/5 + TRL docs, NOT /deep-research). Reference-free alignment loss folding preference + SFT into one term (origin: machine translation, general alignment). Consumes SUMMED sequence log-probs (DPO-style, NOT length-normalized — TRL loss_type="sigmoid" sums; only ipo/simpo average). L_prefer=−logσ(β(logπ_w−logπ_l))=softplus(−β(lw−ll)), NO reference model (π_ref=uniform prior U, cancels — Eq.3). L_NLL=−logπ_w (NLL/behavior-cloning on CHOSEN only — Eq.4). L_CPO=mean(L_prefer+λ·L_NLL) (Eq.5; λ=TRL cpo_alpha default 1, paper implicit 1). β default 0.1. grad: ∂(−logσ(z))/∂z=σ(z)−1 → ∂L/∂lw=−β·σ(−z)−λ, ∂L/∂ll=+β·σ(−z) (each /N). vs SimPO (§R71): SimPO length-NORMALIZED reward + target margin γ + NO NLL (=CPO with cpo_alpha=0 but averaged); CPO summed + BC regularizer. matches nn.CPO/OpCPO. §V16 tier-1: cpo_test.go — Parity vs independent numpy @1e-12 + AlphaZero=preference-only + GradCheck (finite-diff w/l) + TrainsPreference (descent raises logπ_w, lowers logπ_l) + errors + ExampleCPO | Xu 2024 arXiv:2401.08417 + TRL CPOTrainer (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R122 | (IA)³ — Infused Adapter by Inhibiting and Amplifying Inner Activations (Liu et al. 2022 arXiv:2205.05638 §3.3; HF PEFT ia3; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §3.3 + PEFT docs, NOT /deep-research). Lightweight PEFT: THREE learned per-feature vectors per block — l_k∈R^{d_k} rescales attention KEYS, l_v∈R^{d_v} rescales VALUES: softmax(Q(l_k⊙Kᵀ)/√d_k)(l_v⊙V); l_ff∈R^{d_ff} rescales FFN hidden: (l_ff⊙γ(W1·x))W2, γ=FFN nonlinearity, before down-proj. Each is ELEMENT-WISE multiply (rescaling), NOT bias/matrix. Core op y=l⊙x, (l⊙x)_{i,j}=l_j·x_{i,j}, l broadcast over seq/batch. INIT TO ONES → adapted model initially == pretrained (identity); only the 3 vectors/block train, all pretrained weights FROZEN; tiny count L(d_k+d_v+d_ff) enc (≈0.01% for T0). grad: dx=g⊙l, dl=Σ_{seq/batch}(g⊙x) (canonical Hadamard-broadcast backprop). Far cheaper than LoRA (§R27)/DoRA (§R70). matches nn.IA3/OpIA3 (rescale [...,d] by l[d], the atomic (IA)³ op; three placed on K/V/FFN in a full adaptation). §V16 tier-1: ia3_test.go — InitIsIdentity + Rescale (independent recompute, rank-2 [tok,d] + rank-3 [b,seq,d]) + GradCheck (finite-diff x + scale) + Trains (fits per-feature target, MSE→0, recovers scale) + errors + ExampleIA3 | Liu 2022 arXiv:2205.05638 + HF PEFT (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R123 | TIES-Merging — TrIm, Elect Sign & Merge (Yadav et al. 2023 arXiv:2306.01708 §3/Alg.1, NeurIPS; mergekit; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §3 + mergekit, NOT /deep-research). Merges N models fine-tuned from a shared base θ_init into one, resolving interference. (1) TASK VECTORS τ_t=θ_t−θ_init per model. (2) TRIM per-parameter-tensor (NOT global): keep top density·numel entries by \|magnitude\|, rest→0 (paper k=20% ⇒ density 0.2). (3) ELECT SIGN per coordinate γ_p=sign(Σ_t τ̂_t^p) (greater total magnitude wins). (4) DISJOINT MERGE: τ_merged^p=(1/\|A_p\|)Σ_{t∈A_p} τ̂_t^p, A_p={t:sign(τ̂_t^p)=γ_p} — MEAN over the sign-AGREEING count (÷\|A_p\|, NOT ÷N; matches paper + mergekit normalize=True default); no agreeing entry ⇒ 0. (5) θ_merged=θ_init+λ·τ_merged, λ default 1 (tuned [0.8,1.8]). Beats plain task-vector averaging (which cancels opposing edits). Pure tensor math (no autograd op — one-shot post-training utility). matches nn.TIESMerge(base, models, density, lambda). §V16 tier-1: merge_test.go — SingleModel identity (density1/λ1 recovers the model) + SignElection (hand {+3,+1,−2}→mean{+3,+1}=2, discard −2) + Trim (top-density by \|mag\|) + Lambda (nonzero base + λ=0.5 hand) + ZeroSumStaysBase (perfect cancel → base) + errors | Yadav 2023 arXiv:2306.01708 + mergekit (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R124 | DARE — Drop And REscale (Yu et al. 2024 arXiv:2311.03099 §3.1, ICML, "LMs are Super Mario"; mergekit; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §3.1 + mergekit, NOT /deep-research). Per-model task-vector sparsification applied BEFORE merging. δ=θ_ft−θ_pre. DROP: i.i.d. per-parameter Bernoulli — keep w.p. 1−p, drop (→0) w.p. p (paper m≈Bernoulli(p), δ̃=(1−m)⊙δ ≡ keep-mask Bernoulli(1−p)). RESCALE: survivors ÷(1−p), factor EXACTLY 1/(1−p) (δ̂=δ̃/(1−p); mergekit `res/=density`, density=1−p). UNBIASED: E[m]=1−p ⇒ E[δ̂]=δ ("approximate original embeddings" — the stated rationale). Result θ_pre+δ̂ per model, then combined by a downstream merge → DARE-linear (Task Arithmetic) or DARE-TIES (§R123). Drops p=0.9–0.99 of SFT deltas with negligible loss (they're redundant). Pure tensor math, seeded Bernoulli (rand/v2 PCG). matches nn.DARE(base, model, dropRate, seed). §V16 tier-1: dare_test.go — NoDrop identity (p=0 ⇒ model) + RescaleValues (base0/ones/p0.75 ⇒ every entry ∈{0,4}) + ExpectationAndDropFraction (n=20k: drop frac≈p + mean≈δ unbiased) + Deterministic (seed reproducible) + TIESComposition (DARE-TIES pipeline) + errors + ExampleDARE | Yu 2024 arXiv:2311.03099 + mergekit (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R125 | xPos — length-extrapolatable positional encoding (Sun et al. 2022 arXiv:2212.10554 §3.2 Eq6/9/Alg1, "A Length-Extrapolatable Transformer"; microsoft/torchscale xpos_relative_position.py; used by RetNet §R114; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Alg.1 + torchscale, NOT /deep-research). xPos = the UNCHANGED RoPE rotation (θ_i=base^(−2i/hd), base 10000) PLUS a per-pair magnitude scale ζ_i^(±n): query fq(q,n)=RoPE(q,n)·ζ^n (XPosDownscale false), key fk(k,m)=RoPE(k,m)·ζ^(−m) (true) → attention score picks up exponential ζ^(n−m) decay in relative distance (length extrapolation). ζ_i=(i/(hd/2)+γ)/(1+γ), i∈[0,hd/2), γ=0.4 (torchscale/RetNet default; paper says γ manually set). SAME ζ_i^n on BOTH elements of pair i. ξ=0 ⇒ pure RoPE. NUMERICAL VARIANTS OMITTED (torchscale-only, absent from paper): scale_base=512 exponent divisor + midpoint recentering min_pos=−(len+offset)//2 — paper uses raw position n (documented, like §R114's omitted GroupNorm). VJP: out=ζ_i^(±n)·rotate(q) → dq=ζ_i^(±n)·rotateᵀ(g) (scale g then inverse-rotate). matches OpRoPE + RoPEAttrs{XPos,XPosGamma,XPosDownscale} (extends the RoPE op — ref-only like RoPE, GPU falls back §I4) + backend.XPosScales + ops.XPos(q,base,downscale). §V16 tier-1: autograd/xpos_test.go — OffIsRoPE (XPos:false byte-identical) + Magnitude (out/rope==ζ_i^(±n) both pair elements) + ScoreDecay (hd=2: score==ζ^(n−m)·rope-score = THE property) + Gradcheck (§V2 finite-diff) + PosZeroIsRoPE + ExampleXPos | Sun 2022 arXiv:2212.10554 + torchscale (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R126 | Concatenate (numpy.concatenate) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper/algorithm (data-movement op, §V16 exempt from research-lite; definitional source = numpy docs, explicitly recorded per §V16's "kein Paper" clause). Join N arrays along an axis: all inputs share rank+dtype and match in every dim except the concat axis, whose extents SUM; output[axis]=Σ inputs[axis]; negative axis counts from end (numpy). Each input's elements copied with the axis coord shifted by that input's cumulative offset. Differentiable: the VJP slices g back into each input's segment along the axis (∂/∂inₖ = g restricted to [offsetₖ:offsetₖ+dimₖ]). Memory-bound → ref/cpu only, GPU falls back (§I4/ADR-0008). matches OpConcat + ConcatAttrs{Axis} + backend.ConcatPlan (shared fwd/VJP shape logic) + ops.Concat(axis, …tensors). §V16 tier-1 (definitional round-trip + §V2 gradcheck): concat_test.go — Forward (numpy [2,3]→axis0 [4,3]/axis1 [2,6] hand-verified) + NDNegAxisRoundTrip (rank-3, axis −1, 3 inputs → slice-back recovers each) + Errors + ExampleConcat; autograd/concat_test.go Gradcheck (finite-diff both inputs == VJP) | numpy.linalg concatenate docs (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R127 | Prompt Tuning — soft-prompt PEFT (Lester et al. 2021 arXiv:2104.08691 §2/§3.2, EMNLP, "The Power of Scale"; HF PEFT prompt_tuning; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §2 + HF PEFT, NOT /deep-research). The SIMPLEST PEFT: prepend a learned soft prompt P∈[k,d_model] (k continuous virtual-token embeddings) to the input token embeddings X∈[seq,d_model] → model input [P;X]∈[k+seq,d_model] (P FIRST, along the sequence axis). ONLY P trains (exactly k·d_model params); all pretrained weights + the token-embedding table FROZEN. Same input-agnostic P for all inputs at train+inference. INIT: paper compares random / sampled-vocab / class-label — class-label best (classification), sampled-vocab strong general default, random worst; HF default = RANDOM (used here — sampled-vocab needs an embedding table, not assumed by a standalone layer). k∈{1,5,20,100,150}, 20 a strong default. Prompt tuning = the INPUT-ONLY simplification of prefix tuning (Li & Liang 2021, which injects learned K/V at EVERY layer). Uses the §R126 concat (differentiable → the soft prompt trains through it). matches nn.PromptTuning{P}/NewPromptTuning(dtype,k,dModel,seed)/Forward(=Concat(P,embeds,axis0))/NumVirtualTokens/Params. §V16 tier-1/§V2: prompt_tuning_test.go — Forward (prepend: first k rows==P, rest==embeds, shape [k+seq,d]) + Gradcheck (dP through concat) + Trains (fit P to a target prompt, MSE→0) + Errors + ExamplePromptTuning | Lester 2021 arXiv:2104.08691 + HF PEFT (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R128 | Slice + Split (numpy x[start:end] / numpy.split) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper/algorithm (data-movement ops, §V16 exempt from research-lite; definitional source = numpy docs, per §V16 "kein Paper" clause). SLICE: extract the half-open [start,end) along an axis; output = input shape with that axis extent → end−start; each out element copies from x with the axis coord shifted +start; negative axis from end. Differentiable: VJP SCATTERS g back into a zero input-shaped tensor at the sliced range (0 elsewhere). SPLIT: cut x into consecutive sized pieces along an axis (numpy sized split — e.g. a fused QKV projection → Q,K,V); sizes must be positive and sum to the axis extent; built as N Slices → the DUAL of Concat (§R126). Because x feeds N slice ops, the autograd tape ACCUMULATES their scattered grads into the full input gradient (dx = concat of the pieces' grads) — so Split trains through. Memory-bound → ref/cpu, GPU falls back (§I4/ADR-0008). matches OpSlice + SliceAttrs{Axis,Start,End} + backend.SlicePlan (shared fwd/VJP) + ops.Slice(x,axis,start,end) + ops.Split(x,axis,…sizes). §V16 tier-1 (definitional + §V2 gradcheck): ops/slice_test.go — SliceForward (numpy rows/cols + neg axis) + SplitConcatInverse (concat(split)==x, round-trip, uneven sizes) + Errors + Example{Slice,Split}; autograd/slice_test.go — SliceGradcheck (scatter, zero grad outside range) + SplitGradientAccumulation (dx=[wa;wb] stacked = tape sums slice grads) | numpy slice/split docs (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R129 | Reshape + ExpandDims + Squeeze + Stack (numpy.reshape/expand_dims/squeeze/stack) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper/algorithm (metadata/data-movement ops, §V16 exempt from research-lite; definitional source = numpy docs, per §V16 "kein Paper" clause). RESHAPE: change shape keeping row-major order + element count (out[flat i]=x[flat i]); target Numel must match. Differentiable: VJP reshapes g back to input shape (dx[flat i]=g[flat i]) — a pure re-layout. EXPANDDIMS(x,axis): insert a size-1 axis (rank+1; axis∈[0,ndim], neg from end+1) = Reshape. SQUEEZE(x,axis): remove a size-1 axis (rank−1; errors if that axis≠size 1) = Reshape. STACK(axis,…t): join along a NEW axis (all inputs same shape → rank+1) = ExpandDims each + Concat (§R126); numpy.stack. All differentiable (compose OpReshape + OpConcat, both have VJPs). Memory-bound → ref/cpu, GPU falls back (§I4/ADR-0008). matches OpReshape + ReshapeAttrs{Shape} + ops.{Reshape,ExpandDims,Squeeze,Stack}. §V16 tier-1 (definitional + §V2 gradcheck): ops/reshape_test.go — ReshapeForward (flat order preserved, incompatible-count errors) + ExpandSqueezeRoundTrip (insert/remove size-1, values preserved, squeeze-non-1 errors) + Stack (numpy stack axis0/axis1, result[i]==input i) + Example{ExpandDims,Stack}; autograd/reshape_test.go ReshapeGradcheck (grad reshapes back to preserved flat position) | numpy reshape/stack docs (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R130 | Sqrt + Abs + Clip (numpy.sqrt/abs/clip) — DEFINITIONAL math ops, no paper (elementwise, §V16 exempt from research-lite; source = numpy docs + standard calculus for the VJPs). Extends the elementwise family (Exp/Log/Tanh/ReLU/GELU/Sigmoid/SiLU → +Sqrt/Abs/Clip). SQRT: y=√x, VJP dx=g/(2√x)=g/(2y) (undefined at x=0). ABS: y=\|x\|, VJP dx=g·sign(x) (0 at x=0, matching numpy/torch). CLIP(x,Lo,Hi): y=clamp(x,[Lo,Hi]) (Lo≤Hi), VJP dx=g where Lo≤x≤Hi else 0 (gradient passes through the unclipped region — the pattern used for gradient clipping / bounded activations). Sqrt/Abs use the shared unaryKernel/unaryVJP; Clip carries ClipAttrs{Lo,Hi}. Reference kernels (the existing elementwise ops are ref-level; GPU has its own where compute-bound). matches OpSqrt/OpAbs/OpClip + ClipAttrs + ops.{Sqrt,Abs,Clip}. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/mathops_test.go SqrtAbsClipForward (√[1,4,9]=[1,2,3], \|·\|, clamp[0,1], Lo>Hi errors) + ExampleClip; autograd/mathops_test.go Sqrt/Abs/ClipGradcheck (finite-diff == derivative) + ClipZeroGradWhenClamped (clamped ends → 0) | numpy sqrt/abs/clip docs + calculus (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R131 | Maximum + Minimum + Where (numpy.maximum/minimum/where) — DEFINITIONAL select ops, no paper (elementwise, §V16 exempt from research-lite; source = numpy docs + subgradient calculus). The binary/select group: MAXIMUM/MINIMUM(a,b) elementwise max/min (via shared binaryKernel = math.Max/Min); WHERE(cond,a,b) elementwise select out=cond?a:b (cond nonzero=true; all same shape, a/b same dtype). VJPs route the gradient to the SELECTED branch (shared "selectVJP" pattern): Maximum da=g where a≥b else 0, db=g where a<b (ties→a, a subgradient choice, measure-zero); Minimum da=g where a≤b; Where da=g where cond≠0, db=g where cond==0, dcond=NIL (non-differentiable mask). Heavily used: max(x,eps) numerical stability, where(mask,·,·) causal/padding masks. matches OpMaximum/OpMinimum/OpWhere + ops.{Maximum,Minimum,Where}. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/select_test.go MaximumMinimumWhereForward (max[1,5,3]/[4,2,3]=[4,5,3], min, where[1,0,1]=[a,b,a] select, shape-mismatch errors) + ExampleWhere; autograd/select_test.go Maximum/Minimum/WhereGradcheck (finite-diff, no ties) + MaximumRouting (dg to larger, explicit da/db) | numpy maximum/minimum/where docs + calculus (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R132 | Bottleneck Adapter — PEFT (Houlsby et al. 2019 arXiv:1902.00751 §2.1/§3, ICML, "Parameter-Efficient Transfer Learning for NLP"; AdapterHub/adapters; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §2.1/Fig.2 + AdapterHub, NOT /deep-research).
