The ORIGINAL PEFT (pre-LoRA). For a hidden activation h∈[n,d]: Adapter(h)=h + f(h·W_down+b_down)·W_up + b_up, W_down[d,r]/W_up[r,d], r≪d, INTERNAL residual, biases on BOTH projections. Nonlinearity f: paper §2.1 "a nonlinearity"; §3.6 GELU, AdapterHub "houlsby" config ReLU → CONFIGURABLE (default GELU, [conflict resolved by config]). NEAR-IDENTITY init: projections near-zero (paper trunc-normal std≈1e-2) → f(Down)·Up≈0, output≈h; here Up zeroed EXACTLY → exact identity at start (cf LoRA B=0). Only adapter params (both projections' W+b) train, all pretrained weights FROZEN; ≈2·d·r params/adapter. PLACEMENT: 2 serial adapters/transformer-layer (after MHA output-proj, after FFN), each before the existing residual+LayerNorm. vs LoRA (§R27): adapter = EXTRA SEQUENTIAL module w/ nonlinearity (adds latency, NOT mergeable); LoRA = parallel low-rank ΔW=BA, no nonlinearity, mergeable. matches nn.Adapter{Down,Up *Linear, Act} + NewAdapter(dtype,d,r,seed,…AdapterOption) + AdapterActivation(op) + Forward(=h+Up(f(Down(h)))) + Params([Down.W,Down.B,Up.W,Up.B]). §V16 tier-1/§V2: adapter_test.go — IdentityAtInit (Up=0 ⇒ Forward(h)==h exactly) + GradcheckE2E (through the module w/ randomized Up: x/Down.W/Up.W finite-diff) + ActivationOption (ReLU zeros neg pre-act where GELU doesn't) + Errors + ExampleAdapter | Houlsby 2019 arXiv:1902.00751 + AdapterHub (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R133 | Prefix Tuning — PEFT (Li & Liang 2021 arXiv:2101.00190 §4, ACL, "Prefix-Tuning: Optimizing Continuous Prompts for Generation"; HF PEFT PrefixEncoder; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §4 + HF PEFT, NOT /deep-research). Prepends p learned continuous vectors (the prefix) to the KEYS+VALUES of EVERY attention layer (past_key_values), NOT input embeddings (that's prompt tuning §R127): K,V∈[seq,d] → attention over [P_K;K],[P_V;V]∈[p+seq,d]; query UNCHANGED; prefix INPUT-INDEPENDENT (same for all inputs). REPARAMETERIZATION (§4.3, for training stability — direct optimization unstable): P=MLP(P'), P'∈[p,k] small learned embedding, MLP = Linear(k→h)→TANH→Linear(h→2·d) → split into P_K,P_V∈[p,d]; MLP droppable at inference (cache P_K/P_V). ONLY prefix params (P'+MLP) train, all pretrained FROZEN (≈0.1%). vs prompt tuning (input-embedding only, special case) / adapters §R132 (residual bottleneck). matches nn.PrefixTuning{Pemb[p,k], Trans1/Trans2 *Linear} + NewPrefixTuning(dtype,p,d,projDim,encHidden,seed) + KV(ctx)→(pk,pv[p,d] via MLP+tanh+Slice) + Apply(ctx,k,v)→prepend via Concat (§R126) → [p+seq,d] + PrefixLen + Params([Pemb,Trans1.W/B,Trans2.W/B]). Single-attention-layer form (num_layers=1); a full model uses one per layer. §V16 tier-1/§V2: prefix_tuning_test.go — Apply (first p rows==prefix P_K/P_V, remaining seq==original k,v unchanged, shape [p+seq,d]) + Gradcheck (Pemb/Trans1.W/Trans2.W finite-diff → prefix trains) + Errors + ExamplePrefixTuning | Li & Liang 2021 arXiv:2101.00190 + HF PEFT (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R134 | Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) — training objective (Gloeckle et al. 2024 arXiv:2404.19737 §2 Eq.1-2, Meta/ICML, "Better & Faster LLMs via Multi-token Prediction"; facebookresearch/multi-token-prediction; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous verbatim §2 Eq.1-2, NOT /deep-research). Predict the next n tokens/position: SHARED TRUNK f_s→z_t, n INDEPENDENT heads f_{h_i} in PARALLEL (not autoregressive at train), SHARED unembedding f_u; head i predicts token t+i. LOSS (Eq.2, UNWEIGHTED equal sum): L_n = −Σ_t Σ_{i=1}^n log P(x_{t+i}\|z_t) = Σ_{i=1}^n CrossEntropy(head_i logits[0:seq−i], targets[i:seq]) — head i's target = i steps ahead (targets LEFT-shifted by i), end positions w/o valid target-i DROPPED. NO per-head λ (the λ-weighting is a DIFFERENT follow-up paper, NOT Gloeckle). n=1 recovers standard next-token CE EXACTLY. n=4 main result (ablations 1,2,4,6,8). INFERENCE: head 1 only (vanilla) or extra heads for SELF-SPECULATIVE decoding (≈3×). Memory-efficient = sequential per-head fwd/bwd, mathematically the plain sum. matches nn.MultiTokenLoss(ctx, heads []*T, targets [seq]) — composes OpSlice (§T198 shift) + OpCrossEntropy + OpAdd, differentiable; heads supplied by the caller's architecture. §V16 tier-1/§V2: mtp_test.go — N1EqualsCrossEntropy (n=1 == next-token CE @1e-12) + SumMatchesManual (n=3 == independent manual Σ shifted CE @1e-12) + Gradcheck (into every head's logits) + Errors (seq≤n, no heads, shape) + ExampleMultiTokenLoss | Gloeckle 2024 arXiv:2404.19737 + facebookresearch/multi-token-prediction (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R135 | R-Drop — regularized dropout (Wu et al. 2021 arXiv:2106.14448 §2.1 Eq.2-4, NeurIPS, "R-Drop: Regularized Dropout for Neural Networks"; dropreg/R-Drop; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous byte-faithful vs paper §2.1 + official repo, NOT /deep-research). Run the SAME input through the net TWICE with independent dropout → P1=softmax(l1), P2=softmax(l2); add a SYMMETRIC-KL consistency regularizer. L_KL=½(KL(P1‖P2)+KL(P2‖P1)) = ½·Σ(p−q)(log p−log q) (identity). CRITICAL: gradient flows through BOTH P1,P2 (NO detach/stop-grad — UNLIKE distillation §R51 where the teacher is frozen). Full loss L=L_NLL+α·L_KL; L_NLL = paper SUM −logP1(y)−logP2(y) (Eq.3) / official repo 0.5·(CE1+CE2) avg (equivalent up to scaling). α: NMT≈5 (swept 1,3,5,7,10), LM≈1, classification smaller. NO extra params, NO architecture change (pure training-time reg on existing dropout). matches nn.SymmetricKL(ctx,l1,l2) — COMPOSED from OpSoftmax+OpLog+OpSub+OpMul+OpSum+OpAXPY (all differentiable → grad to both, no new op/VJP needed), mean-over-rows; + nn.RDropLoss(ctx,l1,l2,targets,α)=½(CE1+CE2)+α·SymmetricKL. §V16 tier-1/§V2: rdrop_test.go — ZeroWhenEqual (SymKL(l,l)=0) + ValueAndSymmetry (== independent Go recompute @1e-12 + SymKL(a,b)==SymKL(b,a) + positive) + GradcheckBothSides (finite-diff into BOTH l1,l2 = R-Drop's key property) + RDropLossComposition + Errors + ExampleSymmetricKL | Wu 2021 arXiv:2106.14448 + dropreg/R-Drop (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R136 | NumPy .npy format (numpy.lib.format v1.0) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (file format, §V16 definitional source = numpy .npy spec + reference numpy, explicitly recorded per §V16 "kein Paper"; §V15 round-trip+fuzz). Single-array interchange: 6-byte magic \x93NUMPY, 2-byte version (1.0), header-len (uint16 v1 / uint32 v2), ASCII Python-dict header {'descr','fortran_order','shape'} padded w/ spaces to a 64-byte multiple ending '\n', then RAW C-order LITTLE-ENDIAN element bytes. descr '<f8'→F64, '<f4'→F32 (also '='/'\|' native/NA accepted; '>' big-endian REJECTED). Supports F32/F64; fortran_order=True rejected. FUZZ-SAFE: bounded header-len (≤1MB) + numel (≤1<<30) before alloc, io.ReadFull errors on short read, no panic on hostile bytes. matches format/npy: Save/Load/SaveFile/LoadFile. §V15 tier-1: npy_test.go — RoundTrip (Save→Load bit-exact: scalar/1-D-trailing-comma/N-D, F32+F64) + LoadNumpyReference (reads real-numpy-written fixtures byte-faithful) + SaveReadableByNumpy (Go-written → real numpy reads EXACT, skip-if-no-.venv) + LoadErrors + Example; fuzz_test.go FuzzLoad (no panic, ≈4M execs) + FuzzRoundTrip (bit-exact, ≈4M execs) | numpy.lib.format spec + reference numpy 2.5.1 (REF = definitional, cross-verified both directions) | high |
| R137 | NumPy .npz archive (numpy.savez / numpy.load) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (file format, §V16 definitional source = numpy .npz convention + reference numpy; §V15 round-trip+fuzz). A ZIP container (stdlib archive/zip) holding ONE .npy member per named array as "`<name>`.npy" (§R136 codec). Save uses ZIP_STORED (uncompressed, matches numpy.savez), sorted names → deterministic; Load reads members (name = trim ".npy"), each decoded by the fuzz-safe npy.Load (reader handles STORED + DEFLATE transparently). FUZZ-SAFE: entry-count cap (≤1<<16) + per-array npy bounds; Load(r io.ReaderAt,size) — malformed zip errors, no panic. matches format/npz: Save/Load/SaveFile/LoadFile. §V15 tier-1: npz_test.go — RoundTrip (multi-array names/shapes/dtypes/values bit-exact) + LoadNumpyReference (reads real numpy.savez archive byte-faithful) + SaveReadableByNumpy (Go-written → real numpy.load EXACT, skip-if-no-.venv) + LoadErrors + ExampleSave; fuzz_test.go FuzzLoad (no panic) + FuzzRoundTrip (bit-exact) — both actual-fuzzed. Completes the numpy interchange pair (npy single-array + npz multi-array = whole model's tensors) | numpy .npz spec + reference numpy 2.5.1 (REF = definitional, cross-verified both directions) | high |
| R138 | BroadcastTo (numpy.broadcast_to) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (data-movement op, §V16 exempt from research-lite; source = numpy docs). The BROADCASTING PRIMITIVE (resolves §B18's deferred general broadcasting). Expand x to a larger target shape: RIGHT-ALIGN the shapes, each input dim must be 1 or equal to the target dim, input may have FEWER dims (treated as leading 1s). Forward: out[oc] = x[ic], ic[a] = 0 if inShape[a]==1 else oc[a+offset] (offset=len(target)−len(in)). Differentiable: VJP SUMS g over the broadcast axes back into the input shape (dx[ic] += g[oc]) — the gradient of replication is summation (so ∂(Σ broadcast(x))/∂x = replication count = ∏ broadcast dims). Memory-bound → ref/cpu, GPU falls back (§I4/ADR-0008). Compose with an elementwise op for broadcasting arithmetic (the follow-up: auto-broadcast Add/Mul/etc.). matches OpBroadcast + BroadcastAttrs{Shape} + backend.BroadcastPlan (shared fwd/VJP coord mapping) + ops.BroadcastTo(x,…dims). §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/broadcast_test.go Forward ([3]→[2,3] row, [2,1]→[2,3] col, scalar→vec, numpy right-align) + Errors (incompatible dim, rank-down) + ExampleBroadcastTo; autograd/broadcast_test.go Gradcheck (dx=Σ over broadcast axes, 2 cases) + GradIsReplicationCount (Σloss → dx=count) | numpy.broadcast_to docs (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R139 | Auto-broadcasting elementwise binary ops (numpy broadcasting for +−×÷/max/min) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (data-movement, §V16 exempt; numpy semantics). Completes §B18/§R138: the elementwise binary ops now BROADCAST when shapes differ (numpy rules via BroadcastShape) instead of erroring. SAME-SHAPE FAST PATH byte-identical (CPU SIMD §V3 tol0 unchanged) — broadcasting is a SEPARATE branch (materialize both operands to the common shape, then the existing kernel). VJPs REDUCE (sum) the output-shaped gradient back to each operand's shape (bcastReduce — no-op when same-shape → zero regression): Add da/db=reduce(g); Sub reduce(g)/reduce(−g); Mul reduce(g·b_bcast)/reduce(g·a_bcast); Div reduce(g/b)/reduce(−g·a/b²) via broadcast-coord loops; Max/Min (selectVJP) route+reduce by broadcast comparison. Shared backend.BroadcastShape (common shape) + BroadcastCoords (coord mapping) used by cpu binOp (broadcastContig materialize) + ref binaryKernel + all VJPs. Memory-bound broadcast path → ref/cpu. matches OpAdd/OpSub/OpMul/OpDiv (cpu SIMD + ref) + OpMaximum/OpMinimum (ref). §V16 tier-1/§V2: autograd/broadcast_binary_test.go — BroadcastForward ([2,3]+[3] row, [2,3]×[2,1] col) + Gradcheck (all 6 ops × bigger-left [2,3]/[3] & bigger-right [2,1]/[2,3], grad reduces correctly, tie-free); existing same-shape suites (cpu bit-identity, nn, autograd) UNCHANGED = zero regression | numpy broadcasting docs (REF = definitional, no research) | high |
| R140 | Var + Std reductions (numpy.var/std, ddof=0) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (data-movement/statistics, §V16 exempt; numpy semantics). Population variance over axes: Var = mean((x−mean)²) = Mean((x − Mean(x,axes,keepdims))², axes); Std = √Var. COMPOSED from Mean/Sub/Mul/Sqrt — VALIDATES the T209 broadcasting (x−mean_keepdims broadcasts) + T200 Sqrt; differentiable through the same dispatch path; axis+keepdims like the other reductions. ddof=0 (numpy default, population); ddof=1 (sample) = follow-up (Sum/(N−ddof) scaling). matches ops.Var(x,axes,keepdims) + ops.Std. §V16 tier-1: varstd_test.go — Full ([1,2,3,4]→Var 1.25, Std √1.25 hand) + Axis ([[1,2,3],[4,5,6]] var axis0=[2.25×3], axis1=[2/3×2], keepdims→[2,1]) + VsNumpy (== numpy.var/std ddof=0 @1e-10, skip-if-no-.venv) + ExampleVar | numpy.var/std docs (REF = definitional, cross-verified vs numpy) | high |
| R141 | Cumsum (numpy.cumsum) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (scan op, §V16 exempt; numpy semantics). Cumulative sum along an axis: out[..,i,..]=Σ_{j≤i} x[..,j,..] ascending (deterministic f64 accum §V10). Dual-purpose: LLM sampling (nucleus/top-p CDF) + prefix sums. DIFFERENTIABLE with a distinct VJP — since x[j] feeds every out[i≥j], the gradient is the REVERSE cumulative sum: dx[j]=Σ_{i≥j} g[i] (so ∂(Σ cumsum(x))/∂x[j] = L−j). Negative axis from end. Memory-bound → ref/cpu, GPU falls back (§I4/ADR-0008). Shared backend.CumsumPlan (axis normalize + reduced shape) + FillLineCoord (line enumeration) drive fwd kernel + VJP. matches OpCumsum + CumsumAttrs{Axis} + ops.Cumsum(x,axis). §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/cumsum_test.go Forward ([1,2,3,4]→[1,3,6,10], matrix axis0/axis1, neg axis) + Errors + VsNumpy (numpy.cumsum @exact, skip-if-no-.venv) + ExampleCumsum; autograd/cumsum_test.go GradIsReverseCount (Σloss→grad=[4,3,2,1]) + Gradcheck (reverse-cumsum VJP, axis 1) | numpy.cumsum docs (REF = definitional, cross-verified vs numpy) | high |
| R142 | Einsum (numpy.einsum) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (general tensor contraction, §V16 exempt; numpy semantics + reference numpy). Einstein-summation over N operands via a subscript spec "s1,s2,…->sO": each letter = an index; a letter repeated across operands (or within one, "ii") is contracted/aliased; any index NOT in the output is SUMMED. Implicit output (no "->") = indices appearing exactly once, alphabetical (numpy). SUBSUMES matmul "ij,jk->ik", batched matmul "bij,bjk->bik", transpose "ij->ji", trace "ii->", diagonal "ii->i", outer "i,j->ij", dot "i,i->", row-sum "ij->i", Frobenius "ij,ij->", elementwise "ij,ij->ij". ENGINE: mixed-radix iteration over every distinct-index assignment (output indices first, then summed), accumulating the product of operands' elements into out[output coords]; index sizes validated consistent across positions. Indices = single lowercase letters; ellipsis (...) NOT supported. FORWARD-only (non-differentiable; VJP = einsum-swap rule is a follow-up). Eager pure-tensor function (like linalg). matches ops.Einsum(spec, …operands). §V16 tier-1: einsum_test.go — Hand (matmul [[4,5],[10,11]], transpose, trace 5, outer, sum 21, rowsum, implicit output) + VsNumpy (matvec/contraction/transpose/batched-matmul == numpy.einsum @1e-10, skip-if-no-.venv) + Errors (operand-count, subscript-len≠ndim, output-index-absent, ellipsis, non-lowercase, non-square-trace) + ExampleEinsum (19,22,43,50) | numpy.einsum docs (REF = definitional, cross-verified vs numpy) | high |
| R143 | Differentiable Einsum — the einsum-swap-rule VJP (extends §R142; numpy.einsum backward). For C=einsum(s₁…sₙ→s_O), the gradient w.r.t. operand k is ITSELF an einsum: dAₖ = einsum(s₁…(skip k)…sₙ,s_O → sₖ; A…(skip k)…, g) — contract the OTHER operands + upstream g (taking the output subscript) down to sₖ. EXACT for pure contractions: every index of sₖ appears in another operand or the output, AND no subscript repeats a letter. UNSUPPORTED (errors on backward, forward still works): single-operand reductions (e.g. "ij->i", index summed appears only in one operand → needs broadcast) + diagonals/traces ("ii", repeated letter → needs scatter). Einsum is now a DISPATCHED OpEinsum (EinsumAttrs{Spec}) — tape-aware; engine moved to backend.EinsumContract + ParseEinsum (shared by kernel + VJP). Covers differentiable matmul/bmm/transpose/outer/dot/Frobenius/elementwise/contraction. §V16: DEFINITIONAL (swap rule = calculus of numpy.einsum) → no research; gradcheck + closed-form cross-check. §V16 tier-1/§V2: autograd/einsum_test.go — Gradcheck (7 patterns: matmul/bmm/transpose/outer/elementwise/dot/contraction, all operands) + MatmulGradClosedForm (dA=g·Bᵀ row-sums, dB=Aᵀ·g col-sums) + UnsupportedGradient (ij->i, ii->, ii->i error on backward, forward works) | numpy.einsum backward = swap rule (REF = definitional calculus, gradcheck-verified) | high |
| R144 | Differentiable Cholesky — reverse-mode VJP (Iain Murray 2016 "Differentiation of the Cholesky decomposition" arXiv:1602.07527 eq.6/9/10; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper eq + PyTorch cholesky_backward + numpy forward, NOT /deep-research). Complements the EAGER linalg.Cholesky (§R120) with a TAPE-differentiable version. Forward = lower L, A=L·Lᵀ (numpy.linalg.cholesky uplo=L, reads lower triangle). Backward maps output cotangent L̄→Ā via: Φ(M)=lower-triangle-with-halved-diagonal (Φ_ij=M_ij for i>j, ½M_ii for i=j, 0 for i<j; eq.6) → P=Φ(Lᵀ·L̄) (eq.10) → S=L⁻ᵀ·P·L⁻¹ (eq.9) → Ā=½(S+Sᵀ). The final ½(S+Sᵀ) is the PyTorch/JAX symmetrization, NOT Murray's raw S+Sᵀ−diag(S): under the autograd tape's FULL Frobenius inner product the raw form double-counts off-diagonals, whereas ½(S+Sᵀ) makes ⟨Ā,dA⟩=⟨L̄,dL⟩ hold for any symmetric perturbation dA (verified by gradcheck). L⁻¹ by forward-substitution. Enables differentiable GP marginal likelihood, whitening, SPD covariance reparam. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/cholesky_test.go (numpy goldens) + autograd/cholesky_test.go (symmetric-dA gradcheck + 1×1 closed form) | Murray 2016 arXiv:1602.07527 eq.6/9/10 + PyTorch cholesky_backward (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R145 | Differentiable log-determinant (SPD) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (numpy.linalg.slogdet + Jacobi's formula; §V16 exempt like the einsum-swap VJP §R143 / numpy ops). Forward logdet(A)=ln det(A)=2·Σ ln L_ii via the Cholesky factor (§R144/R120) — stable (no direct-product overflow), sign=+1 for SPD (numpy.linalg.slogdet). Backward = Jacobi's formula: ∂ logdet(A)/∂A = A⁻ᵀ = A⁻¹ for symmetric A, so Ā = ḡ·A⁻¹ with A⁻¹ = L⁻ᵀL⁻¹ from the same factor (L⁻¹ by forward-substitution). A⁻¹ is already symmetric ⇒ NO ½-symmetrization needed (contrast the Cholesky VJP §R144 where S is non-symmetric); ⟨Ā,dA⟩ = d logdet holds for symmetric dA under the tape's full Frobenius product. The workhorse term of a Gaussian/GP marginal log-likelihood (−½ logdet(K)−…) and normalizing-flow log-densities. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/logdet_test.go VsNumpy (3×3=3.5835…, 4×4=6.5981… vs numpy.linalg.slogdet @1e-12) + Diagonal (ln 30) + Errors; autograd/logdet_test.go Gradcheck (symmetric-dA, n=1..5) + GradIsInverse (Ā=A⁻¹, [0,0]=49.361…) | numpy.linalg.slogdet + Jacobi's formula (REF = definitional calculus, gradcheck-verified) | high |
| R146 | Differentiable SPD solve — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (numpy.linalg.solve + standard reverse-mode of a linear solve; §V16 exempt like §R145/§R143). Forward X=A⁻¹B via the Cholesky factor A=LLᵀ (§R144/R120): forward-subst L·Y=B then back-subst Lᵀ·X=Y (LAPACK dpotrs); B/X a vector [n] or matrix [n,k]. Completes the GP triad (Cholesky §R144 + logdet §R145 + solve): the GP marginal likelihood is −½ yᵀα − ½ logdet(K) − …, α=SolveSPD(K,y). Backward from dX=−A⁻¹·dA·X+A⁻¹·dB: B̄=A⁻ᵀḠ=A⁻¹Ḡ (another SPD solve, A symmetric), Ā_raw=−B̄Xᵀ → symmetrized Ā=−½(B̄Xᵀ+XB̄ᵀ) (½ = composition-correct for symmetric A under the tape's full Frobenius product, matching the Cholesky VJP §R144). B̄ reuses the forward op. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/solvespd_test.go VsNumpy (vector [1,2,3]→[28.583…,−7.667…,1.333…] + matrix [n,2] vs numpy.linalg.solve @1e-9) + Residual (A·X==B) + Errors; autograd/solvespd_test.go Gradcheck (B̄ per-entry + Ā symmetric-pair, n=1..4, k∈{1,3}) + GradClosedForm (diag A: B̄=1/A_ii, Ā_ii=−b_i/A_ii²) | numpy.linalg.solve + reverse-mode linear solve (REF = definitional calculus, gradcheck-verified) | high |
| R147 | Differentiable QR (reduced) — reverse-mode VJP (Seeger, Hetzel, Dai, Meissner & Lawrence 2017 "Auto-Differentiating Linear Algebra" arXiv:1710.08717 §QR; Townsend "Differentiating the QR decomposition"; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper + PyTorch linalg_qr_backward + TF _QrGrad, NOT /deep-research). Forward = reduced Householder QR of m×n (m≥n): A=Q·R, Q[m,n] orthonormal cols (QᵀQ=Iₙ), R[n,n] upper-tri (numpy.linalg.qr mode='reduced', LAPACK dgeqrf). Backward, given output cotangents Q̄,R̄: M=R·R̄ᵀ−Q̄ᵀ·Q (n×n); copyltu(X)=tril(X)+tril(X,−1)ᵀ (symmetric from the lower triangle, diagonal once); Ā=[Q̄+Q·copyltu(M)]·R⁻ᵀ. ONE formula covers m=n AND m>n: the full (unprojected) Q̄ already carries the tall-case orthogonal-complement term (I−QQᵀ)Q̄·R⁻ᵀ (PyTorch/TF confirm — they add the full Q̄ before the single R⁻ᵀ solve). QR unique only up to per-column sign (Q[:,j],R[j,:] co-negated) — our Householder (α=−sign(x₀)‖x‖) matches LAPACK on all but sign-ambiguous reflectors, so parity checks align by R[j,j] sign. MULTI-OUTPUT op ⇒ needed a new autograd path (§B-note): additive VJPMulti registry + Tape.backwardMulti (gathers every output cotangent, zero-filling unused outputs) — existing single-output VJPs untouched; unlocks future SVD/eigh backward. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/qr_test.go (VsNumpy sign-aligned + QᵀQ=I/R-upper/QR=A properties) + autograd/qr_test.go Gradcheck (square+tall, Q̄&R̄ active) + QOnly (zero-cotangent path) | Seeger et al. 2017 arXiv:1710.08717 + Townsend + PyTorch/TF linalg_qr_backward (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R148 | Differentiable symmetric eigendecomposition (eigh) — reverse-mode VJP (Ionescu, Vantzos & Sminchisescu 2015 "Matrix Backpropagation for Deep Networks with Structured Layers" arXiv:1509.07838 / ICCV'15; Giles 2008 "Collected Matrix Derivative Results" NA-08-01 §3.1; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper + PyTorch linalg_eigh_backward, NOT /deep-research). Forward = A=V·diag(w)·Vᵀ (real symmetric; w ASCENDING, V columns orthonormal, numpy.linalg.eigh) via the shared internal/linalg.SymEig (cyclic Jacobi, also used by PCA/GaLore), input symmetrized to (A+Aᵀ)/2. Backward, given cotangents w̄ (eigenvalues) + V̄ (eigenvectors): F_ij=1/(w_j−w_i) i≠j else 0 (Giles/PyTorch SIGN — verified: dV[:,k]=Σ_{j≠k}[V_jᵀ dA V_k/(w_k−w_j)]V_j ⇒ the (i,j) coeff is 1/(w_j−w_i); the 1/(w_i−w_j) form only coincides when VᵀV̄ is antisym off-diag, FALSE for arbitrary V̄); G=V·(diag(w̄)+F∘(Vᵀ·V̄))·Vᵀ; Ā=½(G+Gᵀ) (symmetric-input convention). Eigenvalue term V·diag(w̄)·Vᵀ exact + degeneracy-free; eigenvector term needs DISTINCT eigenvalues (F singular at ties — documented, no epsilon guard, like PyTorch). MULTI-output op (uses the §T217 VJPMulti path). §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/eigh_test.go (VsNumpy w exact + V per-column-sign-aligned + QᵀQ=I/recon properties) + autograd/eigh_test.go Gradcheck (w̄ & V̄ active, sign-aligned finite-diff, symmetric-pair, n=2..4) + EigenvaluesOnly (V̄=0 ⇒ Ā=V diag(w̄) Vᵀ=I for Σw) | Ionescu et al. 2015 arXiv:1509.07838 + Giles 2008 §3.1 + PyTorch linalg_eigh_backward (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R149 | Differentiable SVD (reduced/thin) — reverse-mode VJP (Townsend 2016 "Differentiating the Singular Value Decomposition" j-towns.github.io/papers/svd-derivative.pdf; Ionescu, Vantzos & Sminchisescu 2015 arXiv:1509.07838; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper + PyTorch svd_backward, NOT /deep-research). Forward = reduced A=U·diag(s)·Vᵀ (m≥n: U[m,n] orthonormal cols, s[n] desc≥0, V[n,n]) via one-sided Jacobi (Hestenes; numpy.linalg.svd full_matrices=False). Backward, given cotangents Ū,s̄,V̄: F_ij=1/(s_j²−s_i²) i≠j else 0; J=F∘(UᵀŪ−ŪᵀU), K=F∘(VᵀV̄−V̄ᵀV) (skew/antisymmetric → gauge-invariant to per-column sign); Ā=U·[diag(s̄)+J·diag(s)+diag(s)·K]·Vᵀ + (m>n) (I−UUᵀ)·Ū·diag(1/s)·Vᵀ (Ū's component ⊥ col(U); vanishes at m=n since V square ⇒ no (I−VVᵀ) twin). Singular-value term U·diag(s̄)·Vᵀ exact/degeneracy-free; U/V terms need DISTINCT NONZERO singular values (F blows up at ties, 1/s at zeros — documented, like PyTorch). Enables nuclear norm ‖A‖_*=Σs (grad U·Vᵀ), spectral norm, low-rank layers, differentiable PCA/pseudoinverse. MULTI-output op (§T217 VJPMulti path). §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/svd_test.go (VsNumpy s exact + U/V per-column-sign-aligned + UᵀU=VᵀV=I/recon) + autograd/svd_test.go Gradcheck (square+tall, Ū&s̄&V̄ active, U/V sign-aligned finite-diff) + NuclearNormGrad (Ā=U·Vᵀ closed form) | Townsend 2016 svd-derivative + Ionescu 2015 arXiv:1509.07838 + PyTorch svd_backward (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R150 | Spectral Normalization (Miyato, Kataoka, Koyama & Yoshida 2018 "Spectral Normalization for Generative Adversarial Networks" arXiv:1802.05957 ICLR'18; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Eq.8/9 + PyTorch parametrizations.spectral_norm, NOT /deep-research). A TRAINING-STABILITY method: normalize a weight by its largest singular value, W_SN=W/σ(W), bounding the layer's Lipschitz constant to 1 (GAN discriminators; also Lipschitz control in transformers/RL). σ(W) via ONE power iteration/forward on persistent singular-vector buffers u,v: v←Wᵀu/‖Wᵀu‖₂, u←Wv/‖Wv‖₂, σ≈uᵀWv (Alg.1/App.A; 15 warm-up iters at init, u≈N(0,1) persisted). u,v STOP-GRADIENT (paper §2.3) → backward differentiates only W/σ with u,v constant; ∂σ/∂W=u₁v₁ᵀ so ∂W_SN/∂W_ij=(1/σ)(E_ij−[u₁v₁ᵀ]_ij·W_SN) (Eq.9). KEY: building σ=uᵀWv + W_SN=W/σ as a DIFFERENTIABLE COMPOSITION makes autograd reproduce Eq.9 exactly — no hand-coded VJP. σ from power iteration → true σ_max=s[0] of SVD (§R149). matches nn.SpectralNorm{W,B,u,v} + NewSpectralNorm(dtype,in,out,seed,opts) + WithSpectralNormIters + SigmaEst; SpectralNormOption apicheck-allowlisted; u,v buffers NOT in Params(). §V16 tier-1: nn/spectral_norm_test.go — SigmaMatchesSVD (σ==ops.SVD(W).s[0] @1e-6, power-iter vs SVD cross-check) + UnitScale (σ_max(W/σ)==1, the Lipschitz-1 property) + Gradcheck (frozen u,v, autograd==finite-diff = Eq.9) + ExampleSpectralNorm | Miyato et al. 2018 arXiv:1802.05957 Eq.8/9 + PyTorch spectral_norm (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R151 | Weight Normalization (Salimans & Kingma 2016 "Weight Normalization: A Simple Reparameterization to Accelerate Training of Deep Neural Networks" arXiv:1602.07868 NeurIPS'16; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous vs paper Eq.2/3/4 + PyTorch parametrizations.weight_norm, NOT /deep-research). A TRAINING method: reparametrize each weight vector w=g·v/‖v‖ (Eq.2) — decouple magnitude g (per output unit) from direction v, conditioning the optimization. Gradient Eq.3: ∇_gL=(∇_wL·v)/‖v‖, ∇_vL=(g/‖v‖)∇_wL−(g·∇_gL/‖v‖²)v. PROJECTION property Eq.4: ∇_vL=(g/‖v‖)M_w∇_wL, M_w=I−ww'/‖w‖² ⇒ v·∇_vL=0 (direction grad ⟂ v). Init: paper = DATA-DEPENDENT (g←1/σ[t], b←−μ[t]/σ[t] from 1st minibatch); the simple g=‖v‖⇒w=v init is PyTorch's right_inverse default (documented divergence — we use g=‖v‖). REUSES the dispatched OpDoRAWeight kernel (m·V/‖V‖_col = exactly g·v/‖v‖ per column; DoRA §R70 IS per-column weight-norm on a frozen base) → autograd reproduces Eq.3 with NO new backward code; here BOTH v AND g train (full weight-normalized training, not PEFT). matches nn.WeightNorm{V,G,B} + NewWeightNorm(dtype,in,out,seed) + EffectiveWeight + Params()={V,G,B}. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/weight_norm_test.go — InitEqualsV (g=‖v‖⇒W=V) + ColumnNorms (‖W[:,j]‖==g[j], the defining property) + Gradcheck (V&g autograd==finite-diff = Eq.3) + GradOrthogonal (V[:,j]·∇_V[:,j]==0 = Eq.4) + ExampleWeightNorm | Salimans & Kingma 2016 arXiv:1602.07868 Eq.2/3/4 + PyTorch weight_norm (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R152 | Logit soft-capping (Gemma-2 technical report, Google DeepMind 2024 arXiv:2408.00118; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous vs HF modeling_gemma2.py + keras_hub GemmaBackbone + tanh calculus, NOT /deep-research). y=cap·tanh(x/cap) elementwise — a smooth differentiable bound to the open interval (−cap,cap): ≈x for \|x\|≪cap (identity, tanh u≈u), saturates gently to ±cap as x→±∞ (no hard clip kink). Gemma-2 applies it in the FORWARD pass (differentiable, a training feature not inference-only) at two points: attention scores QKᵀ/√d before softmax (cap=50) and final LM-head logits before the loss (cap=30). Backward dy/dx=1−tanh²(x/cap)=1−(y/cap)² (from output y, no recompute). matches OpSoftCap + SoftCapAttrs{Cap} + ops.SoftCap(x,cap); elementwise/memory-bound → ref+cpu, GPU falls back (§I4/ADR-0008), like tanh/gelu. §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/softcap_test.go — Formula (==cap·tanh(x/cap) @1e-12, cap=30) + Limits (≈x small, →±cap large, strictly inside for moderate) + Errors (cap≤0) + Gradcheck (VJP==finite-diff) + ExampleSoftCap (30·tanh: 1000→30, 2→1.997) | Gemma-2 report arXiv:2408.00118 + HF modeling_gemma2.py (attn 50 / final 30) (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R153 | Grokfast — accelerated grokking by amplifying slow gradients (Lee, Ahn, Kim & Kim 2024 "Grokfast: Accelerated Grokking by Amplifying Slow Gradients" arXiv:2405.20233; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs the official ironjr/grokfast gradfilter_ema reference impl, NOT /deep-research). Grokking (delayed generalization long after fitting train) is driven by a SLOW-varying (low-frequency) gradient component; Grokfast-EMA isolates it with a per-parameter gradient EMA and AMPLIFIES it → up to ≈50× faster generalization. μ_t=α·μ_{t−1}+(1−α)·g_t (low-pass EMA of the raw gradient); ĝ_t=g_t+λ·μ_t (passed to the base optimizer). A DROP-IN gradient transform BEFORE the base step (base SGD/Adam/… unchanged). μ INITIALIZED to the first gradient g_0 (not zero — matches reference: grads={n:p.grad.detach()} then updated). Defaults α=0.98, λ=2.0. λ=0 = exact base no-op. Grokfast-MA variant (windowed deque, default window=100, λ=5.0) = follow-up. matches nn.Grokfast{Base,Params,Lambda,Alpha} + NewGrokfast(base,params,opts) + WithGrokfastLambda/Alpha; float64 state (§V10); GrokfastOption apicheck-allowlisted. §V16 tier-1: nn/grokfast_test.go — EMARecurrence (ĝ==independent μ/ĝ recomputation @1e-12, μ seeded g_0) + InitFirstGrad (ĝ_0=g_0·(1+λ)) + LambdaZeroNoOp (ĝ=g) + ConvergesSGD (wraps SGD, convex quadratic loss→<1%) + ExampleGrokfast | Lee et al. 2024 arXiv:2405.20233 + ironjr/grokfast gradfilter_ema (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R154 | Locally Typical Sampling (Meister, Pimentel, Wiher & Cotterell 2023 "Locally Typical Sampling" arXiv:2202.00666 TACL; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous vs paper Def.3.5/Eq.7/12 + HF TypicalLogitsWarper, NOT /deep-research). A truncation sampler that keeps tokens whose INFORMATION CONTENT is near-average, filtering both the too-predictable and the too-surprising. Over the current distribution p: entropy H=−Σ p_i log p_i (nats); per-token typicality score_i=\|−log p_i − H\|=\|H+log p_i\| (abs surprisal-minus-entropy); sort ASCENDING (most typical first); greedily add tokens until cumulative prob ≥ τ (crossing token included, ≥1 always kept); renormalize. τ (mass) default 0.9, paper uses 0.9/0.95; τ≥1 (or 0) = no-op. Added as another filter in the Sampler.Dist pipeline (after min-p), same shape as top-p but sorting by typicality not prob. matches Sampler.Typical + WithTypical(τ). §V16 tier-1: nlp/typical_test.go — HandComputed (p=[.4,.3,.2,.1], H≈1.2799, order 1,2,0,3; τ=0.7 drops token3 → [.444,.333,.222,0], τ=0.45 keeps {1,2} → [0,.6,.4,0]) + NoOp (τ=1 unchanged) + PrefixProperty (kept = ascending-score prefix, renorm sum 1, ≥1 kept) + ExampleWithTypical | Meister et al. 2023 arXiv:2202.00666 + HF TypicalLogitsWarper (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R155 | Shampoo — preconditioned second-order optimizer (Gupta, Koren & Singer 2018 "Shampoo: Preconditioned Stochastic Tensor Optimization" arXiv:1802.09568 ICML'18; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Alg.1/Thm.7 + google-research/scalable_shampoo, NOT /deep-research). For a matrix param W[m,n] with gradient G: two FULL-matrix preconditioners L (m×m), R (n×n) init to ε·I, accumulated AdaGrad-style L←L+G·Gᵀ, R←R+Gᵀ·G (plain sum, Alg.1 has NO decay/momentum); update W←W−η·L^{−1/4}·G·R^{−1/4}. The −1/4 exponent = the order-2 case of −1/(2·order); an order-1 (vector) param reduces to AdaGrad (exponent −1/2, g/√Σg²). L^{−1/4}=V·diag(λ^{−1/4})·Vᵀ from the symmetric eigendecomposition (L sym-PSD; ε ridge keeps λ≥ε), reusing internal/linalg.SymEig — LEVERAGES the eigh infrastructure (§R148/§T218) as the matrix-root engine. Roots recomputed each step (Alg.1; amortize-every-k = distributed-variant follow-up). matches nn.Shampoo{Params,LR,Eps} + NewShampoo(params,lr,opts) + WithShampooEps; float64 state §V10; ShampooOption apicheck-allowlisted. §V16 tier-1: nn/shampoo_test.go — DiagonalStep (diagonal G ⇒ Ĝ_ii=g_i/√(ε+g_i²), full matrix step hand-checked vs Alg.1) + VectorAdaGrad (order-1 = g/(√Σg²+ε)) + Converges (convex quadratic→<1%) + ExampleShampoo; shampoo_internal_test.go InvMatrixRoot (diag(16,81)→diag(½,⅓) + (M^{−1/4})⁴·M=I) | Gupta et al. 2018 arXiv:1802.09568 Alg.1 + scalable_shampoo (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R156 | GPTQ — accurate post-training weight quantization (Frantar, Ashkboos, Hoefler & Alistarh 2022 "GPTQ: Accurate Post-Training Quantization for Generative Pre-trained Transformers" arXiv:2210.17323 ICLR'23; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs the official IST-DASLab/gptq gptq.py fasterquant, NOT /deep-research). CALIBRATION-AWARE PTQ (unlike data-free NF4/RTN): for a linear weight W[out,in] + calibration activations X[in,samples], minimize the layer output error ‖WX−ŴX‖²_F. H=2·X·Xᵀ (in×in reconstruction Hessian); damp=percdamp·mean(diag H) added to diagonal (default 0.01), dead columns (diag==0)→H_ii=1, W[:,i]=0; Hinv=cholesky(H⁻¹, UPPER) (upper-triangular). Greedy column loop i: q=quant(W[:,i]); d=Hinv[i,i]; err=(w−q)/d; W[:,i:]−=err⊗Hinv[i,i:] (outer product — the not-yet-quantized columns COMPENSATE the rounding error). LEVERAGES linalg.Inverse + linalg.Cholesky (§R120/§R144): upper factor of H⁻¹ = Lᵀ from the lower Cholesky. KEY property: GPTQ ≪ RTN output error; reduces EXACTLY to RTN when H diagonal (off-diag Hinv[i,i:]=0 ⇒ no propagation). matches nn.GPTQuantize(w,x,quant,opts) + WithGPTQDamp + nn.UniformQuantizer(levels,lo,hi) helper; GPTQOption apicheck-allowlisted. §V16 tier-1: nn/gptq_test.go — BeatsRTN (‖(W−Ŵ)X‖ strictly < RTN by clear margin on correlated inputs) + DiagonalEqualsRTN (X=I ⇒ GPTQ==RTN exact) + Errors + ExampleGPTQuantize (redistributes vs naive rounding). NOTE: core non-blocked GPTQ; lazy-batch blocking + act-order = perf follow-ups | Frantar et al. 2022 arXiv:2210.17323 + IST-DASLab/gptq fasterquant (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R157 | AWQ — Activation-aware Weight Quantization (Lin, Tang, Tang, Yang, Dang, Gan & Han 2023 "AWQ: Activation-aware Weight Quantization for LLM Compression and Acceleration" arXiv:2306.00978 MLSys'24; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs official mit-han-lab/llm-awq auto_scale.py _search_module_scale + quantizer.py, NOT /deep-research). Insight: salient weights are those multiplied by LARGE ACTIVATIONS (not large-magnitude weights). Protect them by per-input-channel scaling. s_X[j]=mean_samples\|X[j,:]\| (activation magnitude per input channel); scale_j=s_X[j]^α clamped ≥1e-4, normalized scales/√(max·min) (geo-mean≈1); Ŵ=Quant(W·diag(scale))·diag(1/scale) — quantize the scaled weight, fold 1/scale back, so W·X preserved and salient channels get less relative rounding error. α∈[0,1) by GRID SEARCH (ratio=i/n_grid, n_grid=20) minimizing output MSE ‖WX−ŴX‖²; α=0 ⇒ scale=1 ⇒ plain RTN. Per-output-row uniform symmetric quantizer, step recomputed from SCALED weights' max-abs (reuses nn.UniformQuantizer §T226). Data-aware PTQ complementing GPTQ (§R156, Hessian-based) + NF4 (§T75, data-free) → completes the RTN/GPTQ/AWQ trio. matches nn.AWQuantize(w,x,levels,opts) + WithAWQAlpha(pin α)/WithAWQGrid(n); AWQOption apicheck-allowlisted. §V16 tier-1: nn/awq_test.go — BeatsRTN (skewed per-channel activations ⇒ ‖(W−Ŵ)X‖ < per-row RTN) + AlphaZeroIsRTN (WithAWQAlpha(0)==per-row RTN exact) + Errors + ExampleAWQuantize (salient channel preserved) | Lin et al. 2023 arXiv:2306.00978 + mit-han-lab/llm-awq auto_scale.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R158 | DeepNorm / DeepNet (Wang, Ma, Dong, Huang, Zhang & Wei 2022 "DeepNet: Scaling Transformers to 1,000 Layers" arXiv:2203.00555; research-lite CONFIRMED 4-agent unanimous vs paper Table 2 + microsoft/torchscale encoder.py/decoder.py, NOT /deep-research). A deep-training-STABILITY method: replace the post-LN residual x'=LayerNorm(x+G(x)) with an UP-SCALED skip branch x'=LayerNorm(α·x+G(x)) (α>1), paired with a β-DOWN-SCALED weight init, to bound the model update ‖ΔF‖ INDEPENDENT of depth → stable training of 100s–1000s of layers. Constants (Table 2): encoder-only N layers α=(2N)^¼, β=(8N)^{−¼}; decoder-only M layers α=(2M)^¼, β=(8M)^{−¼}; encoder-decoder enc α_e=0.81·(N⁴M)^{1/16}, β_e=0.87·(N⁴M)^{−1/16}, dec α_d=(3M)^¼, β_d=(12M)^{−¼}. β = Xavier GAIN applied to the FFN (fc1,fc2), value-proj, output-proj weights; q/k use standard gain. α=β=1 recovers vanilla post-LN. matches nn.DeepNorm{LN,Alpha} + NewDeepNorm(dtype,d,alpha) + Forward(ctx,x,sublayerOut)=LayerNorm(α·x+G) via OpAXPY+LayerNorm (differentiable, composes with any sublayer); DeepNormEncoder/DeepNormDecoder/DeepNormEncoderDecoder constant helpers; DeepNormInit(w,fanIn,fanOut,β,seed)=Xavier·β. §V16 tier-1: nn/deepnorm_test.go — Constants (enc/dec/enc-dec vs Table-2 formulas) + Residual (Forward==LayerNorm(α·x+g)) + AlphaOnePostLN (α=1==LN(x+g)) + Init (DeepNormInit==β·XavierUniform same seed, range bound) + ExampleDeepNorm/ExampleDeepNormEncoder (α=3.76,β=0.19 @100 layers) | Wang et al. 2022 arXiv:2203.00555 Table 2 + microsoft/torchscale (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R159 | GKD generalized Jensen-Shannon divergence distillation (Agarwal, Vieillard, Zhou, Stanczyk, Ramos, Geist & Bachem 2024 "On-Policy Distillation of Language Models: Learning from Self-Generated Mistakes" arXiv:2306.13649 ICLR'24; research-lite CONFIRMED formula + endpoints vs paper §3 Eq.1/2 + HuggingFace TRL gkd_trainer, NOT /deep-research). A distillation divergence extending the forward-KL soft-target KD (§DistillLoss). P=softmax(teacher), Q=softmax(student), M=β·P+(1−β)·Q; JSD^β(P‖Q)=β·KL(P‖M)+(1−β)·KL(Q‖M), mean over rows. BOUNDED even for disjoint supports (unlike KL); β=0.5 = standard SYMMETRIC JSD (JSD^β(P‖Q)=JSD^{1−β}(Q‖P)); interpolates distillation direction: β→0 ≈ FORWARD KL(P‖Q)=KL(teacher‖student), β→1 ≈ REVERSE KL(Q‖P)=KL(student‖teacher) (research CORRECTED my initially-swapped endpoint labels). Degenerate endpoints taken literally: β=0=forward KL(teacher‖student), β=1=reverse KL(student‖teacher) (matches TRL). Differentiable w.r.t. student logits. COMPOSED from Softmax/Log/Sub/Mul/AXPY/Sum (like SymmetricKL §R135) → autograd-differentiable, no fused op. matches nn.GKDLoss(ctx,studentLogits,teacherLogits,β), β∈[0,1]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/gkd_test.go — Formula (β∈{0,0.3,0.5,1} == independent JSD/KL recompute @1e-9) + Symmetric (β=0.5 swap-invariant) + NonNegative + Gradcheck (∂/∂student == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleGKDLoss (identical logits→0) | Agarwal et al. 2024 arXiv:2306.13649 §3 + TRL gkd_trainer (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R160 | SOAP — Adam in Shampoo's eigenbasis (Vyas, Morwani, Zhao, Brandfonbrener, Shao & Kakade 2024 "SOAP: Improving and Stabilizing Shampoo using Adam" arXiv:2409.11321; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Alg.3 + official nikhilvyas/SOAP soap.py, NOT /deep-research). Combines Shampoo's 2nd-order rotation with Adam's per-coordinate robustness. For W[m,n], G: preconditioner EMAs L←β₂L+(1−β₂)GGᵀ (m×m), R←β₂R+(1−β₂)GᵀG (n×n); eigenvector bases Q_L,Q_R (eigenvectors of L,R) recomputed every `Freq` steps (default 10), reused between. Per step: rotate G'=Q_LᵀGQ_R; standard bias-corrected Adam on G' (M'←β₁M'+(1−β₁)G', V'←β₂V'+(1−β₂)G'², N'=M̂'/(√V̂'+ε)); rotate back N=Q_LN'Q_Rᵀ; W−=lr·N. Q_L=Q_R=I ⇒ reduces EXACTLY to Adam. On eigenbasis refresh: M' rotated into the new basis (project-back with old Q, project with new Q), V' kept in place (reference approximation). Non-matrix params → plain Adam. BUILDS on the eigh (§R148) + Shampoo (§R155) infrastructure (reuses internal/linalg.SymEig). float64 state §V10; defaults β₁=β₂=0.95, ε=1e-8, Freq=10. matches nn.SOAP{Params,LR,Beta1,Beta2,Eps,Freq} + NewSOAP(params,lr,opts) + WithSOAPBetas/Eps/Freq; SOAPOption apicheck-allowlisted. §V16 tier-1: nn/soap_test.go — ReducesToAdam1x1 (1×1 == independent Adam trajectory) + OneStepParity (matrix step == manual recompute: L/R EMA→eig→rotate→Adam→rotate-back) + Converges (quadratic across refreshes→<1%) + ExampleSOAP; soap_internal_test.go RotationInverse (rotateBack∘rotateForward=id, QᵀQ=I) | Vyas et al. 2024 arXiv:2409.11321 Alg.3 + nikhilvyas/SOAP soap.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R161 | Expert Choice routing for MoE (Zhou, Lei, Liu, Du, Huang, Zhao, Dai, Le & Laudon 2022 "Mixture-of-Experts with Expert Choice Routing" arXiv:2202.09368 NeurIPS'22; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §3 Eq.1-4 + Google Research blog, NOT /deep-research). INVERTS token-choice (§R61, each token picks top-k experts → can overload experts, needs aux balance loss): each EXPERT picks its top-k tokens. Affinity S=Softmax(X·Wg) ∈ Rⁿˣᵉ (rows softmax over experts, Eq.2). Routing: G,I=TopK(Sᵀ,k) — per-expert top-k over the TOKEN dim → I∈Rᵉˣᵏ selected token indices, G∈Rᵉˣᵏ gate weights (=affinities), P=Onehot(I) dispatch. Per-expert capacity k=n·c/e (c=capacity factor=avg experts/token, default 2≈GShard top-2; Eq.1). KEY: each expert processes EXACTLY k tokens → PERFECT load balance BY DESIGN, no auxiliary loss (unlike Switch/GShard); a token may be selected by 0/1/several experts (heterogeneous compute). Combine (Eq.3-4): X_out[l,d]=Σ_{i,j}P[i,j,l]·G[i,j]·X_e[i,j,d]. matches nn.ExpertChoiceRoute(scores,capacity)→(tokens,gates) + ExpertChoiceCapacity(n,e,c)=round(n·c/e) + ExpertAffinity(logits)=row-softmax + ExpertChoiceCombine(tokens,gates,expertOut,n,dim). §V16 tier-1: nn/expert_choice_test.go — LoadBalance (each expert exactly k tokens, total=e·k) + TopK (selected = top-k by affinity, gates=affinities, expert0→[0,2]) + Heterogeneous (token to several/zero experts) + Combine (Σ gate·expertOut) + ExampleExpertChoiceRoute | Zhou et al. 2022 arXiv:2202.09368 §3 Eq.1-4 + Google Research blog (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R162 | Soft MoE — fully-differentiable Mixture-of-Experts (Puigcerver, Riquelme, Mustafa & Houlsby 2023 "From Sparse to Soft Mixtures of Experts" arXiv:2308.00951 ICLR'24; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper §2.1 Alg.1 + fkodom/google-research reference, NOT /deep-research). Replaces the HARD top-k routing of token-choice (§R61)/Expert-Choice (§R161) with SOFT convex mixtures — no argmax, no token dropping, no load-balancing loss, smooth in X and Φ. n tokens X[n,d], e experts × p slots (e·p total), learned Φ[d,e·p]. Logits L=X·Φ [n,e·p]. DISPATCH D=softmax over TOKENS (per-column): each slot = convex combo of ALL tokens; slot inputs X̃=Dᵀ·X [e·p,d]. Each expert runs its p slots: Ỹ[i]=f_{⌊i/p⌋}(X̃[i]). COMBINE C=softmax over SLOTS (per-row): each token reads a convex combo of ALL slots; Y=C·Ỹ [n,d]. Both D,C from the SAME L. FULLY DIFFERENTIABLE → clean end-to-end gradcheck. matches nn.SoftMoE{Phi,Experts,SlotsPerExpert} + NewSoftMoE(dtype,dim,hidden,numExperts,slotsPerExpert,seed); Forward = MatMul→Transpose(OpTranspose)→Softmax(=Dᵀ)→MatMul→per-expert Slice/SwiGLU/Concat→Softmax(=C)→MatMul. NEEDED a tape-recorded transpose (§R163) — the view-based tensor.Transpose broke the dispatch-path gradient. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/soft_moe_test.go ForwardParity (independent L/D/X̃/C/Y recompute) + Gradcheck (x,Φ,expert weight all == finite-diff — proves full differentiability) + SoftmaxNorm/shape + ExampleSoftMoE | Puigcerver et al. 2023 arXiv:2308.00951 §2.1 + fkodom soft_moe.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R163 | Tape-recorded 2-D transpose (numpy.T) — DEFINITIONAL, no paper (§V16 exempt). OpTranspose: out[j,i]=in[i,j] for a rank-2 matrix — a DISPATCHED op (unlike the view-based tensor.Transpose §view.go) so it RECORDS on the autograd tape; its gradient is transpose(g) (transpose is self-adjoint). Motivated by Soft MoE (§R162): the dispatch softmax-over-tokens = Softmax(Lᵀ), and the view-transpose silently dropped the gradient back to L (transpose not a recorded node) → wrong x/Φ grads; the dispatched op fixes it and is a generally-useful primitive. ref-only kernel (elementwise-cheap; GPU/cpu fall back §I4). matches ops.Transpose(x). §V16 tier-1/§V2: ops/transpose_test.go Forward (out[j,i]=in[i,j], rank-1 error) + ExampleTranspose; autograd/transpose_test.go Gradcheck (∂(ΣW⊙xᵀ)/∂x[i,j]=W[j,i]) | numpy.T (REF = definitional) | med |
| R164 | Maximal Update Parametrization (μP / μTransfer) — Yang, Hu, Babuschkin, Sidor, Liu, Farhi, Ryder, Pachocki, Chen & Gao 2022 "Tensor Programs V: Tuning Large Neural Networks via Zero-Shot Hyperparameter Transfer" arXiv:2203.03466; research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs paper Table 3/8 + microsoft/mup + EleutherAI μTransfer guide, NOT /deep-research. Makes OPTIMAL hyperparameters (LR, init, multipliers) STABLE across model WIDTHS → tune a small proxy, transfer to a large target (μTransfer). Relative to a base width, width-mult m=width/BaseWidth. Per weight category (Table 8, microsoft/mup form, ADAM; SGD differs): INPUT/embedding & biases → Adam LR ×1, mult ×1, init var Θ(1); HIDDEN → Adam LR ×1/m, mult ×1, init var Θ(1/fan_in) [standard fan-in init]; OUTPUT/readout → Adam LR ×1/m, mult ×1/m (MuReadout output_mult/width_mult), init var Θ(1/fan_in); ATTENTION logits ×1/head_dim (NOT 1/√head_dim). vs SP where readout LR/mult do NOT scale with width → optimum drifts, no transfer. Coordinate-check: activation coords stay Θ(1)/width-independent under μP, blow up under SP. matches nn.MuP{BaseWidth} + NewMuP + WidthMult/HiddenLRScale/ReadoutLRScale/InputLRScale/ReadoutMult (all 1/m or 1) + AttentionScale(d)=1/d + nn.StdAttentionScale(d)=1/√d. §V16 tier-1: nn/mup_test.go ScalingConstants (Table-8 exact: 1/m hidden/readout LR+mult, input 1, attn 1/d≠1/√d) + SPContrast (readout LR/mult shrink with width) + ExampleMuP; nn/mup_coord_test.go CoordinateCheck (empirical 3-layer MLP + Adam: μP hidden-activation width-drift ≪ SP over 8× width — the μP property) | Yang et al. 2022 arXiv:2203.03466 Table 3/8 + microsoft/mup (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R165 | InfoNCE / contrastive loss (symmetric in-batch negatives) — van den Oord, Li & Vinyals 2018 (InfoNCE, arXiv:1807.03748) + Chen, Kornblith, Norouzi & Hinton 2020 (NT-Xent/SimCLR, arXiv:2002.05709) + Radford et al. 2021 (CLIP, arXiv:2103.00020); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs CLIP Fig.3 pseudocode + open_clip loss.py + google-research/simclr objective.py, NOT /deep-research. Trains matched embedding pairs (image↔text, or two augmented views) by pulling positives together + pushing in-batch negatives apart — foundational for embedding/dense-retrieval models (RAG). Given a,b ∈ Rⁿˣᵈ matched by index, optional per-row L2-normalize (→ cosine); logits S=(a·bᵀ)/τ ∈ Rⁿˣⁿ (τ=temperature; CLIP learns logit_scale=1/τ; smaller τ sharpens/up-weights hard negatives); diagonal S[i,i]=positive, off-diag=in-batch negatives. SYMMETRIC loss L=½(CrossEntropy(S,arange)+CrossEntropy(Sᵀ,arange)), standard softmax-CE with each row/col's true class=own index. Minimized when S[i,i] dominates its row/col. CLIP form (A≠B, no self-mask); SimCLR NT-Xent = single-encoder/two-aug/2N-samples with self-exclusion (k≠i) = variant follow-up. COMPOSED from OpTranspose(§R163)/MatMul/AXPY/Sqrt/Sum/Div/OpCrossEntropy → differentiable w.r.t. a,b. matches nn.InfoNCE(ctx,a,b,temperature,normalize) + l2NormalizeRows. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/infonce_test.go — Formula (±normalize, τ∈{0.1,1} == independent symmetric-CE recompute @1e-9) + Properties (n=1→0, aligned a=b < misaligned) + Gradcheck (a,b ±normalize == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleInfoNCE | van den Oord 2018 arXiv:1807.03748 + CLIP arXiv:2103.00020 Fig.3 + open_clip/simclr (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R166 | Scaled-cosine Query-Key Normalization (QKNorm) — Henry, Dachapally, Pawar & Chen 2020 ("Query-Key Normalization for Transformers", EMNLP Findings, arXiv:2010.04245); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary PDF (aclanthology.org/2020.findings-emnlp.379 p.4248) + emergentmind, NOT /deep-research. Bounds attention logits by L2-normalizing Q,K along head_dim per head so each Q̂·K̂ entry is a cosine similarity ∈ [−1,1], then REPLACING the fixed 1/√d_k with a single LEARNED scalar g: attn=softmax(g·Q̂K̂ᵀ)·V (Eq.4→5) ⇒ pre-softmax logits bounded in [−g,+g]. Decouples logit magnitude from d_k, stabilizes low-resource/long-context training. Init g₀=log₂(L²−L) (Eq.3) where L = 97.5th-PERCENTILE training seq length (data-derived, NOT raw max seq_len). Complements LayerNorm/RMSNorm (does NOT replace it, unlike ScaleNorm). L2-normalize per head = q̂ᵢ=qᵢ/‖qᵢ‖ (Eq.2). COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpMul/OpSum(keepdims)/OpAdd(eps-floor)/OpSqrt/OpDiv (L2-norm last axis) + OpTranspose(§R163)/OpMatMul/OpSoftmax → grad w.r.t. q,k,v AND learned g. matches nn.QKNorm{G,Eps}.Attention(ctx,q,k,v,causal) + NewQKNorm(dtype,initG) + QKNormInitG(percentileSeqLen)=log₂(L²−L). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/qknorm_test.go — Parity (±causal == independent scaled-cosine reference @1e-9) + WeightsProperties (v=I ⇒ rows nonneg + sum-to-1 softmax + causal upper-triangle==0) + InitG (log₂(12)) + Gradcheck (q,k,v,G ±causal == finite-diff) + Errors (rank≠2, head_dim mismatch) + ExampleQKNorm | Henry 2020 arXiv:2010.04245 Eq.2/3/4/5 (PAPER = tier2 §V16) | high |
| R167 | Differential Transformer attention (DiffAttn) — Ye, Dong, Zhang, Zhu, Yang, Wang, Ma & Wei 2024 (Microsoft/Tsinghua, arXiv:2410.05258); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary arxiv.org/html/2410.05258 §2.1 Eq.1-4 + microsoft/unilm Diff-Transformer/multihead_diffattn.py, NOT /deep-research. Cancels common-mode "attention noise" by subtracting TWO 1/√d-scaled softmax maps (query/key split into two groups) applied to a SHARED V (Eq.1): DiffAttn(X)=(softmax(Q₁K₁ᵀ/√d)−λ·softmax(Q₂K₂ᵀ/√d))·V — like a differential amplifier, sharpening mass on relevant context + reducing hallucination/activation-outliers. λ REPARAMETERIZED (Eq.2) from four learnable d-vectors: λ=exp(λ_q1·λ_k1)−exp(λ_q2·λ_k2)+λ_init (· = dot product). Depth-dependent offset λ_init=0.8−0.6·exp(−0.3·(l−1)), 1-based layer l (Eq.3; unilm code depth=l−1). Post: head=(1−λ_init)·GroupNorm(head) — per-head RMSNorm ('subln', no cross-head mix, fixed (1−λ_init) scale) then Concat·Wᴼ (Eq.3-4). REF inits λ-vectors to ZEROS ⇒ λ starts exactly at λ_init (faithful, not simplification). COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpTranspose(§R163)/OpMatMul(scores + λ dot via [1,d]·[d,1])/OpMul(1/√d + λ scale)/OpExp/OpSub/OpAdd(causal mask)/OpSoftmax → grad w.r.t. q1,k1,q2,k2,v AND all four λ-vectors. matches nn.DiffAttention{LambdaQ1/K1/Q2/K2 [1,d], LambdaInit}.Attention(ctx,q1,k1,q2,k2,v,causal) + NewDiffAttention(dtype,d,layer) + DiffLambdaInit(layer) + SublayerScale()=1−λ_init. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/diffattn_test.go — Parity (±causal, nonzero λ-vectors exercising exp-dot == independent reference @1e-9) + CommonModeCancellation (identical q/k groups + zero λ-vecs ⇒ DiffAttn=(1−λ_init)·ordinary-attention, the defining property) + LambdaInit (Eq.3: l=1→0.2, deep→0.8; SublayerScale=1−λ_init) + Gradcheck (q1,k1,q2,k2,v + 4 λ-vectors == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleDiffAttention | Ye 2024 arXiv:2410.05258 §2.1 Eq.1/2/3 + microsoft/unilm multihead_diffattn.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R168 | Mixture-of-Depths (MoD) — Raposo, Ritter, Richards, Lillicrap, Humphreys & Santoro 2024 (Google DeepMind, arXiv:2404.02258); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary arxiv.org/html/2404.02258 §3.4 Eq.1 + §3.5, NOT /deep-research. Dynamic per-token compute: each block gets a FIXED budget — a scalar router r_i=w_θᵀ·x_i (learned linear→scalar) selects the top-k tokens by weight (EXPERT-CHOICE routing, k=round(capacity·S) fixed per-sequence, capacity≈12.5%, NOT a per-token threshold; threshold β=1−C/S is a derived percentile P_β) and ONLY those k tokens are processed by the block. Eq.1: selected x_i^(l+1)=r_i·f(X̃)+x_i (block output × RAW router logit r_i — no sigmoid on the residual path; sigmoid appears ONLY in the §3.5 BCE aux-predictor loss), skipped x_i^(l+1)=x_i (pure residual passthrough). r_i multiplies the block output ⇒ router on the gradient path ⇒ learnable. Routes same FLOPs to fewer tokens (faster) or deepens at fixed budget. Autoregressive sampling (top-k non-causal): §3.5 aux MLP predictor (stop-grad input, ≈99% acc) OR BCE aux-loss on router logits (out of core-forward scope, noted). DIFFERENTIABLE gather/scatter via one-hot selection matrix S[k,seq]: gathered=S·x, weights=S·(x·w_router), Combine y=x+Sᵀ·(weights⊙processed) — grad w.r.t. x AND router (selection treated constant = straight-through, correct: VJP of argsort is 0 a.e.). matches nn.MixtureOfDepths{Router [d,1], Capacity}.Route(ctx,x)→(gathered,weights,sel) + Combine(ctx,x,processed,weights,sel) + NewMixtureOfDepths(dtype,d,capacity) + NumProcessed(seq)=round(cap·seq)∈[1,seq]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/mod_test.go — RouteSelection (top-k by raw logit == independent argsort, gathered==x rows, weight==RAW logit no-sigmoid) + CombineResidual (selected=x+r·processed, skipped=x exactly) + NumProcessed (256/2048 @12.5% etc) + Gradcheck (x + router == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleMixtureOfDepths | Raposo 2024 arXiv:2404.02258 §3.4 Eq.1 + §3.5 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R169 | Gated Linear Attention (GLA) — Yang, Wang, Shen, Panda & Kim 2023/2024 ("Gated Linear Attention Transformers with Hardware-Efficient Training", arXiv:2312.06635); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1/§4.1 Eq.3-4 + authors' fla-org/flash-linear-attention gla.py, NOT /deep-research. Linear-time (O(L·d²)) sequence modeling with a matrix-valued state and a DATA-DEPENDENT per-key-dimension forget gate — distinct from RetNet's data-INDEPENDENT scalar decay γ (§R114) and from softmax O(L²). Recurrence (Eq.3): S_t=Diag(α_t)·S_{t-1}+k_tᵀv_t (S∈R^{d_k×d_v}; α_t∈(0,1]^{d_k} scales each ROW of the state per key-dim; k_tᵀv_t rank-1 outer product); output o_t=q_t·S_t ∈R^{1×d_v}. Gate α_t=σ(x_t·W_α¹·W_α²+b)^{1/τ} (low-rank d×16·16×d_k, τ=16) — CALLER-computed (projection-agnostic core, like QKNorm/DiffAttn). α_t=1 ⇒ reduces to ungated linear attention o_t=q_t·Σ_{s≤t}k_sᵀv_s (Eq.1). Chunk-parallel form uses log-space cumulative gate (numerically stable) — we implement the numerically-stable SEQUENTIAL recurrence directly (data-dependent gate ≠ fixed-γ OpRetention kernel). COMPOSED (differentiable) per step from OpSlice(row)/OpTranspose(outer + gate col)/OpMatMul(kᵀv outer + q·S)/OpMul(Diag(α) broadcast [d_k,d_v]*[d_k,1])/OpAdd + OpConcat(stack o_t) → grad w.r.t. q,k,v AND gate. matches nn.GatedLinearAttention(ctx,q,k,v,gate). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/gla_test.go — Parity (== independent recurrence @1e-9, data-dependent gate) + ReducesToLinearAttention (gate=1 == independent ATTENTION-form oracle o_t=Σ_{s≤t}(q_t·k_s)v_s) + Causality (future key cannot change earlier output) + Gradcheck (q,k,v,gate == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleGatedLinearAttention | Yang 2023/24 arXiv:2312.06635 §3.1/§4.1 Eq.3-4 + fla-org/flash-linear-attention (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R170 | DeltaNet — delta-rule linear attention (Yang, Wang, Zhang, Shen & Kim 2024, "Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length", arXiv:2406.06484; delta rule from Schlag et al. 2021); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §2.2/§3.1 Eq.3/§3.3 + fla-org/flash-linear-attention delta_rule/naive.py + author blog (sustcsonglin.github.io/blog/2024/deltanet), NOT /deep-research. Where linear attention/GLA ACCUMULATE k→v outer products, DeltaNet UPDATES memory by the PREDICTION ERROR (Widrow-Hoff delta rule) so a repeated key OVERWRITES its value instead of piling up — better memory management, distinct from GLA's diagonal gating (§R169) + RetNet's fixed γ (§R114). Recurrence (Eq.3): S_t=S_{t-1}(I−β_t k_t k_tᵀ)+β_t v_t k_tᵀ = S_{t-1}+β_t(v_t−S_{t-1}k_t)k_tᵀ (memory S∈R^{d_v×d_k}; v_t−S_{t-1}k_t = value prediction error; β_t∈(0,1) scalar writing-strength = σ(W_β x_t), caller-computed). Output o_t=S_t q_t — the QUERY (not the key) reads the UPDATED memory (§2.2). BOTH q,k L2-normalized post-SiLU (§3.3) — module normalizes q,k over d_k (SiLU feature-map = caller's projection). DEFINING PROPERTY: β_t=1 + ‖k̂‖=1 ⇒ S_t k̂_t=v_t (exact overwrite, since (I−k̂k̂ᵀ)k̂=0). COMPOSED (differentiable) per step from qkL2NormalizeLastAxis(q,k) + OpSlice/OpTranspose/OpMatMul(S·k pred + err·kᵀ outer + S·q read)/OpSub(v−Sk)/OpMul(β)/OpAdd + OpConcat → grad w.r.t. q,k,v,β. matches nn.DeltaNet(ctx,q,k,v,beta). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/deltanet_test.go — Parity (== independent delta recurrence @1e-9) + ExactOverwrite (β=1 same-key retrieval==v; second β=1 write ⇒ v_b OVERWRITE not v_a+v_b — the distinctive delta property vs linear-attn accumulate) + ZeroBetaNoUpdate (β=0 empty-state ⇒ 0 out) + Causality + Gradcheck (q,k,v,β) + Errors + ExampleDeltaNet | Yang 2024 arXiv:2406.06484 §2.2/§3.1 Eq.3/§3.3 + fla-org delta_rule/naive.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R171 | Gated DeltaNet — gated delta rule (Yang, Kang, Hofmann, Zhang, van den Berg & Kim 2024, "Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule", arXiv:2412.06464, ICLR'25; NVIDIA/MIT); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.3 Eq.10 + footnote 4 + fla-org/flash-linear-attention gated_deltanet.py/fused_recurrent.py, NOT /deep-research. CURRENT SOTA linear-attention (basis of Qwen3-Next / Kimi-Linear): UNIFIES a scalar data-dependent DECAY gate α_t (Mamba2/SSD-style forgetting — rapid memory clearing) WITH DeltaNet's delta-rule error-correction (§R170 — targeted overwrite), beating either alone. Recurrence (Eq.10): S_t=α_t·S_{t-1}(I−β_t k_t k_tᵀ)+β_t v_t k_tᵀ = α_t·S_{t-1}+β_t·(v_t−α_t·S_{t-1}k_t)·k_tᵀ — the scalar α_t∈(0,1) decays the WHOLE previous-state/delta term but NOT the β_t v_t k_tᵀ write (matches fla: b_h*=exp(b_g) BEFORE the delta update). Output o_t=S_t q_t. TWO EXACT REDUCTIONS: α_t=1 ⇒ plain DeltaNet; β_t=0 ⇒ scalar-decay memory S_t=α_t S_{t-1}. α_t=exp(−exp(A_log)·softplus(·)) (Mamba2 param), β_t=σ(·) — both caller-computed scalars; q,k L2-normalized (§3.4). COMPOSED (differentiable) per step from qkL2NormalizeLastAxis(q,k) + OpSlice/OpMul(α decay + β)/OpTranspose/OpMatMul(pred+outer+read)/OpSub/OpAdd + OpConcat → grad w.r.t. q,k,v,α,β. matches nn.GatedDeltaNet(ctx,q,k,v,alpha,beta). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/gated_deltanet_test.go — Parity (== independent gated-delta recurrence @1e-9) + AlphaOneEqualsDeltaNet (α=1 == independent nn.DeltaNet oracle @1e-12, the reduction) + GeometricDecay (write β=1 then β=0/α=0.5 ⇒ o_t=0.5ᵗ·o_0, the forgetting DeltaNet lacks) + Causality + Gradcheck (q,k,v,α,β) + Errors + ExampleGatedDeltaNet | Yang 2024 arXiv:2412.06464 §3.3 Eq.10 + fla-org gated_deltanet.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R172 | SmoothQuant activation-weight smoothing (Xiao, Lin, Seznec, Wu, Demouth & Han 2022/2023, "SmoothQuant: Accurate and Efficient Post-Training Quantization for LLMs", arXiv:2211.10438, ICML'23; MIT/NVIDIA); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §4 Eq.3-4 + official mit-han-lab/smoothquant smooth.py, NOT /deep-research. LLM activations have systematic per-input-channel OUTLIER dims that are far harder to quantize than weights; SmoothQuant MIGRATES that difficulty into the weights via a per-input-channel smoothing factor s∈R^{C_in}, a MATHEMATICALLY-EQUIVALENT rescaling of a linear layer Y=X·W (Eq.3): X̂=X·diag(s)⁻¹ (activation DIVIDED per channel), Ŵ=diag(s)·W (weight input-channel ROW MULTIPLIED) ⇒ X̂·Ŵ=X·W EXACTLY, so X̂ (per-tensor) + Ŵ (per-channel) both quantize cleanly to INT8 (W8A8). Smoothing factor (Eq.4): s_j=max(\|X_j\|)^α/max(\|W_j\|)^(1−α) — α on activation-max NUMERATOR, (1−α) on weight-max DENOMINATOR; max\|X_j\| over calibration tokens, max\|W_j\| over the j-th input-channel row. Default α=0.5 ("well-balanced sweet spot"; GLM-130B uses 0.75 — my research question's 0.6 was REFUTED by all 3 agents). Applied offline, scale fused into preceding LayerNorm at deploy. Pure-f64 POST-TRAINING transform (not differentiable — like AWQuantize §R143/GPTQ). matches nn.SmoothQuant(x,w,alpha)→(x̂,ŵ,scale) + SmoothQuantScale(actAbsMax,weightAbsMax,alpha) (degenerate zero-max channel → s=1 no-op, no div0). Distinct from AWQ (weight-only, act^α scale folded BACK) — SmoothQuant EXPOSES x̂ for activation quant + balances vs weight-max. §V16 tier-1: nn/smoothquant_test.go — Equivalence (X̂Ŵ==XW @1e-9 for α∈{0,0.5,1} with injected ×40 outlier channel — THE property) + ScaleFormula (Eq.4 s=[√4/√2,√1/√8]; zero-channel→1) + MigratesOutliers (×100 outlier ⇒ s_0=10, global \|X̂\| reduced 100→10, \|Ŵ\| increased) + Errors + ExampleSmoothQuant | Xiao 2022 arXiv:2211.10438 §4 Eq.3-4 + mit-han-lab/smoothquant smooth.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R173 | Matryoshka Representation Learning (MRL) — Kusupati, Bhatt, Rege, Wallingford, Sinha, Ramanujan, Howard-Snyder, Chen, Kakade, Jain & Farhadi 2022 (NeurIPS, arXiv:2205.13147); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.1/§3 + official RAIVNLab/MRL MRL.py + HF blog, NOT /deep-research. Trains ONE embedding so that each nested PREFIX z_{1:m} is independently usable — one model serves many embedding sizes (adaptive retrieval/deployment; OpenAI text-embedding-3, Nomic, Snowflake truncate MRL embeddings). Loss (Eq.1): L=Σ_{m∈M} c_m·CE(W^(m)·z_{1:m}, y) — SUM of softmax cross-entropy over nested prefixes of the SAME embedding; nesting set M={8,16,...,d}. Uniform importance c_m=1 (paper §3 "we set all c_m=1"; RAIVNLab default rel_importance=ones). MRL-E (efficient, implemented): SINGLE shared head W∈R^{d×C} tied across granularities, m uses first m rows W_{1:m} (plain MRL = separate W^(m) per m). COARSE-TO-FINE property: earliest coords appear in the loss at EVERY granularity (last only at largest m) ⇒ more gradient ⇒ front-loaded nested reps. COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpSlice(z[:,:m] axis1 + W[:m,:] axis0)/OpMatMul/OpCrossEntropy(mean-over-batch)/OpAdd → grad w.r.t. z AND w. matches nn.MatryoshkaLoss(ctx,z,w,targets,nestingDims). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/matryoshka_test.go — Parity (== independent Σ nested-prefix CE @1e-9) + SingleGranularityIsCE ({d} == plain CrossEntropy @1e-12) + GradientNesting (adding granularity m changes grad ONLY on coords <m, coords ≥m EXACTLY unchanged — the nesting structure, exact) + Gradcheck (z,w == finite-diff) + Errors (empty/non-increasing/out-of-range/dim-mismatch) + ExampleMatryoshkaLoss | Kusupati 2022 arXiv:2205.13147 Eq.1/§3 + RAIVNLab/MRL MRL.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R174 | DoLa — Decoding by Contrasting Layers (Chuang, Xie, Luo, Kim, Glass & He 2023, ICLR'24, arXiv:2309.03883; MIT/Microsoft); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §2.2-2.3 Eq.2-5 + official voidism/DoLa dola.py, NOT /deep-research. Inference-time FACTUALITY decoding: factual knowledge resolves in LATER transformer layers, so DoLa amplifies it by contrasting the final ("mature") layer's next-token distribution against an earlier ("premature") layer's (early-exit through the SAME output head). Distinct from model-level contrastive decoding (nlp.ContrastiveLogits §R82, needs a 2nd amateur MODEL) — DoLa contrasts LAYERS of ONE model. (1) Premature-layer selection: M=argmax_{j∈J} JSD(q_N‖q_j) — the MOST divergent candidate layer (Eq.2). (2) JSD=½KL(p‖m)+½KL(q‖m), m=(p+q)/2 (symmetric, ∈[0,ln2]) (Eq.3). (3) Contrastive score F(x)=log q_N(x)−log q_M(x)=logsoftmax_N−logsoftmax_M — PURE log-ratio, coeff 1 each, NO β (Eq.5); matches ref diff_logits=final−base. (4) Adaptive Plausibility Constraint: V_head={x: q_N(x)≥α·max_w q_N(w)} on the MATURE dist, else −∞; α=0.1 (Eq.4; log-space thresh=log(α)+max logsoftmax_N, exactly ref probs_max+log(relative_top)). Pure-function decoding utility (like ContrastiveLogits); caller provides mature + candidate premature-layer logits. matches nlp.DoLaLogits(mature,premature,alpha)→(scores,selectedLayer) + JensenShannon(p,q). §V16 tier-1: nlp/dola_test.go — JensenShannon (JSD([1,0],[0,1])=ln2, symmetric, JSD(p,p)=0) + SelectsMostDivergentLayer (argmax-JSD; single→0) + ContrastiveFormula (== logsoftmax_N−logsoftmax_M @1e-12) + AdaptivePlausibility (p_N<α·max → −∞) + UniformPrematurePreservesArgmax + Panics + ExampleDoLaLogits | Chuang 2023 arXiv:2309.03883 §2.2-2.3 Eq.2-5 + voidism/DoLa dola.py (PAPER+REF-IMPL = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R175 | Gumbel-Softmax / Concrete relaxation (Jang, Gu & Poole 2016 "Categorical Reparameterization with Gumbel-Softmax", arXiv:1611.01144, ICLR'17; concurrent Maddison, Mnih & Teh 2016 "The Concrete Distribution", arXiv:1611.00712); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Jang §2 Eq.1-2/§2.2 + Maddison §3 + PyTorch gumbel_softmax docs, NOT /deep-research. The reparameterization trick for DISCRETE variables — enables gradient-based training through a categorical choice (differentiable MoE routing, discrete latents/VAEs, hard attention). (1) Gumbel-Max trick: an exact categorical draw is onehot(argmax_i(logit_i+g_i)) with i.i.d. Gumbel noise g_i=−log(−log(u_i)), u_i≈Uniform(0,1) (Eq.1). (2) Gumbel-Softmax relaxation (Eq.2): y_i=softmax((logit_i+g_i)/τ) — temperature-τ softmax over (logit+gumbel); noise externally supplied ⇒ y is a smooth differentiable fn of logits. (3) τ→0 ⇒ one-hot (recovers Gumbel-Max); τ→∞ ⇒ uniform. (4) Straight-Through (ST-GS §2.2): hard one-hot forward, soft-gradient backward (∇z≈∇y), impl y=y_hard−stopgrad(y_soft)+y_soft. GoAI has NO stopgrad op → ST realized via detach-BY-VALUE-COPY (eager: materialize y_soft's forward values into an untracked const; out=OpSub(y_soft,ySoftConst)+yHardConst ⇒ forward=y_hard, backward grad flows via y_soft coeff 1). COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpAdd(logit+g)/OpMul(1/τ broadcast)/OpSoftmax; ST adds OpSub/OpAdd + argmax one-hot. matches nn.GumbelSoftmax(ctx,logits,noise,tau) + nn.GumbelSoftmaxHard(...) + SampleGumbelNoise(seed,shape). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/gumbel_test.go — ZeroNoiseIsTemperedSoftmax (noise=0 == softmax(logits/τ) @1e-9) + IsDistribution (rows nonneg sum-1) + LowTempApproachesOneHot (τ=0.01 ⇒ ≈one-hot at argmax(logit+g)) + SampleGumbelNoise (deterministic/finite + mean≈γ=0.5772 over 20k) + Gradcheck (logits, linear read-out) + HardStraightThrough (one-hot forward at argmax + ST grad == soft grad EXACTLY for linear loss — the ST property) + Errors + ExampleGumbelSoftmax | Jang 2016 arXiv:1611.01144 §2 Eq.1-2/§2.2 + Maddison 2016 arXiv:1611.00712 (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R176 | VQ-VAE vector quantization (van den Oord, Vinyals & Kavukcuoglu 2017, "Neural Discrete Representation Learning", NeurIPS, arXiv:1711.00937; DeepMind); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1-3.2 Eq.1-3 + DeepMind Sonnet vqvae.py, NOT /deep-research. Discrete latent representation learning (image/audio tokenizers, neural codecs): each encoder vector z_e maps to its NEAREST codebook entry. (1) Quantize (Eq.1-2): z_q=e_k, k=argmin_j‖z_e−e_j‖₂ (Euclidean NN; Sonnet dist=‖x‖²−2x·e+‖e‖²). (2) Straight-through (§3.2): decoder input z_q_st=z_e+sg(z_q−z_e) ⇒ forward=z_q, gradient copied identity to z_e (Sonnet: inputs+stop_gradient(quantized−inputs)). USES the OpStopGradient op (§T248). (3) VQ loss (Eq.3): recon(external) + codebook ‖sg[z_e]−e‖² (detaches ENCODER → trains codebook toward encoder) + β·commitment ‖z_e−sg[e]‖² (detaches CODEBOOK → trains encoder toward codebook); each side trained by exactly ONE term. (4) β=0.25 default (robust β∈[0.1,2.0]). z_q gathered via one-hot selection matmul (sel@codebook, differentiable→codebook); loss = mean(sqE+β·sqC) = mean(sqE)+β·mean(sqC). NOTE: finite-diff gradcheck INVALID through stop_gradient (sg blocks grad but forward value still tracks input) → verified vs EXACT CLOSED-FORM gradients instead. matches nn.VectorQuantizer{Codebook [K,D], Beta}.Quantize(ctx,ze)→(zqST,loss,indices) + NewVectorQuantizer(dtype,k,d,beta). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/vqvae_test.go — NearestAndForward (argmin L2 + zqST fwd==codebook[idx]) + StraightThroughGradient (grad_ze==coef identity, grad_codebook==0 detached from recon) + LossGradientRouting (loss parity @1e-9 + grad_ze==2β/N·(ze−zq) commit-only + grad_codebook==Σ 2/N·(e−ze) codebook-only, the two-way sg split) + ZeroBetaNoEncoderGradient (β=0 ⇒ encoder grad 0, codebook still trained) + Errors + ExampleVectorQuantizer | van den Oord 2017 arXiv:1711.00937 §3.1-3.2 Eq.1-3 + DeepMind Sonnet vqvae.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R177 | FSQ Finite Scalar Quantization (Mentzer, Minnen, Agustsson & Tschannen 2023, "Finite Scalar Quantization: VQ-VAE Made Simple", arXiv:2309.15505, ICLR'24; Google); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1/Fig.2/App.A.1 + google-research/fsq fsq.ipynb + lucidrains finite_scalar_quantization.py, NOT /deep-research. Drop-in codebook-FREE replacement for VQ-VAE (§R176): removes the learned codebook (⇒ no codebook collapse) AND all auxiliary losses. Per channel i with L_i levels round each scalar to a finite grid; implicit codebook = Cartesian product of per-channel level sets, size ∏L_i, ZERO stored params/losses. (1) Bound (App.A.1): h=(L−1)/2, offset o=0 odd/½ even, shift s=atanh(o/h); bounded=h·tanh(z+s)−o ⇒ round hits L symmetric levels ({−⌊L/2⌋..⌊L/2⌋} for odd). (2) Round STE (§3.1): ẑ=bounded+sg(round(bounded)−bounded) — round grad=identity (USES OpStopGradient §T248). (3) Normalize: code=ẑ/⌊L/2⌋ (=levels//2, DIFFERS from bounding half (L−1)/2 for even L — §V16-corrected from my (L−1)/2). (4) NO learned codebook, NO commitment/codebook/entropy loss (Fig.2 "Parameters: -", "Aux.Losses: -"). Flat index = mixed-radix Σ(round+⌊L/2⌋)·∏_{j<i}L_j. NOTE: finite-diff INVALID through sg → verified vs EXACT closed-form (odd L: d(code)/dz=1−tanh²(z)). matches nn.FSQ{Levels}.Quantize(ctx,z)→(codes,indices) + NewFSQ(levels) + CodebookSize() + Params()=nil. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/fsq_test.go — CodesOnGrid (code·⌊L/2⌋ integer, ≤L distinct/channel, idx∈[0,∏L)) + IndexEncoding (mixed-radix, L=3×3 corners 0/4/8/2) + StraightThroughGradient (grad_z==coef·(1−tanh²) closed-form) + NoParams + Errors + ExampleFSQ | Mentzer 2023 arXiv:2309.15505 §3.1/App.A.1 + google-research/fsq + lucidrains (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R178 | Auxiliary-Loss-Free Load Balancing (Wang, Gao, Chen, Xie & Dai 2024, "Auxiliary-Loss-Free Load Balancing Strategy for Mixture-of-Experts", arXiv:2408.15664; the MoE routing of DeepSeek-V3 arXiv:2412.19437 §2.1.2); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.3 + Algorithm 1 + DeepSeek-V3, NOT /deep-research. Balances MoE expert load WITHOUT the classic auxiliary balance loss (MoEBalanceLoss/Switch), whose gradient fights the LM objective and hurts quality. Keeps a per-expert BIAS b_i adjusted by a GRADIENT-FREE control rule: (1) SELECTION uses biased score — top-K experts by (s_i+b_i) (Eq.3: g_{i,t}=s_{i,t} if s_{i,t}+b_i∈Topk({s_j+b_j},K) else 0); bias added ONLY for selection. (2) WEIGHT = ORIGINAL affinity s_i (bias NOT added to the output-combining weight → no bias term in output, no gradient). (3) UPDATE (Algorithm 1): after each batch, load c_i = token count per expert, error e_i=c̄−c_i (c̄=mean load), b_i ← b_i + u·sign(e_i) — raise under-loaded, lower over-loaded. (4) b is a CONTROL variable, gradient-free (never in the autograd graph). (5) default u=0.001. Pure-utility (like an optimizer's state, no tensors/autograd). matches nn.LossFreeBalancer{Bias []float64, Rate}.Route(affinity,k)→(experts,weights) + Update(loads []int) + NewLossFreeBalancer(numExperts,u). Distinct from MoEBalanceLoss (§existing aux-loss). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/lossfree_test.go — BiasedSelection (top-k by s+b, weight==original s not s+b) + UpdateRule (loads[3,0,0]⇒bias[−u,u,u]; balanced⇒unchanged) + BalancesSkewedRouting (affinity=[1,0,0] but 300-round bias control ⇒ every expert ≥20% — the defining property) + Edge (k>N clamp, k≤0 nil, empty loads no-op, default u=0.001) + ExampleLossFreeBalancer | Wang 2024 arXiv:2408.15664 Eq.3/Alg.1 + DeepSeek-V3 arXiv:2412.19437 (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R179 | DropPath / Stochastic Depth (Huang, Sun, Liu, Sedra & Weinberger 2016, "Deep Networks with Stochastic Depth", ECCV, arXiv:1603.09382; modern per-sample form in timm/layers/drop.py, used by DeiT/Swin/ConvNeXt/ViT); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3 Eq.2/4/5 + timm drop_path(), NOT /deep-research. Regularizes deep residual nets by randomly dropping WHOLE residual branches — trains an implicit ensemble of shallower nets, stabilizing very deep transformers. Sibling to Dropout (§R55) but drops PER-SAMPLE branches, not per-element. (1) Original train: y=x+b_l·f_l(x), b_l≈Bernoulli(p_l), NO train rescale; (Eq.5) test scales branch by survival p_l. (2) Linear decay (Eq.4): p_l=1−(l/L)(1−p_L), p_0=1, p_L=0.5 typical (earlier layers survive more). (3) Modern timm DropPath (implemented): INVERTED dropout per-sample — train out=x·bernoulli(keep)/keep (keep=1−rate), identity at eval; E[out]=x. (4) Mask is ONE value PER SAMPLE (shape (N,1,...,1)) broadcast over all other dims — whole branch dropped together. No params; per-sample mask multiplied via OpMul → grad through Mul VJP (kept rows scaled by 1/keep, dropped rows 0). matches nn.DropPath{Rate,Training}.Forward(ctx,x)/Train/Eval + NewDropPath(rate,seed) + StochasticDepthSurvival(l,total,pLast)=1−(l/L)(1−pLast). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/droppath_test.go — PerSampleMask (row uniform, ∈{0,1/keep}, both drop+keep seen) + EvalIdentity (eval & rate0 == x) + ExpectationPreserved (mean≈x over 400 trials) + Gradient (per-sample grad 0 or 1/keep, uniform in row) + StochasticDepthSurvival (p(L,L)=pLast, p(0)=1, monotone, p(6,12,.5)=0.75) + Errors (rate≥1 panic) + ExampleDropPath | Huang 2016 arXiv:1603.09382 §3 Eq.2/4/5 + timm drop.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | med |
| R180 | ColBERT late-interaction MaxSim (Khattab & Zaharia 2020, "ColBERT: Efficient and Effective Passage Search via Contextualized Late Interaction over BERT", SIGIR, arXiv:2004.12832; Stanford); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.2-3.3 Eq.3 + stanford-futuredata/ColBERT colbert.py, NOT /deep-research. Multi-vector dense retrieval / RAG scoring: keep per-TOKEN embeddings for query+doc and score by LATE INTERACTION — each query token matches its best doc token, summed (Eq.3): S(q,d)=Σ_{i∈\|q\|} max_{j∈\|d\|} q̂_i·d̂_j. (1) Sum-over-query-tokens of max-over-doc-tokens (repo scores.max(1).values.sum(-1)). (2) Token sim = dot on L2-NORMALIZED embeddings = cosine ∈[−1,1] (repo F.normalize(Q/D,p=2,dim=2)); BOTH q and doc normalized. (3) Trained via pairwise softmax CE over S(q,d+)/S(q,d−). (4) Differentiable end-to-end (interaction has NO trainable params; max sub-differentiable → grad flows to argmax doc token per query token — GoAI OpMax extremumVJP routes to first-hit max). COMPOSED (differentiable) from qkL2NormalizeLastAxis(q,doc) + OpTranspose/OpMatMul(cosine matrix) + OpMax(axis1)/OpSum → grad w.r.t. q AND doc. NOTE: finite-diff gradcheck VALID here (no stop_gradient; max sub-diff away from ties). matches nn.MaxSim(ctx,q,doc). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/maxsim_test.go — Parity (== independent Σmax cosine @1e-9) + ExactMatchScoresNq (doc⊇query ⇒ score≈Nq) + Ranking (S(q,q)>S(q,rand)) + Gradcheck (q,doc == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleMaxSim | Khattab 2020 arXiv:2004.12832 §3.2-3.3 Eq.3 + stanford-futuredata/ColBERT colbert.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R181 | Classifier-Free Guidance for LLMs (Sanchez, Fan, Spangher, Levi, Ammanabrolu & Biderman 2023, "Stay on topic with Classifier-Free Guidance", arXiv:2306.17806); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.6-7/§2.2 + HuggingFace UnbatchedClassifierFreeGuidanceLogitsProcessor, NOT /deep-research. Inference-time PROMPT-ADHERENCE decoding (distinct from ContrastiveLogits §R82/DoLa §R174): run the model twice per step — WITH prompt c (conditional) + WITHOUT/negative prompt (unconditional) — and extrapolate in LOG-PROB space (Eq.7): log P̂(w)=log P(w)+γ·(log P(w\|c)−log P(w)) = γ·log P(w\|c)−(γ−1)·log P(w). γ=1 recovers the plain conditional; γ>1 (rec. ≈1.5, range 1.25–2) sharpens toward prompt-favored tokens. Equivalent γ-reweighted form P̂∝P(w\|c)^γ/P(w)^(γ−1) (Eq.6). Combine log_softmax of both branches, renormalize via softmax (matches HF scores_processed=γ·(logsoftmax_cond−logsoftmax_uncond)+logsoftmax_uncond). NEGATIVE prompting: unconditional branch = negative prompt c̄ ⇒ pushes AWAY (Eq.5). Pure-function decoding utility (like ContrastiveLogits/DoLaLogits); returns log-prob-space scores ready for a Sampler. matches nlp.GuidedLogits(conditional,unconditional,gamma). §V16 tier-1: nlp/cfg_test.go — GammaOneIsConditional (softmax(guided γ=1)==softmax(cond) @1e-9) + Formula (== lu+γ(lc−lu) @1e-12) + AmplifiesPromptAdherence (cond favors 0 / neg favors 1 ⇒ γ=2 raises P(0), lowers P(1)) + Panics + ExampleGuidedLogits | Sanchez 2023 arXiv:2306.17806 Eq.6-7/§2.2 + HuggingFace logits_process.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R182 | No-repeat n-gram blocking (HuggingFace NoRepeatNGramLogitsProcessor/_calc_banned_ngram_tokens + fairseq; tri-gram blocking from Paulus, Xiong & Socher 2017 "A Deep Reinforced Model for Abstractive Summarization", arXiv:1705.04304 §2.5); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs HF source + fairseq sequence_generator.py + Paulus §2.5, NOT /deep-research. HARD generation constraint (distinct from soft ApplyPenalties §R57): forbids repeating ANY n-gram. (1) Context = last n−1 tokens seq[L−(n−1):L]; for every prior i where seq[i:i+n−1]==context, ban the following token seq[i+n−1] (completing that n-gram would repeat it). (2) Banned tokens' logits = −∞ (hard block, unselectable this step). (3) No-op when n≤0 or L<n (HF guard cur_len+1<ngram_size ≡ my L<n: at L=n−1 history has no complete n-grams so no bans either way). (4) n=3 = classic tri-gram blocking (Paulus 2017 "never output the same trigram more than once"). Pure-function utility (mutates logits in place, ignores out-of-range history), applied before temperature/top-k like ApplyPenalties. matches nlp.NoRepeatNGramBlock(logits,generated,n). §V16 tier-1: nlp/ngram_test.go — Trigram ([1,2,3,1,2] n=3 ⇒ token 3=−∞, rest untouched) + BigramMultiple ([0,1,0,2,0] n=2 ⇒ 1&2 both banned) + Noop (L<n, n=0, empty) + OutOfRange (completing token ≥vocab ignored, no panic) + ExampleNoRepeatNGramBlock | Paulus 2017 arXiv:1705.04304 §2.5 + HF NoRepeatNGramLogitsProcessor + fairseq (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | med |
| R183 | Group Normalization (Wu & He 2018, "Group Normalization", ECCV, arXiv:1803.08494; FAIR); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.1-2/6-7 §3.1-3.2 Fig.3 + PyTorch GroupNorm docs, NOT /deep-research. Batch-independent normalization: split C channels into G groups, normalize each PER SAMPLE + PER GROUP (unlike BatchNorm) → stable at any batch size (vision/diffusion transformers, detectors). (1) For [N,C]: μ,σ² over the C/G channels of each group per sample; x̂=(x−μ)/√(σ²+ε). (2) BIASED (population) variance — divide by group size m, not m−1 (Eq.2). (3) Per-CHANNEL affine y=γ⊙x̂+β, γ,β learnable [C] (Eq.6, NOT per-group). (4) G=1 ⇒ LayerNorm (all channels one group); G=C ⇒ InstanceNorm (each channel alone). (5) ε=1e-5 default, G=32 default.
