COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpReshape([N,C]→[N,G,C/G])/OpMean(axis2 keepdims ×2 = biased μ,var)/OpSub/OpMul/OpAdd(ε)/OpSqrt/OpDiv + reshape-back + per-channel affine (γ,β [C]→[1,C]) → grad w.r.t. x,γ,β. matches nn.GroupNorm{Gamma,Beta [C],Groups,Eps}.Forward(ctx,x [batch,C]) + NewGroupNorm(dtype,groups,c). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/groupnorm_test.go — Parity (== independent per-group biased-var ref @1e-9) + G1EqualsLayerNorm (G=1 == existing nn.LayerNorm oracle @1e-9, the reduction) + Standardizes (γ=1/β=0 ⇒ each group mean≈0 var≈1) + Gradcheck (x,γ,β == finite-diff) + Errors (groups∤C panic, rank≠2) + ExampleGroupNorm | Wu & He 2018 arXiv:1803.08494 Eq.1-2/6 §3.1-3.2 + PyTorch (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | med |
| R184 | Barlow Twins self-supervised loss (Zbontar, Jing, Misra, LeCun & Deny 2021, "Barlow Twins: Self-Supervised Learning via Redundancy Reduction", ICML, arXiv:2103.03230; FAIR); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.1-2 §2 + facebookresearch/barlowtwins main.py, NOT /deep-research. NEGATIVE-FREE SSL (distinct from contrastive InfoNCE §R165 — no negatives, no temperature, no momentum encoder): make two augmented views agree by pushing their cross-correlation toward IDENTITY, avoiding collapse via redundancy reduction. (1) Standardize each of the D features to mean-0/unit-std over the BATCH (BatchNorm1d affine=False, ε=1e-5). (2) Cross-correlation C=(Ẑᴬ)ᵀẐᴮ/N ∈[−1,1]^{D×D}. (3) Loss (Eq.1): L=Σ_i(1−C_ii)² + λ·Σ_{i≠j}C_ij² — invariance (diagonal→1, features agree across views) + redundancy-reduction (off-diag→0, decorrelate features); FAIR code on_diag=diagonal(c).add(−1).pow(2).sum()+λ·off_diagonal(c).pow(2).sum(). (4) λ=0.005 (paper; FAIR code default 0.0051). COMPOSED (differentiable) from batch-standardize (OpMean(axis0)×2/OpSub/OpMul/OpAdd(ε)/OpSqrt/OpDiv) + OpTranspose/OpMatMul(C=ẐᴬᵀẐᴮ/N) + weighted (1 diag/λ off) squared-error vs I + OpSum → grad w.r.t. za,zb. matches nn.BarlowTwinsLoss(ctx,za,zb [N,D],lambda). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/barlow_test.go — Parity (== independent standardize+cross-corr ref @1e-9) + IdentityCorrelationIsZero (orthogonal-feature identical views ⇒ C=I ⇒ loss≈0) + RedundancyPenalty (identical features ⇒ loss≈2λ, scales linearly with λ) + Gradcheck (za,zb == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleBarlowTwinsLoss | Zbontar 2021 arXiv:2103.03230 Eq.1-2 §2 + facebookresearch/barlowtwins main.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R185 | Flow Matching / Rectified Flow generative objective (Lipman, Chen, Ben-Hamu, Nickel & Le 2022 "Flow Matching for Generative Modeling", arXiv:2210.02747 OT-CFM; Liu, Gong & Liu 2022 "Rectified Flow", arXiv:2209.03003; the objective of Stable Diffusion 3, Esser et al. 2024 arXiv:2403.03206); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.1-2/9/22-23 + TorchCFM + facebookresearch/flow_matching, NOT /deep-research. Diversifies to GENERATIVE modeling (multimodal/diffusion-alternative): train a network to transport noise→data along the straight-line OT path by regressing a velocity field — no diffusion SDE, no noise schedule, just an MSE. (1) Straight-line path: x_t=(1−t)x0+t·x1 (x0≈N(0,I) noise at t=0, x1 data at t=1). (2) CONSTANT target velocity u_t=dx_t/dt=x1−x0 (t-independent — the key simplification). (3) CFM loss (Eq.9/23): L=E‖v_θ(x_t,t)−(x1−x0)‖² MSE (TorchCFM compute_ut→x1−x0, mean((vt−ut)²)). (4) Sampling: x0≈N(0,I), integrate ODE dx/dt=v_θ forward t=0→1 (Euler x←x+dt·v). SD3 = same structure, reversed t-convention. COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpMul(scalar)/OpAdd(interp) + OpSub(target x1−x0, residual)/OpMul/OpMean(MSE) + OpMul/OpAdd(Euler) → grad w.r.t. vPred (model output). matches nn.FlowInterpolate(ctx,x0,x1,t) + FlowMatchingLoss(ctx,vPred,x0,x1) + FlowEulerStep(ctx,x,v,dt). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/flowmatch_test.go — Interpolate (t=0→x0, 1→x1, ½→mid @1e-9) + MatchingLoss (== mean‖v−(x1−x0)‖² + zero at perfect pred) + EulerRecoversTarget (Euler(x0,x1−x0,dt=1)=x1 exactly — the straight-line property) + Gradcheck (vPred == finite-diff) + Errors + ExampleFlowMatchingLoss | Lipman 2022 arXiv:2210.02747 Eq.9/23 + Liu 2022 arXiv:2209.03003 Eq.1-2 + SD3 (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R186 | DDPM Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models (Ho, Jain & Abbeel 2020, NeurIPS, arXiv:2006.11239; UC Berkeley); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.4/11/14 §2-3.4 + HF diffusers DDPMScheduler + lucidrains denoising-diffusion-pytorch, NOT /deep-research. The canonical DIFFUSION generative objective, counterpart to Flow Matching (§R185: DDPM=SDE/noise-schedule/ε-pred vs Flow=ODE/linear/velocity). (1) Linear noise schedule β_1..β_T from 1e-4 to 0.02 (T=1000); α_t=1−β_t, ᾱ_t=Π_{s≤t}α_s (1→0). (2) Closed-form forward (Eq.4): q(x_t\|x0)=N(√ᾱ_t x0,(1−ᾱ_t)I) ⇒ x_t=√ᾱ_t·x0+√(1−ᾱ_t)·ε (variance-preserving, ε≈N(0,I)). (3) Simplified loss (Eq.14): L=E‖ε−ε_θ(x_t,t)‖² — ε-PREDICTION MSE (not x0/velocity; lucidrains pred_noise/F.mse_loss, HF prediction_type=epsilon). (4) Reverse mean (Eq.11): μ_θ=(1/√α_t)(x_t−(β_t/√(1−ᾱ_t))ε_θ), σ_t²=β_t or posterior β̃_t. x0-prediction inverse x0=(x_t−√(1−ᾱ)ε)/√ᾱ (DDIM; recovers x0 exactly with true ε). COMPOSED from scalar-muls + OpAdd/OpSub/OpMul/OpMean → DDPMLoss differentiable w.r.t. ε_θ. matches nn.DDPMSchedule{Beta,Alpha,AlphaBar} + NewDDPMSchedule(T,βstart,βend) + DDPMForward(ctx,x0,eps,ᾱ) + DDPMLoss(ctx,epsPred,eps) + DDPMPredictX0(ctx,xt,eps,ᾱ). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/ddpm_test.go — Schedule (linear β, α=1−β, ᾱ cumprod-decreasing; default ᾱ ≈1→≈0) + Forward (Eq.4 @1e-9, endpoints, variance-preserve) + PredictX0RecoversX0 (inverse of forward, exact) + Loss (Eq.14 MSE + zero at perfect) + Gradcheck (epsPred) + Errors + ExampleDDPMSchedule/Forward | Ho 2020 arXiv:2006.11239 Eq.4/11/14 + HF diffusers + lucidrains (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R187 | DDIM Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models sampler (Song, Meng & Ermon 2020/2021 ICLR, arXiv:2010.02502; Stanford); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.12/16 §4.1-4.2 + HF Diffusion Course + garg-aayush DDIM.md, NOT /deep-research. Accelerated + optionally-DETERMINISTIC diffusion sampler that reuses a DDPM-trained ε-model (§R186): non-Markovian reverse ⇒ 10-50 steps instead of T, and a probability-flow ODE at η=0. Reverse step (Eq.12) from t to an earlier step: x_prev=√ᾱ_prev·x̂0 + √(1−ᾱ_prev−σ²)·ε_θ + σ·z, where x̂0=(x_t−√(1−ᾱ_t)ε_θ)/√ᾱ_t (=DDPMPredictX0 §R186 reused). σ_t=η·√((1−ᾱ_prev)/(1−ᾱ_t))·√(1−ᾱ_t/ᾱ_prev) (Eq.16). η∈[0,1]: η=0 ⇒ σ=0 DETERMINISTIC (no z, implicit ODE), η=1 ⇒ original DDPM ancestral stochastic. ACCELERATED: ᾱ_t/ᾱ_prev are for an arbitrary SUBSEQUENCE τ⊂{1..T} (§4.2). Note paper's α_t ≡ DDPM's ᾱ_t. COMPOSED from DDPMPredictX0 + scalar-muls/OpAdd → deterministic w.r.t. inputs. matches nn.DDIMStep(ctx,xt,epsPred,alphaBarT,alphaBarPrev,eta,z). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/ddim_test.go — DeterministicFormula (η=0 == √ᾱ_prev·x̂0+√(1−ᾱ_prev)·ε @1e-9) + ForwardConsistency (η=0 + true ε: DDIM(forward(x0,ᾱ_t))==forward(x0,ᾱ_prev) EXACTLY — the defining property) + StochasticParity (η=1 full Eq.12/16 incl σ·z) + FinalStepAndDeterminism (ᾱ_prev=1 ⇒ x̂0; η=0 ignores z) + Errors + ExampleDDIMStep | Song 2020 arXiv:2010.02502 Eq.12/16 §4.1-4.2 + HF Diffusion Course (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R188 | RWKV-4 WKV operator (Peng et al. 2023, "RWKV: Reinventing RNNs for the Transformer Era", EMNLP Findings, arXiv:2305.13048 §3.1.2 Eq.16/14); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary + ACL Anthology PDF + johanwind blog, NOT /deep-research. Extends the linear-attention/RNN family (Mamba/RetNet/GLA/DeltaNet/GatedDeltaNet → +RWKV) with a genuinely DIFFERENT mechanism: NOT a matrix-valued state but D INDEPENDENT SCALAR weighted-averages (channel-wise) — Transformer-quality with RNN O(1)/step inference. wkv_t=(Σ_{i<t} e^{−(t−1−i)w+k_i}v_i + e^{u+k_t}v_t)/(Σ_{i<t} e^{−(t−1−i)w+k_i} + e^{u+k_t}): recency-decayed weighted sum of past values (per-channel decay w>0; NOTE offset (t−1−i) ⇒ the immediately-previous token i=t−1 has ZERO decay) + current token with per-channel BONUS u (e^{u+k_t}, not decayed). t=1 ⇒ wkv_1=v_1. w,u are [D] vectors. Full time-mixing (caller): o_t=W_o·(σ(r_t)⊙wkv_t), receptance gate — out of core-WKV scope. IMPLEMENTED as the numerically-stable running-max recurrence (aa/bb/pp per channel), pure-f64 forward utility (like RetentionRecurrent §R114). matches nn.WKV(k,v [seq,D], w,u [D]). §V16 tier-1: nn/rwkv_test.go — Parity (stable kernel == direct formula @1e-9) + FirstTokenIsValue (wkv_1=v_1) + UniformIsRunningMean (w=k=u=0 ⇒ running mean) + LargeBonusIsCurrentToken (huge u ⇒ v_t; caught decay≠bonus subtlety — large w alone does NOT isolate current token since i=t−1 undecayed) + Errors + ExampleWKV | Peng 2023 arXiv:2305.13048 §3.1.2 Eq.16 + ACL Anthology + johanwind (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R189 | VICReg Variance-Invariance-Covariance Regularization (Bardes, Ponce & LeCun 2021/2022 ICLR, "VICReg", arXiv:2105.04906; Meta AI/Inria); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §4.1 Eq.1-6 + facebookresearch/vicreg main_vicreg.py, NOT /deep-research. NEGATIVE-FREE SSL, distinct from Barlow Twins (§R184: VICReg=3 EXPLICIT regularizers vs Barlow=cross-correlation-to-identity): prevents collapse with a variance HINGE (a mechanism no other method has). Three terms on views za,zb [N,D] (§4.1): (1) invariance s=(1/N)Σ_i‖z_i−z'_i‖² MSE (Eq.5; NOTE paper /N vs ref F.mse_loss /(N·D) differ by D — IMPLEMENTED the paper form). (2) variance v(Z)=(1/D)Σ_j max(0, γ−√(Var_j+ε)) per-dim hinge (Eq.1-2; γ=1, ε=1e-4, unbiased N−1 batch var; blocks collapse by forcing each dim std≥1). (3) covariance c(Z)=(1/D)Σ_{i≠j}Cov_ij² off-diagonal-squared (Eq.3-4; Cov=(1/(N−1))·centeredᵀcentered; decorrelates dims). Total ℓ=λs+μ[v(za)+v(zb)]+ν[c(za)+c(zb)] (Eq.6); λ=μ=25, ν=1 default. COMPOSED (differentiable) from OpSub/OpMul/OpSum(invariance) + center/OpSqrt/OpReLU(variance hinge) + OpTranspose/OpMatMul/off-diag-mask(covariance) → grad w.r.t. za,zb; coefficients folded into pre-reduction scale (avoids rank-0 broadcast). matches nn.VICRegLoss(ctx,za,zb [N,D],lambda,mu,nu). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/vicreg_test.go — Parity (== independent 3-term ref @1e-9) + VarianceHingePenalizesCollapse (identical-rows ⇒ std≈0 ⇒ loss≈μ·2·γ=49.5, the anti-collapse mechanism) + SpreadDecorrelated (orthogonal high-var cols ⇒ loss≈0) + Gradcheck (za,zb) + Errors (N<2, shape) + ExampleVICRegLoss | Bardes 2021 arXiv:2105.04906 §4.1 Eq.1-6 + facebookresearch/vicreg (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R190 | LLM.int8() 8-bit matmul with outlier decomposition (Dettmers, Lewis, Belkada & Zettlemoyer 2022, NeurIPS, arXiv:2208.07339; the bitsandbytes method); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1 Eq.7/§3.2 Eq.8 + HF hf-bitsandbytes-integration blog, NOT /deep-research. Inference quantization DISTINCT from weight-only GPTQ(§R141)/AWQ(§R143) + smoothing SmoothQuant(§R172): mixed-precision to handle the systematic OUTLIER feature dims that emerge in large transformers (would crush naive int8). (1) VECTOR-WISE int8 (Eq.7): per-ROW X absmax scale s_x[i], per-COL W absmax scale s_w[j] → X̂,Ŵ int8 (round(127··/absmax)); int32 accum; dequant by OUTER PRODUCT Y=(X̂Ŵ)·s_x[i]·s_w[j]/127² (Cx⊗Cw/127², Fig.2). (2) MIXED-PRECISION DECOMPOSITION (Eq.8): a feature dim h is an OUTLIER if any \|X[:,h]\|≥α (α=6.0 default); Y=X[:,O]·W[O,:] (fp16/high-prec) + dequant(X̂[:,R]·Ŵ[R,:]) (int8). \|O\|≤7 for ≤13B. (3) The split X·W=X[:,O]·W[O,:]+X[:,R]·W[R,:] is EXACT (Eq.8's ≈ is only int8 quant of R). Pure-f64 inference utility (like AWQ/GPTQ/SmoothQuant), not differentiable. matches nn.LLMInt8MatMul(x [t,C_in], w [C_in,C_out], threshold)→(y,outlierDims). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/llmint8_test.go — OutlierDetection (\|X[:,h]\|≥threshold flagged) + DecompositionExact (threshold=0 ⇒ all fp16 ⇒ ==X·W @1e-9) + BeatsNaiveWithOutliers (×60 outlier col ⇒ mixed(α=6) error ≪ 0.1·naive-int8(all) error — THE defining benefit) + NoOutlierApprox (N(0,1) ⇒ pure int8 rel-err <5%) + Errors + ExampleLLMInt8MatMul | Dettmers 2022 arXiv:2208.07339 §3.1 Eq.7/§3.2 Eq.8 + HF blog (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R191 | Contrastive Search decoding / SimCTG (Su, Lan, Wang, Yogatama, Kong & Collier 2022, "A Contrastive Framework for Neural Text Generation", NeurIPS, arXiv:2202.06417); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Eq.3/5 §3.1-3.2/§4 + HF introducing-csearch blog, NOT /deep-research. Inference decoding DISTINCT from the distribution-combining decoders (ContrastiveLogits §R82 / DoLa §R174 / CFG §R181): a REPRESENTATION-similarity criterion that counters degenerate repetition. Rule (Eq.5): x_t=argmax_{v∈top-k} {(1−α)·p_θ(v\|x_<t) − α·max_{1≤j≤t−1} cos(h_v, h_{x_j})} — model CONFIDENCE (prob) minus a DEGENERATION PENALTY = MAX (not mean) cosine similarity of the candidate's last-layer representation h_v to every prior token's, cos∈[−1,1]. α∈[0,1]: α=0 ⇒ greedy; larger α ⇒ stronger anti-repetition. Defaults α=0.6, k=8 (k≈3-10). h_v from concat(x_<t,v) at the output layer (caller supplies representations). Pure-function decoding utility. matches nlp.ContrastiveScore(probs,maxSim,alpha) + MaxContextCosine(candReps,contextReps [][]float64) (per-candidate max cosine to context) + cosine helper. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/contrastive_search_test.go — Formula (== (1−α)p−α·maxSim) + GreedyAtZero (α=0 argmax==prob argmax) + AntiRepetitionAtOne (α=1 ⇒ least-similar) + Tradeoff (high-prob-repetitive loses to novel at α=0.6) + MaxContextCosine (max cosine; empty context→0) + Panic + ExampleContrastiveScore | Su 2022 arXiv:2202.06417 Eq.3/5 §3.2/§4 + HF csearch blog (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R192 | SimCTG contrastive TRAINING loss (Su, Lan, Wang, Yogatama, Kong & Collier 2022, arXiv:2202.06417 §3.1 Eq.2); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1 Eq.2/3/4 + official github.com/yxuansu/SimCTG lossfunction.py, NOT /deep-research. The TRAINING-time counterpart of Contrastive-Search decoding (§R191): MLE-only training leaves token representations ANISOTROPIC (packed in a narrow cone, pairwise cos≈1) which is what makes greedy decoding degenerate — this loss decorrelates/isotropizes them so the degeneration penalty of contrastive search becomes discriminative. L_CL (Eq.2) = (1/(n(n−1)))·Σ_i Σ_{j≠i} max(0, ρ − s(h_i,h_i) + s(h_i,h_j)), s=cosine (Eq.3), h_i = last/output-layer token representation, ρ = margin. Since s(h_i,h_i)=1 each off-diag term = max(0, ρ−1+s(h_i,h_j)): a hinge that is 0 once two DISTINCT tokens' cosine drops below 1−ρ else penalizes them. Default ρ=0.5 (§6.2/App.E; ρ=0 ⇒ degenerates to MLE). Full objective L=L_MLE+L_CL EQUALLY weighted (Eq.4, no coefficient). DIFFERENTIABLE scalar (composed L2-normalize+matmul+diag-mask+ReLU+off-mask+sum), reads gold=diag(S) like the ref impl so the L2 ε cancels. matches nn.SimCTGContrastiveLoss(h [seq,dim], margin)→scalar + const SimCTGMargin=0.5. §V16 tier-1/§V2/V-GRAD: nn/simctg_test.go — Formula(==cosine-hinge ref @1e-9) + OrthogonalIsZero(cos0<1−ρ ⇒ 0, also proves diagonal excluded) + IdenticalPairsGiveMargin(cos1 ⇒ ρ) + ZeroMarginIsZero(ρ=0 ⇒ 0 any reps) + MarginIncreasesLoss(monotone in ρ) + Gradcheck(clustered active-hinge reps, central-diff @1e-4) + Errors + ExampleSimCTGContrastiveLoss | Su 2022 arXiv:2202.06417 §3.1 Eq.2/3/4 + yxuansu/SimCTG lossfunction.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R193 | Wanda pruning (Sun, Liu, Bhojanapalli, Vishwanathan & Kolter 2023/2024, "A Simple and Effective Pruning Approach for Large Language Models", ICLR 2024, arXiv:2306.11695); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3 Eq.1/2 + official github.com/locuslab/wanda lib/prune.py + torchao WandaSparsifier, NOT /deep-research. Post-training COMPRESSION distinct from the quantizers (GPTQ §R141/AWQ §R143/NF4/SmoothQuant §R172/LLM.int8 §R190 change bit-width) and from merging (TIES/DARE): it ZEROS weights (sparsity). Saliency (Eq.1) S_ij=\|W_ij\|·‖X_j‖₂ where ‖X_j‖₂ = L2 norm of input feature j over ALL N×L calibration tokens (one scalar per input channel, shared by every output reading j) — a small weight on a loud feature beats a large weight on a dead one (the win over magnitude pruning). COMPARISON GROUP per-OUTPUT (Eq.2): rank/prune within each output neuron's incoming C_in weights (per row of [C_out,C_in]; here per COLUMN of the codebase's [C_in,C_out] X·W layout), NOT global/per-input. NO weight update — surviving weights unchanged (Table-1 distinction from SparseGPT's inverse-Hessian update). Unstructured (target ratio) + structured N:M (2:4/4:8: zero the n smallest per m consecutive inputs). Pure-f64 utility (like the quantizers), not differentiable. matches nn.WandaScore(w [C_in,C_out], x [tok,C_in])→S + WandaPrune(w,x,sparsity)→(pruned,mask) + WandaPruneNM(w,x,n,m)→(pruned,mask). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/wanda_test.go — ScoreFormula(==\|W\|·‖X‖ ref @1e-9) + KeepsHighActivationSmallWeight(0.1·loud kept over 1.0·quiet — THE metric win) + SparsityLevel(⌊s·C_in⌋ zeros/col) + PerOutputComparisonGroup(each col to own k, not global) + NMStructured(2:4 keeps 2/block) + SparsityEdges(0=no-op,1=all-zero) + Errors + ExampleWandaPrune | Sun 2023 arXiv:2306.11695 §3 Eq.1/2 + locuslab/wanda prune.py + torchao (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R194 | SparseGPT one-shot pruning (Frantar & Alistarh 2023, "SparseGPT: Massive Language Models Can Be Accurately Pruned in One-Shot", ICML 2023, arXiv:2301.00774); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary Alg.1/Eq.3-4 §3.2-3.4 + official github.com/IST-DASLab/sparsegpt sparsegpt.py, NOT /deep-research. The ACCURACY-tier sibling of Wanda (§R193) and pruning analog of GPTQ (§R141): same OBS inverse-Hessian machinery as GPTQ but ZEROS lowest-saliency weights (mask) instead of rounding, and — unlike Wanda's no-update — COMPENSATES via a weight update (the Table-1 Wanda-vs-SparseGPT distinction). Reconstruction Hessian H=2XXᵀ (paper writes XXᵀ; overall scale CANCELS in the saliency ratio + compensation under relative damping, so GPTQ-consistent 2XXᵀ is equivalent), diag-dampened, Hinv=upper Cholesky of H⁻¹ (as GPTQ). Saliency (Alg.1) ε=W[r,c]²/[Hinv]_cc² (SQUARED inv-Hessian Cholesky diagonal; code W1²/diag(Hinv1)²); prune ⌊sparsity⌋ smallest-ε PER ROW (per output, Alg.1) in blocks of Bs=128 cols using the running-compensated weights. On prune W[r,c]→0, error e=W[r,c]/[Hinv]_cc propagated to cols j>c via W[:,j]-=e·Hinv[c,j] (OBS/GPTQ update; kept weights error-0). NOTE paper-vs-code: Alg.1 masks per-row (impl'd, exact per-row sparsity + matches code N:M path); released code's UNSTRUCTURED path instead thresholds each block globally. Immediate full-propagation to all j>c is numerically identical to the paper's block+global update. Pure-f64 utility (like GPTQ/Wanda), not differentiable. matches nn.SparseGPTPrune(w [out,in], x [in,samples], sparsity, ...opt)→(pruned,mask) + WithSparseGPTDamp/Block. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/sparsegpt_test.go — ReconstructionBeatsMagnitude(‖(W−Ŵ)X‖_F strictly < magnitude prune — THE OBS benefit) + ZeroSparsityIsIdentity(==W exact, mask 1s) + FullSparsityIsZero + SparsityLevel(⌊s·in⌋/row) + DiagonalHessianNoCompensation(orthogonal X ⇒ diag H ⇒ kept weight EXACTLY unchanged, prunes smaller) + Errors + ExampleSparseGPTPrune | Frantar 2023 arXiv:2301.00774 Alg.1/Eq.3-4 §3.2-3.4 + IST-DASLab/sparsegpt sparsegpt.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R195 | Medusa tree-based speculative decoding primitives (Cai, Li, Geng, Peng, Chen, Lee & Dao 2024, "Medusa: Simple LLM Inference Acceleration Framework with Multiple Decoding Heads", ICML 2024, arXiv:2401.10774); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §2.3.1 + §2.1.2/§2.3.2/Fig.2 + official github.com/FasterDecoding/Medusa medusa/model/utils.py, NOT /deep-research. K extra decoding heads predict tokens t+1…t+K in parallel; their top candidates form a TREE verified in ONE forward pass. Two mechanisms provided as pure fns (INFERENCE accel, distinct from the exact-rejection SpeculativeSample §R53 which needs a draft model): (A) TREE ATTENTION (§2.1.2/§2.3.2/Fig.2) — each candidate token attends ONLY to its ancestors on the root-path (never sibling branches), so one pass scores every root-to-node path; position ids = node DEPTH in the tree. Ancestor reachability is definitional/combinatorial (§V16-exempt); the Medusa framing is paper-cited. A linear chain reproduces the causal mask. (B) TYPICAL ACCEPTANCE (§2.3.1, adapted from Hewitt et al. 2022 typical sampling §R91) — accept candidate iff p_orig(x) > min(ε, δ·exp(−H(p_orig))), H=Shannon entropy in nats; NOT distribution-exact (unlike §R53) but keeps only "typical" tokens; high entropy (uncertain) ⇒ lower threshold ⇒ accept more, low entropy ⇒ stricter (near δ). First/greedy token unconditionally accepted; decode takes the LONGEST accepted prefix of a path. NOTE paper-vs-code: numeric defaults ε=0.3 (posterior_threshold), δ=0.09 (posterior_alpha) are from the REF CODE utils.py, NOT the paper text (which only sweeps ε∈[0.01,0.25] + notes Hewitt α=√ε §3.3.2). matches nlp.MedusaTreeMask(parent []int)→(mask [n,n] additive 0/−∞, depth []int) + TypicalAcceptance(probs,token,ε,δ)→bool + consts MedusaEpsilon/MedusaDelta. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/medusa_test.go — TreeMaskChainIsCausal + TreeMaskBranches(ancestor-only, no sibling attn, depths) + TreeMaskPanic + TypicalAcceptanceFormula(==min(ε,δexp(−H))) + Peaked(top accept/tail reject) + EntropyEffect(same p=0.05 accepted@highH rejected@lowH — THE typical behavior) + Panic + Examples | Cai 2024 arXiv:2401.10774 §2.1.2/§2.3.1/§2.3.2 + FasterDecoding/Medusa utils.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R196 | Sinkhorn-Knopp entropic optimal transport (Cuturi 2013, "Sinkhorn Distances: Lightspeed Computation of Optimal Transport", NeurIPS, arXiv:1306.0895) + SwAV online clustering (Caron, Misra, Mairal, Goyal, Bojanowski & Joulin 2020, "Unsupervised Learning of Visual Features by Contrasting Cluster Assignments", NeurIPS, arXiv:2006.09882); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Cuturi Eq.2/Lemma2/§4.1 Alg.1 + SwAV Eq.3-5 §3.1 + POT ot.bregman.sinkhorn docs, NOT /deep-research. Enables the OT/doubly-stochastic SSL path missing next to Barlow/InfoNCE/VICReg/MaxSim (unlocks SwAV/DINO-style equipartitioned clustering). Objective (Cuturi Eq.2, ε=1/λ): P*=argmin_P ⟨P,C⟩ − ε·H(P) s.t. P·1=r, Pᵀ·1=c, H(P)=−ΣP_ij(logP_ij−1). Solved by Sinkhorn-Knopp on the Gibbs kernel K=exp(−C/ε): u=v=1, iterate u←r⊘(Kv), v←c⊘(Kᵀu); plan P=diag(u)·K·diag(v) (POT docs identical: K=exp(−M/reg), u=a/(Kv), v=b/(Kᵀu), P=diag(u)K diag(v)). P≥0; row sums→r, col sums→c (doubly-stochastic w.r.t. marginals); a CONSTANT SHIFT of C rescales K by a scalar absorbed into u,v ⇒ P unchanged (used for the min-cost numerical stabilization). SwAV (Eq.4-5): Sinkhorn-Knopp on exp(scores/ε) under EQUIPARTITION (uniform marginals Q1=1/K·1, Qᵀ1=1/B·1), only ≈3 iterations ("fast and sufficient", code --sinkhorn_iterations=3). SIGN nuance: SwAV writes max Tr(QᵀCᵀZ)+εH = mirror of the min-cost −εH under C=neg-similarity. Pure-f64 utility (SwAV applies under stop-gradient for the target code), not differentiable. matches nn.Sinkhorn(cost [m,n], r [m], c [n], eps, iters)→P [m,n]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/sinkhorn_test.go — Marginals(row/col sums→r,c @1e-6) + Nonnegative + ZeroCostOuterProduct(uniform K ⇒ P=r·cᵀ exact @1e-9 — closed form) + Convergence(err↓ with iters) + DoublyStochastic(uniform sq ⇒ rows&cols=1/n, SwAV) + Errors(rank/len/eps≤0/iters<1/mass-imbalance) + ExampleSinkhorn | Cuturi 2013 arXiv:1306.0895 Eq.2/§4.1 + Caron 2020 arXiv:2006.09882 §3.1 Eq.3-5 + POT docs (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R197 | SwAV swapped-prediction loss (Caron, Misra, Mairal, Goyal, Bojanowski & Joulin 2020, arXiv:2006.09882 §3 Eq.1/2); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3 Eq.1/2 + official github.com/facebookresearch/swav main_swav.py, NOT /deep-research. The clustering-based SSL objective that COMPLETES SwAV atop the Sinkhorn codes (§R196) — distinct from instance-contrastive InfoNCE, redundancy-reduction Barlow, variance-covariance VICReg: two augmented views projected onto K trainable PROTOTYPES, each view's features predict the OTHER view's (detached Sinkhorn) code. Loss (Eq.1): L=ℓ(z_t,q_s)+ℓ(z_s,q_t) — SWAPPED (t predicts s's code, s predicts t's). Per-term (Eq.2, NOT Eq.6 — research corrected the guess): ℓ(z,q)=−Σ_k q^(k) log p^(k), p^(k)=softmax_k(z·c_k/τ). Softmax temperature default τ=0.1 (code --temperature). Codes q are DETACHED targets (stop-gradient; code out.detach() then distributed_sinkhorn); gradient flows only through the prediction p (features+prototypes). Official code EXACTLY: subloss −= mean(sum(q * log_softmax(x/τ))). DIFFERENTIABLE via a stable soft cross-entropy (logits/τ → max-shift log-softmax → −q·logp → mean-batch), composed from OpMul/OpMax/OpSub/OpExp/OpSum/OpLog. matches nn.SwAVLoss(scoresT,scoresS,codeT,codeS [B,K], temp)→scalar + const SwAVTemperature=0.1. §V16 tier-1/§V2/V-GRAD: nn/swav_test.go — Formula(==swapped-pred ref @1e-9) + UniformClosedForm(uniform scores+codes ⇒ L=2·lnK) + SwappedStructure(codes enter cross: self-aligned pairing < cross pairing — proves T pairs with codeS) + Gradcheck(wrt both score matrices, central-diff @1e-4) + WithSinkhornCodes(end-to-end Sinkhorn→SwAV finite+positive) + Errors + ExampleSwAVLoss | Caron 2020 arXiv:2006.09882 §3 Eq.1/2 + facebookresearch/swav main_swav.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R198 | LSQ — Learned Step Size Quantization (Esser, McKinstry, Bablani, Appuswamy & Modha 2020, "Learned Step Size Quantization", ICLR 2020, arXiv:1902.08153); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §2 Eq.1-2 + §2.1 Eq.3 + §2.2 grad-scale + §2.1 init (ar5iv full text), NOT /deep-research. The FIRST quantization-aware TRAINING (QAT) method here — distinct from all the post-training quantizers (GPTQ §R141/AWQ §R143/NF4/SmoothQuant §R172/LLM.int8 §R190/Wanda §R193/SparseGPT §R194): fake-quantizes weights/activations DURING training with a LEARNABLE step size s trained by SGD, so the net adapts to low-bit inference. Forward (Eq.1-2): v̂=round(clip(v/s,−Qn,Qp))·s. Bounds (§2 after Eq.2): signed/weights Qn=2^(b−1),Qp=2^(b−1)−1; unsigned/act Qn=0,Qp=2^b−1. STE gradient (Eq.3): ∂v̂/∂v=1 in clip interval else 0; ∂v̂/∂s=−v/s+round(v/s) in range, −Qn below, Qp above. Grad SCALE (§2.2): loss grad wrt s ×= g=1/√(N·Qp) to balance s's updates with the weights'. Init (§2.1): s=2·mean(\|v\|)/√Qp. KEY INSIGHT: composes ENTIRELY from existing ops (no new backend op) — round-STE via StopGradient like FSQ (§T248): v̂bar=vbar+sg(round(vbar)−vbar); the (÷s,clip,STE-round,×s) autograd composition AUTOMATICALLY yields the exact LSQ ∂/∂s (incl. the −v/s and saturation branches, via OpClip's in-range VJP); grad-scale via s'=g·s+sg((1−g)·s) (value s, grad ×g). Differentiable wrt v(STE) AND s(LSQ). matches nn.LSQQuantize(v [rows,cols], s [1,1], qn, qp, gradScale)→v̂ + LSQBounds(bits,signed)→(qn,qp) + LSQGradScale(numel,qp) + LSQInitStep(v,qp). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/lsq_test.go — ForwardParity(==round(clip)·s @1e-12) + QuantizationGrid(out/s ∈ integer [−Qn,Qp]) + ClipSaturation(±Qp·s/−Qn·s) + Bounds + GradScaleAndInit + InputGradientSTE(∂/∂v=1 in-clip/0 out — DEFINED STE convention, NOT finite-diff which is invalid through round) + StepSizeGradient(∂L/∂s==gradScale·Σ[round−v/s\|−Qn\|Qp] closed form) + Errors + ExampleLSQQuantize | Esser 2020 arXiv:1902.08153 §2 Eq.1-3/§2.1-2.2 (ar5iv PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R199 | DINO self-distillation loss (Caron, Touvron, Misra, Jégou, Mairal, Bojanowski & Joulin 2021, "Emerging Properties in Self-Supervised Vision Transformers", ICCV 2021, arXiv:2104.14294); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1 Eq.1-2/Eq.4 + §3.2/§5.3 + official github.com/facebookresearch/dino main_dino.py, NOT /deep-research. SELF-DISTILLATION SSL — student matches a momentum TEACHER (EMA of student, nn.EMA), NO labels/negatives/clustering; distinct from InfoNCE(contrastive)/Barlow-VICReg(statistics)/SwAV(Sinkhorn codes). Anti-collapse by TWO opposing ops on the teacher: CENTERING (subtract running mean C ⇒ no single dim dominates) + SHARPENING (teacher temp τ_t < τ_s ⇒ target stays peaked); they balance. P_s=softmax(g_s/τ_s), P_t=softmax((g_t−C)/τ_t) (Eq.1); loss=H(P_t,P_s)=−Σ_k P_t log P_s (Eq.2), min over STUDENT only, teacher STOP-GRADIENT (code teacher_out.detach()). Centering EMA (Eq.4): C←m·C+(1−m)·mean_batch(g_t), m=0.9. Defaults τ_s=0.1, τ_t 0.04→0.07 warmup; teacher EMA λ 0.996→1. Official code EXACTLY: softmax((teacher−center)/temp).detach() + −Σ teacher·log_softmax(student). DIFFERENTIABLE via swavSoftCE (reused stable log-softmax) wrapping the stop-grad teacher target. matches nn.DINOLoss(studentLogits,teacherLogits,center [1,K], tauS,tauT)→scalar + DINOCenterUpdate(center,teacherLogits,momentum)→[1,K] + consts DINOStudentTemp/TeacherTemp/CenterMomentum. §V16 tier-1/§V2/V-GRAD: nn/dino_test.go — Formula(==centered+sharpened CE ref @1e-9) + PerfectMatch(g_s=(τ_s/τ_t)(g_t−C) ⇒ P_s=P_t ⇒ loss==H(P_t) teacher entropy — closed form) + CenterUpdate(==m·C+(1−m)·batchmean @1e-12) + Gradcheck(wrt student logits, central-diff @1e-4) + Errors + ExampleDINOLoss. NOTE this iteration first drafted aux-loss-free MoE then found it ALREADY existed (nn.LossFreeBalancer §R? / arXiv:2408.15664) — deleted the duplicate, pivoted to DINO. Teacher-weight EMA handled by existing nn.EMA (weightavg.go), so only the loss+centering added | Caron 2021 arXiv:2104.14294 §3.1 Eq.1-2/Eq.4 + facebookresearch/dino main_dino.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R200 | SnapKV prompt KV-cache compression (Li, Huang, Yang, Yang, Zhang, Cai, Feng, Xu, Chen et al. 2024, "SnapKV: LLM Knows What You are Looking for Before Generation", NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2404.14469); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §4.1-4.2 Eq.2-3/Listing 1 + official github.com/FasterDecoding/SnapKV snapkv_utils.py, NOT /deep-research. PREFILL-time KV compression, distinct from the DECODE-time policies in nlp/kvevict.go — StreamingLLM sink+recent (§R73) and H2O accumulated-attention (§R73): SnapKV uses the OBSERVATION WINDOW (last L_obs prompt tokens, default 32) whose queries' attention predicts which earlier positions matter. (1) Importance (Eq.2) = per-head column-sum of the softmaxed attention over the observation-window query rows, computed over the PREFIX keys only (code attn[...,-window:,:-window].sum(dim=-2)). (2) POOLING (Listing 1): 1D MAX-pool (kernel default 7, stride 1, same-length padding kernel//2) over the prefix importance BEFORE top-k — clusters contiguous important spans (ablation-critical). (3) SELECTION (Eq.3): Top_k(C, budget−window) on POOLED scores among the prefix, and ALWAYS concat the observation window → compressed prompt KV. (4) Per-head, PREFILL only (skips if q_len<budget), not during decode. Pure functions (like H2O/StreamingLLM in kvevict.go). NOTE defaults: paper 32/kernel-7/maxpool; repo constructor 64/5/avgpool overridden to paper values in configs (configurable — kernel is a caller param here; max-pool per paper). matches nlp.SnapKVPool(scores,kernel)→[]float64 + SnapKVKeep(obsAttn [win][seq], budget, kernel)→[]int (ascending kept prompt indices). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/snapkv_test.go — Pool(spike spreads to kernel neighbours, kernel≤1 no-op) + KeepsObservationWindow(last win always kept) + KeepsImportantPrefix(heavy-hitter prefix kept, low evicted) + BudgetSize(\|keep\|==budget, ascending) + UnderBudget(seq≤budget keeps all) + Empty + ExampleSnapKVKeep | Li 2024 arXiv:2404.14469 §4.1-4.2 Eq.2-3/Listing 1 + FasterDecoding/SnapKV snapkv_utils.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R201 | Self-Extend grouped-attention length extrapolation (Jin, Han, Tang, Yang, Fan, Han & Hu 2024, "LLM Maison Longer Than Its Training Length with Self-Extend", ICML 2024, arXiv:2401.01325); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs primary §3.1-3.2 Eq.3/7/Algorithm 1 + official github.com/datamllab/LongLM self_extend_patch/Llama.py + bhavinjawade review, NOT /deep-research. TRAINING-FREE inference length extrapolation, DISTINCT from the RoPE-frequency-rescaling in backend/rope.go (linear PI §R64 / YaRN NTK-by-parts §R66 / xPos §R125): Self-Extend regroups POSITIONS (not frequencies) via a BI-LEVEL scheme. (1) NEIGHBOR attention: for keys with relative distance q−k < w_n (neighbor window), the TRUE relative position q−k (local structure exact). (2) GROUPED attention: distant keys (q−k ≥ w_n) use floor-divided positions ⌊p/G⌋; effective relative position = ⌊q/G⌋−⌊k/G⌋ + (w_n − ⌊w_n/G⌋). The additive SHIFT (w_n − ⌊w_n/G⌋) keeps the mapping CONTINUOUS/monotonic across the neighbor↔grouped boundary (code group_query_position = q//G + w − w//G, group_key_position = k//G). Grouping compresses large distances into the pretrained range → max original length (L−w_n)·G+w_n (Eq.4), no fine-tuning. G, w_n the two hyperparams (G≈4-16, w_n≈512-1024); G=1 ⇒ ⌊p/1⌋=p ⇒ identity/normal attention. Pure integer function (feed to RoPE/relative attention). matches nlp.SelfExtendRelPos(q,k,window,group)→int + SelfExtendPositions(seqLen,window,group)→[seq][seq] causal relpos matrix. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/self_extend_test.go — Neighbor(d<w ⇒ true d) + Grouped(==⌊q/G⌋−⌊k/G⌋+w−⌊w/G⌋) + Monotonic(non-decreasing as k recedes, no boundary jump) + GroupOneIdentity(G=1 ⇒ q−k) + Compression(max relpos ≤ L/G+w « L) + PositionsMatrix(causal, diag 0) + ExampleSelfExtendRelPos | Jin 2024 arXiv:2401.01325 §3.1-3.2 Eq.3/7/Alg.1 + datamllab/LongLM Llama.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R202 | Jacobi (parallel) decoding (Santilli, Severino, Postolache, Maiorca, Mancusi, Marin & Rodolà 2023, "Accelerating Transformer Inference for Translation via Parallel Decoding", ACL 2023, arXiv:2305.10427; the same fixed-point iteration underlies Lookahead Decoding Fu et al. 2024 arXiv:2402.02057 + CLLMs Kou et al. 2024 arXiv:2403.00835); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Santilli §3.1-3.4 Eq.3-5/Alg.1/Prop.1 + Fu §2 Alg.1 + Kou §3.1, NOT /deep-research. INFERENCE acceleration DISTINCT from draft-model speculative (§R53) and Medusa tree (§R195): NO draft model, NO extra heads — it reframes greedy AR decoding as solving the triangular system y_i=argmax p(y_i\|y_<i,x) by Jacobi FIXED-POINT iteration. (1) Update: from an initial guess, y_i^{t+1}=argmax p(y_i\|y_<i^t,x) for ALL positions in PARALLEL each step (one forward pass over the whole block), using the previous iteration's prefix (Alg.1). (2) The fixed point EQUALS the sequential greedy AR sequence — LOSSLESS/output-equivalent (Prop.1). (3) Converges in ≤ m steps (m=gen tokens) via FRONT-TO-BACK locking (position 0's prediction never depends on any y ⇒ correct after step 1; position i locks once y_<i fixed); k≪m in practice = the speedup. (4) Stop when the block is unchanged y^{t}=y^{t-1} (Eq.5) + EOS. Pure function with a model-step closure (like Medusa primitives took precomputed inputs). matches nlp.JacobiDecode(step JacobiStep, prompt,init []int, maxIters)→(gen []int, iters) + type JacobiStep func([]int)[]int (parallel-greedy pass: per-position argmax next token). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/jacobi_test.go — EqualsGreedy(==sequential greedy on a prefix-sum-mod mock LM) + ConvergesInGenLen(iters≤m+1) + FixedPoint(re-applying step reproduces y) + InitIndependent(any init ⇒ same result, only iters differ) + Edges(genLen0/maxIters0) + ExampleJacobiDecode. NOTE Lookahead Decoding (Fu 2024) extends this with an n-gram pool (pure Jacobi rarely does multi-token replacement) — parked follow-up | Santilli 2023 arXiv:2305.10427 §3.1-3.4 Eq.3-5/Prop.1 + Fu 2024/Kou 2024 (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R203 | HQQ — Half-Quadratic Quantization (Badri & Shrivastava 2023, "Half-Quadratic Quantization of Large Machine Learning Models", mobiusml.github.io/hqq_blog + github.com/mobiusml/hqq hqq/core/optimize.py); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs blog Q_{z,s}/Q⁻¹ + optimize.py (lp_norm=0.7, beta=1, kappa=1.01, iters=20), NOT /deep-research. The ONLY CALIBRATION-FREE (data-free) quantizer here — distinct from the activation-aware GPTQ(§R141)/AWQ(§R143)/SmoothQuant(§R172) and fixed-grid NF4: quantizes WEIGHTS ALONE. Asymmetric affine W_q=round(W/s+z) clamp[0,2^b−1], dequant Ŵ=s·(W_q−z) per group. KEY: scale s FIXED from the group range ((max−min)/(2^b−1)), only the ZERO-POINT z optimized, minimizing a ROBUST Lp-norm (p<1, default 0.7) of the error — outliers don't drag z off the bulk. HALF-QUADRATIC split (aux var W_e): alternate (a) shrink_lp(x,β)=sign(x)·relu(\|x\|−(1/β)\|x\|^(p−1)) [p=1 ⇒ soft-threshold], (b) closed-form z=mean_group(W_q−(W−W_e)/s); recompute W_q=round(W/s+z) each step; β←κ·β (κ=1.01, β_0=1, iters=20). Code uses reciprocal-scale (algebraically identical). Pure-f64 data-free PTQ (0 iters = round-to-nearest). matches nn.HQQuantize(w, bits, groupSize, ...opt)→(codes,scale,zero) + DequantizeHQQ + WithHQQLpNorm/Iters. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/hqq_test.go — RoundTripFormula(ŵ==s(code−z) @1e-12) + CodesInRange([0,2^b−1] for b∈2,3,4,8) + BeatsRTN(Lp-error < RTN/0-iters on log-normal+outlier data — THE robust benefit) + ConstantGroup(s=0 guard) + Groups(per-group scale/zero + partial last group) + ExampleHQQuantize; hqq_internal_test.go ShrinkLp(prox formula parity, p=1 soft-threshold + p=0.7) | Badri 2023 mobiusml hqq_blog + mobiusml/hqq optimize.py (BLOG+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R204 | EDM deterministic sampler / Heun 2nd-order (Karras, Aittala, Aila & Laine 2022, "Elucidating the Design Space of Diffusion-Based Generative Models", NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2206.00364, Algorithm 1, S_churn=0); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Alg.1 + official github.com/NVlabs/edm generate.py (d_cur=(x_hat−denoised)/t_hat; 0.5·d_cur+0.5·d_prime; if i<num_steps−1), NOT /deep-research. FAST diffusion sampling, completes the diffusion track (DDPM SDE §R186 / DDIM 1st-order ODE §R187 / FlowMatch): a 2nd-order ODE solver. Probability-flow ODE in σ-parameterization: dx/dσ=(x−D(x;σ))/σ (D=denoiser; score=(D−x)/σ²). Over a decreasing schedule σ_0>…>σ_N=0: (1) Euler predictor d_i=(x_i−D(x_i;σ_i))/σ_i, x_{i+1}=x_i+(σ_{i+1}−σ_i)d_i. (2) Heun corrector if σ_{i+1}≠0: d'_i=(x_{i+1}−D(x_{i+1};σ_{i+1}))/σ_{i+1}, x_{i+1}=x_i+(σ_{i+1}−σ_i)(d_i+d'_i)/2 [average of the two derivatives]. (3) LAST step σ_{i+1}=0 skips the corrector (Euler-only, avoids /0). 2nd-order (O(h²)) vs Euler 1st-order (O(h)) → far fewer NFE. Pure-f64 with a denoiser closure (like Jacobi's model-step). matches nn.EDMSample(d Denoiser, x, sigmas []float64, heun bool)→[]float64 + EDMEulerStep + EDMHeunStep + type Denoiser func([]float64,float64)[]float64. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/edm_test.go — EulerStepFormula + HeunExactForLinearDrift(σ-linear x-indep drift ⇒ Heun EXACT @1e-9, Euler inexact — the trapezoidal 2nd-order proof) + ConvergenceOrder(halve step ⇒ Heun err/4 [order 2], Euler err/2 [order 1] on nonlinear drift, analytic reference) + HeunLastStepIsEuler(σ_next=0 no NaN) + SampleHeunBeatsEuler + ExampleEDMSample(linear drift ⇒ exact −7.5) | Karras 2022 arXiv:2206.00364 Alg.1 + NVlabs/edm generate.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R205 | EDM preconditioning + σ-schedule + loss weighting (Karras et al. 2022, arXiv:2206.00364 Table 1 "EDM" col / Eq.5-7); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs official github.com/NVlabs/edm networks.py(EDMPrecond)/loss.py(EDMLoss)/generate.py, NOT /deep-research. The DESIGN-SPACE half of EDM (completes the sampler §R204): wraps a raw network F into a well-conditioned denoiser. (1) PRECOND (Table 1): D(x;σ)=c_skip·x+c_out·F(c_in·x; c_noise), c_skip=σ_data²/(σ²+σ_data²), c_out=σ·σ_data/√(σ²+σ_data²), c_in=1/√(σ²+σ_data²), c_noise=ln(σ)/4; σ_data default 0.5. Keeps network input + training target unit-variance across σ (σ→0 ⇒ c_skip→1,c_out→0 ⇒ D=x; large σ ⇒ c_skip→0). (2) LOSS WEIGHT λ(σ)=1/c_out²=(σ²+σ_data²)/(σ·σ_data)² ⇒ λ·c_out²=1 (uniform effective loss). (3) TRAINING NOISE ln(σ)≈N(P_mean=−1.2, P_std=1.2) [not implemented — one-line caller helper, parked]. (4) SAMPLING SCHEDULE (Eq.5): σ_i=(σ_max^{1/ρ}+i/(N−1)(σ_min^{1/ρ}−σ_max^{1/ρ}))^ρ for i<N, σ_N=0 appended; defaults σ_max=80, σ_min=0.002, ρ=7 (ρ=1 ⇒ linear-in-σ). Pure-f64. matches nn.EDMPrecond(σ,σ_data)→(cSkip,cOut,cIn,cNoise) + EDMDenoise(f,x,σ,σ_data)→D (usable as a Denoiser in EDMSample) + EDMLossWeight(σ,σ_data) + EDMSigmas(n,σ_min,σ_max,ρ)→[n+1] + const EDMSigmaData=0.5. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/edm_precond_test.go — PrecondFormulas(4 coefs @1e-12) + PrecondLimits(σ→0 c_skip→1/c_out→0; σ→∞ c_skip→0/c_in→0) + DenoiseWrapsF(D==c_skip·x+c_out·f(c_in·x,c_noise)) + LossWeight(λ·c_out²==1) + SigmasSchedule(endpoints σ_max/σ_min/0, strictly decreasing, ρ=1 linear) + Integration(EDMDenoise+EDMSigmas+EDMSample finite) + ExampleEDMPrecond(c_skip=0.5 at σ=σ_data) | Karras 2022 arXiv:2206.00364 Table 1/Eq.5-7 + NVlabs/edm networks.py/loss.py/generate.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R206 | Sequence packing without cross-contamination (Krell, Kosec, Perez & Fitzgibbon 2021, "Efficient Sequence Packing without Cross-contamination: Accelerating Large Language Models without Impacting Performance", arXiv:2107.02027); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.1/§3.2.1/§3.2.2/§4.1 + HF packing-with-FA2 blog / DataCollatorWithFlattening, NOT /deep-research. TRAINING-EFFICIENCY data technique: padding every short document to context length L wastes compute; instead concatenate multiple documents into one length-L pack (up to 50–89% padding eliminated, ≈2× throughput). Two corrections give "mathematical equivalence" to unpacked training (§3.2/§4.2 no perf impact): (1) BLOCK-DIAGONAL attention mask (§3.2.2): a token attends ONLY within its own document — token i attends j iff same document AND j≤i (intra-doc causal, cross-doc masked) — no cross-contamination. (2) POSITION IDS RESET per document (§3.2.1): [0,1,2,0,1] not [0,1,2,3,4], each doc sees positions as if alone. (3) Bin-packing (§3.1) fills packs near capacity: paper proposes SPFHP/NNLSHP (99.7% eff); FIRST-FIT-DECREASING is the common baseline (§4.1, implemented here). Pure functions (like the Medusa/H2O utilities). matches nlp.PackSequences(seqs [][]int, maxLen)→(blocks,docIDs [][]int) [FFD, over-long truncated] + DocumentCausalMask(docIDs)→[L,L] additive 0/−∞ + DocumentPositions(docIDs)→[]int (per-doc reset). §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/packing_test.go — PackSequences(≤maxLen, all tokens preserved, FFD ⇒ tight 2 blocks) + DocumentCausalMask(single-doc==causal, cross-doc blocked, within-doc future blocked) + DocumentPositions([0,1,2,0,1]) + PackTruncatesOverLong + PackingIntegration(pos-reset⟺mask-boundary) + ExampleDocumentPositions | Krell 2021 arXiv:2107.02027 §3.1-3.2 + HF packing-FA2 (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R207 | Bradley-Terry reward-model loss (Christiano, Leike, Brown, Martic, Legg & Amodei 2017, "Deep RL from Human Preferences", arXiv:1706.03741 §2.2.3; Stiennon 2020 arXiv:2009.01325; Ouyang 2022 InstructGPT arXiv:2203.02155 §3.5 Eq.1; Touvron 2023 Llama-2 arXiv:2307.09288 §3.2.2 Eq.2); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs InstructGPT §3.5 Eq.1 + Llama-2 §3.2.2 Eq.2 + TRL RewardTrainer, NOT /deep-research. The EXPLICIT reward-model objective of CLASSIC RLHF — the reward model that this library's PPO/GRPO/RLOO optimize against — distinct from the reward-model-FREE direct methods (DPO §R? / IPO / KTO which fold the reward implicitly). A reward model r_θ(x,y) outputs a SCALAR; Bradley-Terry P(y_w≻y_l)=σ(r_w−r_l); NLL loss L=−E[log σ(r_w−r_l)] (chosen−rejected sign, InstructGPT Eq.1). Stable via −log σ(z)=softplus(−z) ⇒ per-pair softplus(r_rejected−r_chosen+margin). Llama-2 MARGIN variant (§3.2.2 Eq.2): L=−log σ(r_w−r_l−m(r)), margin SUBTRACTED inside σ (larger for stronger stated preference ⇒ forces bigger reward gap); margin 0 = plain BT. RM = pretrained/SFT LM with the unembedding replaced by a scalar head; RM→PPO/GRPO pipeline. DIFFERENTIABLE (OpSub/OpAdd/OpSoftplus/OpMean composition). matches nn.BradleyTerryLoss(ctx, rewardChosen, rewardRejected [batch], margin)→scalar. §V16 tier-1/§V2/V-GRAD: nn/reward_test.go — Formula(==mean −log σ(r_w−r_l−m) @1e-9, m∈{0,0.5}) + EqualRewardsIsLn2(r_w==r_l ⇒ σ(0)=.5 ⇒ ln2) + PerfectSeparation(r_w≫r_l ⇒ ≈0) + Gradcheck(both reward vecs, central-diff @1e-4) + GradientDirection(∂L/∂r_chosen<0 push up, ∂L/∂r_rejected>0 push down — THE training signal) + MarginIncreasesLoss + Errors + ExampleBradleyTerryLoss | InstructGPT arXiv:2203.02155 §3.5 Eq.1 + Llama-2 §3.2.2 Eq.2 + TRL (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R208 | Elastic Weight Consolidation / EWC (Kirkpatrick, Pascanu, Rabinowitz, Veness, Desjardins, Rusu, Milan, Quan, Ramalho, Grabska-Barwinska, Hassabis, Clopath, Kumaran & Hadsell 2017, "Overcoming catastrophic forgetting in neural networks", PNAS 114(13):3521-3526, arXiv:1612.00796, Eq.3); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Sec.2/Eq.3 + Huszár 2018 arXiv:1712.03847, NOT /deep-research. First CONTINUAL-LEARNING method here — a training methodology for catastrophic-forgetting mitigation. After training task A to θ*_A, training on task B minimizes L_B(θ)+Ω(θ) with the EWC penalty Ω(θ)=Σ_i (λ/2)·F_i·(θ_i−θ*_{A,i})² (Eq.3): a Fisher-weighted quadratic spring anchoring each parameter to θ*. F = DIAGONAL Fisher information at θ*_A = E[(∂log p/∂θ_i)²] (paper: "from first-order derivatives alone"; explicit squared-grad form = standard impl, Huszár 2018), estimated as the mean squared per-sample log-lik gradient. Properties: high-F (important) params stiffly anchored, low-F free; uniform/identity F ⇒ plain scaled L2; penalty=0 at θ=θ*; multi-task ⇒ penalties accumulate (sum of quadratics). DIFFERENTIABLE penalty (OpSub/OpMul/OpSum composition; scalar-scale via OpAXPY rank-0 zero to avoid rank promotion) + pure-f64 Fisher estimator. matches nn.EWCPenalty(ctx, params,refParams,fisher []*T, lambda)→scalar + EWCFisher(gradSamples [][]*T)→[]*T. §V16 tier-1/§V2/V-GRAD: nn/ewc_test.go — PenaltyFormula(==(λ/2)ΣF(θ−θ*)² @1e-9) + UniformFisherIsL2 + AtOptimumIsZero + FisherWeighting(high-F penalized ∝ F — THE protection) + Gradcheck(central-diff @1e-4) + Fisher(mean squared grad) + Errors + ExampleEWCPenalty. NOTE: a panic surfaced a real production bug — final OpMul(scalar, scalarTensor[1,1]) PROMOTED the rank-0 result to [1,1]; fixed with the InfoNCE OpAXPY-rank-0-zero idiom | Kirkpatrick 2017 arXiv:1612.00796 Eq.3 + Huszár 2018 (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R209 | FIM — Fill-in-the-Middle training-data transform (Bavarian, Jun, Tezak, Schulman, McLeavey, Tworek & Chen 2022, "Efficient Training of Language Models to Fill in the Middle", arXiv:2207.14255; the recipe behind Codex/StarCoder/Code Llama/DeepSeek-Coder infilling); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3/§3.1 + Appendix D (SPM variant-2) + Code Llama arXiv:2308.12950 §2.3, NOT /deep-research. Teaches a left-to-right causal LM to INFILL by REORDERING a fraction of training documents: (1) split doc into prefix/middle/suffix at TWO uniform-random points (doc=prefix+middle+suffix). (2) PSM: `<PRE>` prefix `<SUF>` suffix `<MID>` middle (`<EOT>`). (3) SPM (Appendix-D VARIANT 2, the adopted/Code-Llama form): `<PRE>` `<SUF>` suffix `<MID>` prefix middle — PRE/SUF adjacent, then suffix, MID, prefix+middle (the §3.1 abstract's `<SUF>`suffix`<PRE>`prefix`<MID>`middle is variant-1, rejected). (4) FIM rate p=0.5 main (0.9 Code Llama), joint 50/50 PSM/SPM (rate = caller/training-loop concern, not implemented here). (5) "FIM-for-free": no L2R harm + LOSSLESSLY REVERSIBLE via sentinels. Pure functions (like packing/H2O). matches nlp.FIMTransform(tokens, s FIMSentinels{Pre,Suf,Mid}, spm bool, rng)→[]int + FIMReconstruct(fim, s, spm)→(doc,ok) [exact inverse]. §V15 ROUND-TRIP+FUZZ + §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/fim_test.go — TransformPSM(`<PRE>` first, 1 of each sentinel, SUF<MID) + TransformSPM(`<PRE>``<SUF>` adjacent) + RoundTrip(reconstruct∘transform==doc, 20 random splits ×both modes) + Edges(empty/single) + RejectsMalformed(ok=false) + FuzzFIMRoundTrip(native go fuzz, 666k execs 0 fail — §V15) + ExampleFIMTransform | Bavarian 2022 arXiv:2207.14255 §3.1/App.D + Code Llama (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R210 | PyramidKV per-layer KV budget allocation (Cai, Zhang, Yue, Ren, Zhang, Wang, Yin, Zhang, Zhang, Liu, Yang, Wang, Wang & Wei 2024, "PyramidKV: Dynamic KV Cache Compression based on Pyramidal Information Funneling", arXiv:2406.02069 §4.2.1 Eq.1 / §4.2.2 Eq.3); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §4.2.1/§4.2.2 + official github.com/Zefan-Cai/KVCache-Factory pyramidkv_utils.py, NOT /deep-research. Completes the KV-compression stack (StreamingLLM §R73 / H2O §R73 / SnapKV §R200): the LAYER-WISE budget allocation orthogonal to WHICH-tokens. MOTIVATION (§4.2.1): attention is broad in LOWER layers but FUNNELS onto a few tokens in HIGHER layers ⇒ allocate MORE KV to lower layers, LESS to higher — a decreasing PYRAMID. Budget = decreasing ARITHMETIC sequence, TOTAL CONSERVED (Σ_l b_l = uniform total): b_l = b_max − (b_max−b_min)·l/(L−1) (Eq.1), endpoints' mean = avg = total/L conserves it. Min/max (code): top b_min small (paper b_min=k_total/(β·m), β steepness default 20; here minBudget is the direct param), bottom b_max = 2·avg − b_min = (budget−win)*2−min_num. Per-layer tokens then selected by SnapKV observation-window scoring (§4.2.2 Eq.3) at that layer's budget. Pure fn (like SnapKV/H2O). matches nlp.PyramidKVBudgets(numLayers, totalBudget, minBudget)→[]int [minBudget clamped [0,avg]; rounding residue folded into bottom layer for exact Σ]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/pyramidkv_test.go — MonotoneConserved(decreasing, Σ==total, bottom>avg) + Endpoints(top==minBudget, bottom≈2avg−min) + Uniform(min=avg ⇒ flat) + Edges(single layer=total, min>avg clamped) + WithSnapKV(each budget drives SnapKVKeep, ≤budget) + ExamplePyramidKVBudgets([160 136 112 88 64 40]) | Cai 2024 arXiv:2406.02069 §4.2.1 Eq.1 + KVCache-Factory pyramidkv_utils.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R211 | Plackett-Luce ranking loss / ListMLE (Plackett 1975 "The Analysis of Permutations"; Luce 1959; Xia, Liu, Wang, Zhang & Li 2008 "Listwise Approach to Learning to Rank" / ListMLE, ICML; RLHF use: Liu et al. 2024 "LiPO: Listwise Preference Optimization" arXiv:2402.01878 Eq.9/Prop.2.3); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs LiPO Eq.9/Prop.2.3 + Xia 2008 ListMLE + DPO PL→BT, NOT /deep-research. The LISTWISE reward-model loss for learning from K-way RANKINGS — the generalization of the pairwise Bradley-Terry (§R207) to full rankings. Plackett-Luce ranking prob = product of SEQUENTIAL SOFTMAX choices (at each rank, best remaining item chosen by softmax over the suffix): P(π\|s)=Π_k exp(s_π(k))/Σ_{j≥k} exp(s_π(j)). NLL (ListMLE, Eq.9): L=Σ_{k=0}^{K-2} [logsumexp(s[k:]) − s[k]] with s in DESCENDING preference order (k=K term=log1=0). K=2 ⇒ EXACTLY Bradley-Terry −log σ(s_0−s_1) (LiPO Prop.2.3). Uniform scores ⇒ L=log(K!). NOTE InstructGPT uses (K choose 2) pairwise-BT decomposition; PL/ListMLE (LiPO) is the DIRECT listwise instance. DIFFERENTIABLE: suffix-logsumexp via −∞ column MASKING (no OpSlice) + one-hot column select + batch-mean via OpAXPY-rank0-zero (EWC §R208 idiom, avoids rank promotion). matches nn.PlackettLuceLoss(ctx, rewards [batch,K])→scalar. §V16 tier-1/§V2/V-GRAD: nn/plackett_luce_test.go — Formula(==ListMLE ref @1e-9) + ReducesToBradleyTerry(K=2 == nn.BradleyTerryLoss @1e-9 — CROSS-CHECK) + UniformIsLogKFactorial(all-equal ⇒ log(K!)) + RewardsCorrectOrder(descending<reversed) + Gradcheck(central-diff @1e-4) + Errors + ExamplePlackettLuceLoss | LiPO 2024 arXiv:2402.01878 Eq.9/Prop.2.3 + Xia 2008 ListMLE (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R212 | SLERP — Spherical Linear Interpolation for weight merging (Shoemake 1985, "Animating Rotation with Quaternion Curves", SIGGRAPH; the default merge method of mergekit, Goddard et al. 2024 "Arcee's MergeKit" arXiv:2403.13257); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Shoemake 1985 + official github.com/arcee-ai/mergekit merge_methods/slerp.py (DOT_THRESHOLD=0.9995) + John Cook, NOT /deep-research. Model merging DISTINCT from the task-vector methods here (TIES §R? merge.go / DARE dare.go): geometric great-circle interpolation of two fine-tunes' weights. Formula: Ω=angle (cosΩ=(a·b)/(‖a‖‖b‖)); SLERP(a,b,t)=sin((1−t)Ω)/sinΩ·a + sin(tΩ)/sinΩ·b (mergekit: s0=sin(theta_0−t·theta_0)/sin_theta_0, s1=sin(t·theta_0)/sin_theta_0). PROPERTIES: endpoints t=0→a,t=1→b; GREAT-CIRCLE geodesic at constant angular velocity ⇒ NORM-PRESERVING for equal-norm inputs (vs LERP sagging toward origin at midpoint); LERP fallback when \|cosΩ\|>0.9995 (near-parallel, sinΩ→0 unstable). Per-tensor application (flatten, single normalized dot). Pure-f64 merge utility (like TIES), not differentiable; apply per tensor. matches nn.SLERP(a, b, t)→tensor + const SLERPDotThreshold=0.9995. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/slerp_test.go — Endpoints(t=0→a,t=1→b) + NormPreserved(equal-norm ⇒ ‖SLERP‖==‖a‖ ∀t — THE great-circle property; LERP midpoint sags to √0.5) + MidpointBisectsAngle(∠(a,mid)==∠(mid,b) — constant angular velocity) + LerpFallback(near-parallel ⇒ linear, no NaN) + Errors + ExampleSLERP(orthogonal units ⇒ midpoint norm 1) | Shoemake 1985 SIGGRAPH + arcee-ai/mergekit slerp.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R213 | T5 span-corruption denoising pretraining objective (Raffel, Shazeer, Roberts, Lee, Narang, Matena, Zhou, Li & Liu 2020, "Exploring the Limits of Transfer Learning with a Unified Text-to-Text Transformer" / T5, arXiv:1910.10683 §3.1.4/Fig.2); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.1.4/Fig.2 + official github.com/google-research/text-to-text-transfer-transformer t5/data/preprocessors.py (random_spans_noise_mask/noise_span_to_unique_sentinel) + HF T5 docs, NOT /deep-research. The seq2seq DENOISING pretraining objective behind T5/UL2 — distinct from causal-LM and from FIM's causal-reorder infilling (§R209): MULTI-span masking for encoder-decoder. (1) drop ≈density (0.15) of tokens as CONSECUTIVE spans, replace each dropped span in the ENCODER INPUT with ONE unique sentinel. (2) mean span length 3 ⇒ num_noise_spans=round(num_noise_tokens/3). (3) sentinels unique + ordered (<extra_id_0>,<extra_id_1>,…; T5 reserves 100, assigned from vocab END descending — here ascending from sentinelBase, equivalent structure). (4) TARGET = per span [sentinel + dropped tokens] in order + a trailing FINAL sentinel ("Thank you `<X>` me to your party `<Y>` week" → "`<X>` for inviting `<Y>` last `<Z>`"). (5) random_spans_noise_mask: num_noise_tokens=round(n·0.15), num_noise_spans=round(/3), non-overlapping interleaved noise/kept spans. Pure functions (like FIM/packing). matches nlp.T5Corrupt(tokens, density, meanSpan, sentinelBase, rng)→(input,target) + T5Reconstruct(input,target,sentinelBase)→(doc,ok) [exact inverse; sentinel iff ≥sentinelBase]. §V15 ROUND-TRIP+FUZZ + §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/span_corrupt_test.go — Structure(target sentinels==input+1, masked≈15%, input−sent+masked==n) + SentinelsOrdered(base,+1,… both streams) + RoundTrip(reconstruct∘corrupt==doc, 30 random) + Edges(empty/single/all-masked) + RejectsMalformed + FuzzT5RoundTrip(native go fuzz, 300k execs 0 fail) + ExampleT5Corrupt | Raffel 2020 arXiv:1910.10683 §3.1.4 + google-research/t5 preprocessors.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R214 | SLiC-HF Sequence Likelihood Calibration alignment loss (Zhao, Joshi, Liu, Khalman, Saleh & Peter J. Liu 2023, "SLiC-HF: Sequence Likelihood Calibration with Human Feedback", arXiv:2305.10425; the calibration objective originates in Zhao, Khalman, Joshi, Narayan, Saleh & Liu 2022, "Calibrating Sequence Likelihood Improves Conditional Language Generation", arXiv:2210.00045); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs SLiC-HF Eq.3/Eq.4 §2.1 + SLiC-2022 Eq.1/Eq.2 §2.2, NOT /deep-research. The ONLY MAX-MARGIN (hinge) direct-alignment loss here — distinct loss GEOMETRY from every logistic −log σ method (DPO §R48 / IPO §R58 / KTO §R59 / CPO §R121 / SimPO+ORPO §R71 / Bradley-Terry): calibrates the policy's sequence likelihoods with a RANK hinge + a cross-entropy pull toward the SFT target, so the gradient VANISHES once the preferred candidate wins by the margin δ (vs logistic losses pushing the gap to ∞). Formulas (CONFIRMED): L_cal=max(0, δ−logPθ(y⁺\|x)+logPθ(y⁻\|x)) [SLiC-HF Eq.4, hinge, margin δ]; L_reg=−logPθ(y_ref\|x) token-level cross-entropy toward the SFT/reference target; TOTAL L=L_cal+λ·L_reg (λ OUTSIDE the reg term). logPθ is the SUMMED sequence log-prob, NOT length-normalized (SLiC-2022 §3.4/Table 2: calibrated models need no length norm — contrast SimPO/ORPO which use AVERAGE logp). Defaults: margin δ=β=1.0 (SLiC-HF §3.2 "ranking margin β of 1.0"); λ has NO fixed numeric default in the paper (empirical; ≈85% of the gain remains with reg removed) → exposed as an explicit caller param, not hardcoded. Differentiable via ex-composition (OpSub/OpAdd/OpReLU/OpMean/OpNeg/OpAXPY; like BradleyTerry/PlackettLuce, NOT a fused backend op). matches nn.SLiCLoss(ctx, preferred, dispreferred, sftTarget, delta, lambda)→tensor [sftTarget=nil or λ≤0 ⇒ bare calibration]. §V16 tier-1/§V-GRAD/§V-PROP/§V2: nn/slic_test.go — Parity(hand-computed hinge 1.0 + reg 1.25) + MarginSatisfiedIsZero(δ-satisfied ⇒ loss AND grad exactly 0 — THE max-margin property) + GradCheck(finite-diff vs analytic on all 3 inputs) + TrainsPreference(GD step raises logPθ(y⁺), lowers logPθ(y⁻), loss↓) + Errors + ExampleSLiCLoss | SLiC-HF arXiv:2305.10425 Eq.4 §2.1 + SLiC-2022 arXiv:2210.00045 §2.2 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R215 | VeRA — Vector-based Random Matrix Adaptation (Kopiczko, Blankevoort & Asano 2024, "VeRA: Vector-based Random Matrix Adaptation", ICLR 2024, arXiv:2310.11454); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.(2) §3.1 + §3.2/§3.3 + Table 6c + HF PEFT VeraConfig, NOT /deep-research. A PEFT method DISTINCT from LoRA §R27 / DoRA §R70 / IA³ §R122 / adapter / prefix+prompt tuning: instead of training per-layer low-rank matrices, VeRA FREEZES a SINGLE pair of random low-rank matrices A,B SHARED (tied) across ALL adapted layers and trains only two tiny per-layer SCALING VECTORS. Forward (Eq.2, paper column conv): h=W0·x+Λ_b·B·Λ_d·A·x, Λ_d=diag(d), Λ_b=diag(b); here in [in,out] row conv y=x·W + b⊙(((x·A)⊙d)·B). Shapes: A r×in / B out×r (row: A[in,r], B[r,out]); d length r, b length out. INIT (§3.3): A,B Kaiming random frozen/shared/never trained; d init single nonzero const d_init; b init ZERO ⇒ ΔW=0 first pass, starts from pretrained. Default d_init=0.1 (§4.1/Table 6c). TRAINABLE/layer = \|d\|+\|b\| = r+out ONLY (matrices static, regenerable from RNG seed) → ≈10× smaller footprint than LoRA's r·(in+out). Differentiable via ex-composition: OpMatMul + OpIA3 (the two ⊙ per-feature scalings — IA³ VJP gives the d,b gradients) + OpAdd; no new backend op. matches nn.VeRAMatrices{A,B,R} + NewVeRAMatrices(in,out,r,dtype,seed) [shared frozen pair] + nn.VeRALinear{W,A,B,D,Bv,R} + NewVeRA(w, m, dInit)→adapter [d init dInit≤0→default, b init 0] + Params()={D,Bv} + const VeRADefaultDInit=0.1. §V16 tier-1/§V-GRAD/§V-PROP/§V2: nn/vera_test.go — ForwardParity(hand-computed y=[1.5,1.25]) + IdentityAtInit(b=0 ⇒ y==x·W ∀ random A,B,d) + ParamFootprint(Params()={d[r],b[out]}, r+out < LoRA r·(in+out)) + GradCheck(finite-diff into d AND b) + Trains(GD step ↓ MSE) + Errors + ExampleVeRALinear | Kopiczko et al. 2024 arXiv:2310.11454 Eq.2 §3.1 + HF PEFT VeraConfig (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R216 | UL2 Mixture-of-Denoisers pretraining objective (Tay, Dehghani, Tran, Garcia, Wei, Wang, Chung, Bahri, Schuster, Zheng, Zhou, Houlsby & Metzler 2022/2023, "UL2: Unifying Language Learning Paradigms", arXiv:2205.05131 §3.1.1–3.1.3/Table 1); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.1.1–3.1.3 + Table 1 (ar5iv), NOT /deep-research. Generalizes T5 span corruption (§R213) by MIXING three denoisers and prefixing each example with a paradigm token so ONE model learns all modes: R-Denoiser (Regular — standard T5 span corruption, short spans/low rate), S-Denoiser (Sequential — PrefixLM: a SINGLE contiguous span at the sequence END, model conditions on prefix & predicts continuation), X-Denoiser (eXtreme — long spans AND/OR high corruption rate). CONFIRMED standard config = 7 denoisers (Table 1): R(μ=3,r=.15), R(μ=8,r=.15), X(μ=3,r=.5), X(μ=8,r=.5), X(μ=64,r=.15), X(μ=64,r=.5), S(r=.25, single span ≈L/4); mixed with APPROX EQUAL participation (≈1/7 each). CONFIRMED: R & X share the IDENTICAL T5 span-corruption mechanism (contiguous spans→sentinels), differing ONLY in (μ,r); S is a single prefix/suffix split; each example PREFIXED with a paradigm token {[R],[S],[X]} (§3.1.3, mode-switching at inference). Lossless data transform (like T5/FIM) → round-trips. Impl reuses nlp.T5Corrupt for R/X; S emits one trailing span in the same sentinel format; paradigm tokens reserve [sentinelBase, sentinelBase+3), span sentinels start at sentinelBase+3. matches nlp.UL2Mode{UL2R,UL2S,UL2X} + UL2Denoiser{Mode,Rate,MeanSpan,Weight} + UL2MixtureOfDenoisers()→[7]denoiser + SampleUL2Denoiser(mix,rng) + UL2Denoise(tokens,d,sentinelBase,rng)→(input,target) + UL2Reconstruct(input,target,sentinelBase)→(doc,mode,ok). §V15 ROUND-TRIP+FUZZ + §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/ul2_test.go — MixtureConfig(exact Table-1 septet) + ParadigmToken(input[0]=base+mode, span stream disjoint from paradigm ids) + Sequential(S = single trailing span ≈r·n = true doc tail) + RoundTrip(reconstruct∘denoise==doc AND mode recovered, all 7 × 20 random) + Edges(empty/single/all modes) + RejectsMalformed + SampleUniform(R×2/X×4/S×1 of 7 within 0.05) + FuzzUL2RoundTrip(native go fuzz, 4.7M execs 0 fail) + ExampleUL2Mode + ExampleUL2Denoise | Tay et al. 2022 arXiv:2205.05131 §3.1 Table 1 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R217 | Synaptic Intelligence (SI) continual-learning importance estimator (Zenke, Poole & Ganguli 2017, "Continual Learning Through Synaptic Intelligence", ICML, arXiv:1703.04200); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.3 (ω), Eq.4 (surrogate loss), Eq.5 (Ω) + ganguli-lab/pathint ref-impl, NOT /deep-research. A continual-learning importance measure DISTINCT from EWC's Fisher (§R208, nn.EWCFisher): where EWC measures importance ONCE at a task optimum via squared log-likelihood gradients, SI integrates each parameter's contribution to the loss drop ONLINE ALONG the whole trajectory. (1) running path integral (Eq.3, MINUS sign): ω_k = Σ_t −g_k(t)·Δθ_k(t), g_k=task-loss grad, Δθ_k(t)=θ_k(t+1)−θ_k(t) — positive when moving k reduced the loss, approximates the total loss decrease attributed to k. (2) consolidation (Eq.5): Ω_k += Σ_{ν} ω_k^ν/((Δθ_k^ν)²+ξ), Δθ_k^ν=total displacement over task ν, ξ damps tiny displacement. (3) surrogate loss (Eq.4): L̃=L_μ+c·Σ_k Ω_k(θ̃_k−θ_k)², θ̃=end-of-previous-task anchor — IDENTICAL quadratic form to EWC (§R208), so SI's Ω+anchor feed nn.EWCPenalty directly (lambda=2c). ξ TASK-DEPENDENT (0.1 permuted-MNIST, 1e-3 split-MNIST — NOT one universal default; SIDefaultDamping=0.1); c=0.1 or 1 per task. Pure float64 master state (§V10, like SWA/EMA); estimator not differentiable (diff lives in EWCPenalty). matches nn.SI{Params} + NewSI(params) + Accumulate(grads) [per step, after optimizer, task-loss grads] + Consolidate(xi) [task end; xi≤0→SIDefaultDamping] + Importance()→Ω tensors + RefParams()→anchor tensors + const SIDefaultDamping=0.1. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/si_test.go — Parity(hand 2-param/2-step: ω=[1.5,−0.5], Ω=[1.5/1.1,−5.0] incl. NEGATIVE Ω to prove path-integral sign, anchor=[1,0]) + MultiTask(Ω sums across tasks, anchor advances to latest) + ComposesWithEWCPenalty(0 at anchor, hand quadratic (2/3)·0.25 when moved) + DefaultDamping(Consolidate(0)⇒ξ=0.1) + Errors(grad count/shape) + ExampleSI | Zenke et al. 2017 arXiv:1703.04200 Eq.3–5 + ganguli-lab/pathint (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R218 | LAMB — Layer-wise Adaptive Moments optimizer for Batch training (You, Li, Reddi, Hseu, Kumar, Bhojanapalli, Song, Demmel, Keutzer & Hsieh 2020, "Large Batch Optimization for Deep Learning: Training BERT in 76 minutes", arXiv:1904.00962, Algorithm 2); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Alg.2 + ymcui/LAMB_Optimizer_TF official_lamb_optimizer.py + NVIDIA/apex FusedLAMB, NOT /deep-research. The only LAYER-WISE trust-ratio optimizer (LARS/LAMB family) here — DISTINCT from all present (Adam/AdamW/SGD/Lion/Muon/Sophia/Shampoo/SOAP/Adafactor/AdEMAMix/Cautious/ScheduleFree/Grokfast/SAM): layers a per-tensor trust ratio on Adam so very large batches stay stable (enabled BERT large-batch pretraining). CONFIRMED Algorithm 2: (1) Adam moments m=β1m+(1−β1)g, v=β2v+(1−β2)g² WITH bias correction m̂=m/(1−β1^t), v̂=v/(1−β2^t); (2) ratio r=m̂/(√v̂+ε) [ε OUTSIDE sqrt], per-layer update dir u=r+λθ (decoupled wd ADDED into the direction); (3) LAYER-WISE trust ratio φ(‖θ‖)/‖u‖ per parameter TENSOR (whole-tensor L2 norms), φ=identity (paper permits clamp min{max{z,γl},γu}; refs default identity); (4) θ ← θ − lr·(φ(‖θ‖)/‖u‖)·u; (5) trust ratio → 1.0 when ‖θ‖=0 OR ‖u‖=0 (no scaling — from apex/TF refs, not paper pseudocode). Defaults ε=1e-6 (not Adam's 1e-8), β1=0.9, β2=0.999 (paper λ=0.01; here wd default 0 per codebase convention, caller-set). Master state float64 (§V10). Mirrors Adam/Lion house style (functional options). matches nn.LAMB{Params,LR,Beta1,Beta2,Eps,WeightDecay} + NewLAMB(params,lr,opts...) + LAMBOption + WithLAMBBetas/WithLAMBEps/WithLAMBWeightDecay + Step(GradFn). §V16 tier-1/§V-PROP/§V2: nn/lamb_test.go — Parity(differential vs independent scalar reimpl over 3-step trajectory, wd≠0, tol 1e-12) + TrustRatioProperty(THE hallmark: wd=0 ⇒ ‖Δθ‖ EXACTLY lr·‖θ‖ INDEPENDENT of gradient scale ×{1,100,0.01} — trust ratio cancels ‖u‖) + TrainingDecreasesLoss(linear+MSE 6 steps) + ZeroWeightFallback(‖θ‖=0 ⇒ trust 1, θ←θ−lr·u hand-checked) + Edge(nil grad skipped, shape mismatch errors) + ExampleLAMB(‖Δθ‖=lr·‖θ‖) | You et al. 2020 arXiv:1904.00962 Alg.2 + ymcui TF/NVIDIA apex refs (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R219 | LLM watermarking — red-green soft watermark + z-score detector (Kirchenbauer, Geiping, Wen, Katz, Miers & Goldstein 2023, "A Watermark for Large Language Models", ICML, arXiv:2301.10226); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Algorithm 2 (soft gen) + Eq.3 §4 (z-score) + official github.com/jwkirchenbauer/lm-watermarking, NOT /deep-research. A provenance/detection capability (new category — not sampling/contrast/beam): biases generation toward a per-step pseudo-random vocab subset only a key holder can reconstruct, detectable WITHOUT the model. (1) SOFT generation (Alg.2): seed a PRNG from a hash of the PREVIOUS token + secret key, partition V into a green list G of size ⌊γ\|V\|⌋ and red R, ADD constant δ to green-list LOGITS before softmax (soft = biases, does not forbid red, vs hard watermark). (2) z-score DETECTION (§4 Eq.3): count green tokens \|s\|_G (each scored vs the list seeded by its predecessor), z=(\|s\|_G−γT)/√(Tγ(1−γ)); null H0 = each token green w.p. γ (Binomial); flag when z>threshold. (3) z>4 ⇒ one-sided FPR≈3e−5 (WatermarkZThreshold=4.0). (4) Defaults δ=2.0; γ=0.25 (official-repo recommended default; paper headline analysis uses γ=0.5). (5) green list = DETERMINISTIC PRF of prev token+key ⇒ generation & detection recompute the identical partition (round-trip). Prev-token hash is an impl choice (paper leaves PRF open) — here a PCG stream keyed by (Key, prevToken); green via partial Fisher–Yates. matches nlp.Watermark{VocabSize,Gamma,Delta,Key} + NewWatermark(vocab,opts...) + WatermarkOption + WithWatermarkGamma/Delta/Key + GreenMask(prev)→[]bool + BiasLogits(logits,prev)→[]float64 + Detect(tokens)→(z,green,scored) + IsWatermarked(tokens) + const WatermarkZThreshold=4.0. §V15 round-trip(embed→detect)+FUZZ + §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/watermark_test.go — GreenMaskDeterministic(reproducible, size ⌊γ\|V\|⌋, prev-dependent) + BiasFavorsGreen(+δ green/0 red exact) + DetectZScoreExact(all-green T=16 γ=.5 ⇒ z EXACTLY 4.0; all-red ⇒ −4.0; long green flagged) + DetectFormula(independent green recount + closed-form z) + Edges(empty/single ⇒ 0) + Errors(vocab≤0, γ∉(0,1), logits len) + FuzzWatermark(1.7M execs 0 fail: detector determinism + z-formula invariant + embed→detect fires) + ExampleWatermark | Kirchenbauer et al. 2023 arXiv:2301.10226 Alg.2/Eq.3 + jwkirchenbauer/lm-watermarking (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R220 | Diverse Beam Search (DBS) — group-diversity decoding (Vijayakumar, Cogswell, Selvaraju, Sun, Lee, Crandall & Batra 2018, "Diverse Beam Search: Decoding Diverse Solutions from Neural Sequence Models", AAAI, arXiv:1610.02424); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Algorithm 1 / Eq.3 / §5.1 + HF HammingDiversityLogitsProcessor, NOT /deep-research. Extends plain beam search (§R54, nlp.BeamSearch) which returns near-identical hypotheses: (1) partition B=width beams into G=groups groups of B'=B/G, decode groups SEQUENTIALLY at each time step; (2) within group g at step t, augmented = Σlogp + λ·Δ(token), Δ vs tokens picked by EARLIER groups 1..g−1 at THIS step; (3) default Δ = HAMMING diversity = −(# earlier groups that selected token this step) ⇒ augmented subtracts λ·count; (4) each group runs ordinary beam search over the augmented scores, keeps its own top B'; (5) λ (diversity strength) has NO single canonical default — paper grid-searches, reports 0.2–0.8 works well, requires λ>0 for any effect (HF config default 0.0); here λ is an explicit caller param, not hardcoded; (6) final hypotheses ranked by RAW length-normalized log-prob (diversity affects the SEARCH only, not reported scores). Synchronous over maxNew steps; completed (eos) beams carried, not penalized; Wu-2016 length penalty (like BeamSearch). matches nlp.DiverseBeamSearch(next NextLogits, start, width, groups, maxNew, eos int, alpha, lambda float64)→([]Beam,error) [reuses NextLogits/Beam from BeamSearch]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/diverse_beam_test.go — Diversity(B'=1, strong λ ⇒ groups pick DISTINCT top-G tokens {0,1,2}) + NoPenaltyCollapses(λ=0 ⇒ all groups pick same best) + HammingThreshold(EXACT penalty magnitude: logit-gap 0.4 ⇒ λ=0.5 flips group-2 to token 1, λ=0.3 does not — since logsoftmax gap == logit gap) + ReducesToBeamSearch(groups=1,λ=0 best == nlp.BeamSearch best, chain 0→1→2→3) + MultiStep(3 groups × 4 steps, distinct leading tokens) + Errors(width%groups≠0, width/groups=0) + ExampleDiverseBeamSearch | Vijayakumar et al. 2018 arXiv:1610.02424 Alg.1/Eq.3 + HF HammingDiversityLogitsProcessor (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R221 | Model Soups — uniform + greedy weight-averaging of fine-tunes (Wortsman, Ilharco, Gadre, Roelofs, Gontijo-Lopes, Morcos, Namkoong, Farhadi, Carmon, Kornblith & Schmidt 2022, "Model soups: averaging weights of multiple fine-tuned models improves accuracy without increasing inference time", ICML, arXiv:2203.05482); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Recipe 1 / p.3 + official github.com/mlfoundations/model-soups main.py, NOT /deep-research. Averaging the WEIGHTS of several models fine-tuned from the SAME pretrained init (different hp/seed/aug) beats the single best model at NO extra inference cost (soup = one model). DISTINCT from SWA/EMA (§weightavg — snapshot averaging along ONE run) and TIES/DARE/SLERP (interference-aware/geometric merges): a plain mean over INDEPENDENT fine-tunes + a greedy selection. (1) UNIFORM soup = unweighted elementwise mean θ=(1/\|S\|)Σθ_i of ALL models. (2) GREEDY soup (Recipe 1): sort models DECREASING by held-out val accuracy; ingredients←{}; for each in order add iff ValAcc(avg(ingredients∪{θ})) ≥ ValAcc(avg(ingredients)); return avg(ingredients). (3) first (best) model always added (empty init). (4) acceptance ≥ non-decreasing; soup recomputed as uniform mean of current ingredients each add. (5) KEY GUARANTEE: greedy soup never underperforms the best individual model on held-out data (by construction). NUANCE: paper text uses ≥; official repo uses strict > on a running-best — here ≥ (matches paper). eval is a caller callback (accuracy of an averaged weight set). Pure float64 mean (§V10). matches nn.UniformSoup(models)→[]tensor + nn.GreedySoup(models, valAcc, eval func([]tensor)float64)→(soup,chosen,err) [reuses TIESMerge-style [][]tensor layout]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/soup_test.go — UniformSoup(mean 9.5/3) + UniformSoupMultiParam(elementwise) + GreedySelection(hand-worked 1.0/5.5/3.0, peak@avg=2 ⇒ keep{0,2} soup 2.0) + SortsByValAcc(highest-valAcc first even when passed last) + NeverWorseThanBest(peakAt2(soup)≥best single — THE guarantee) + Single + Errors(empty/shape/valAcc-len/nil-eval) + ExampleGreedySoup | Wortsman et al. 2022 arXiv:2203.05482 Recipe 1 + mlfoundations/model-soups (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R222 | PiSSA — Principal Singular values and Singular vectors Adaptation (Meng, Wang & Zhang 2024, "PiSSA: Principal Singular Values and Singular Vectors Adaptation of Large Language Models", NeurIPS, arXiv:2404.02948); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3 Eqs.2–5 + HF peft pissa_finetuning, NOT /deep-research. A PEFT INITIALIZATION distinct from LoRA §R27 (random A + zero B ⇒ adapts the weight RESIDUAL) / DoRA §R70 / VeRA §R215: PiSSA SVD-factorizes W and seeds the LoRA matrices from the TOP-r PRINCIPAL components, so training updates the high-singular-value directions (which carry most of the weight) → faster convergence, better optimum. (Eq.2/3) W=U·diag(σ)·Vᵀ; A=U[:,:r]·diag(σ[:r])^½, B=diag(σ[:r])^½·V[:,:r]ᵀ ⇒ A·B = U_r·diag(σ_r)·Vᵀ_r (rank-r principal reconstruction; √σ split EVENLY between A,B). (Eq.4) frozen residual W_res=W−A·B=U[:,r:]·diag(σ[r:])·V[:,r:]ᵀ. (Eq.5) forward Y=X(W_res+A·B)=XW at init ⇒ identity-like start; only A,B train; NO α/r scaling (scale 1), UNLIKE LoRA. Reuses OpSVD (ops.SVD, a=U·diag(s)·Vᵀ, m≥n; in<out handled by decomposing Wᵀ and swapping factor roles W=V·diag(σ)·Uᵀ). Returns a *LoRALinear (W=W_res frozen, A[in,r], B[r,out], Alpha=r ⇒ α/r=1) so LoRALinear.Forward/Params apply unchanged. matches nn.NewPiSSA(w, r)→*LoRALinear. §V16 tier-1/§V-PROP/§V2: nn/pissa_test.go — IdentityAtInit(W_res+A·B=W ⇒ Forward==x·W, [4,3] in≥out) + IdentityWideWeight([2,4] in<out transpose branch) + PrincipalComponents(diag[3,1] r=1 ⇒ A·B=[[3,0],[0,0]] top-1, W_res=[[0,0],[0,1]] — proves principal selection + σ ordering, sign-invariant) + FullRankZeroResidual(r=min ⇒ W_res≈0) + Trains(Params={A,B}, GD ↓MSE) + Errors(rank range, non-2D) + ExampleNewPiSSA | Meng et al. 2024 arXiv:2404.02948 Eqs.2–5 + HF peft pissa_finetuning (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R223 | SimSiam — Simple Siamese self-supervised representation learning (Chen & He 2021, "Exploring Simple Siamese Representation Learning", CVPR, arXiv:2011.10566); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.1–3 / Alg.1 + official facebookresearch/simsiam (builder.py/main_simsiam.py), NOT /deep-research. A non-contrastive SSL loss DISTINCT from the contrastive/redundancy-reduction family here (InfoNCE §R? / Barlow Twins / VICReg / SwAV / DINO): learns from two augmented views with NO negative pairs, NO momentum encoder, NO large batches — a predictor head + STOP-GRADIENT is enough to avoid collapse. Both views → same encoder → projections z1,z2; predictor → p1,p2. (Eq.1) negative cosine similarity D(p,z)=−(p/‖p‖)·(z/‖z‖). (Eq.2+3) symmetrized loss L=½·D(p1,stopgrad(z2))+½·D(p2,stopgrad(z1)); official code: −0.5·(cos(p1,z2)+cos(p2,z1)) with z1.detach()/z2.detach(). (4) STOP-GRADIENT is ESSENTIAL — gradient flows ONLY through the predictor branch p, NEVER through the z it is matched against; without it the loss collapses to the degenerate min −1. Reuses nn.l2NormalizeRows (differentiable per-row L2) + OpStopGradient; ex-composition (no new op). matches nn.SimSiamLoss(ctx, p1, z1, p2, z2)→tensor [batch,dim each; batch-mean, range [−1,1]]. §V16 tier-1/§V-GRAD/§V2: nn/simsiam_test.go — ValueParity(hand −0.65355339 from cos .6/.7071) + PerfectAlignment(identical ⇒ −1 bound) + StopGradient(THE property: grad(z1),grad(z2) nil/zero; grad(p1),grad(p2) nonzero) + GradCheck(finite-diff into p1 branch) + Symmetry(swap views invariant) + Errors(shape, rank-1) + ExampleSimSiamLoss | Chen & He 2021 arXiv:2011.10566 Eq.1–3 + facebookresearch/simsiam (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R224 | MAS — Memory Aware Synapses continual-learning importance (Aljundi, Babiloni, Elhoseiny, Rohrbach & Tuytelaars 2018, "Memory Aware Synapses: Learning what (not) to forget", ECCV, arXiv:1711.09601); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.2 (importance) / Eq.3 (regularizer), NOT /deep-research. The THIRD continual-learning importance estimator, completing EWC (§R208, Fisher) + SI (§R217, path integral): all feed the SAME quadratic anchor (nn.EWCPenalty) but estimate importance differently. (Eq.2) Ω_i = (1/N)·Σ_n \|g_i(x_n)\|, g(x_n)=∂‖F(x_n;θ)‖₂²/∂θ — the mean MAGNITUDE (absolute value / L1) of the gradient of the network OUTPUT's SQUARED L2 norm. KEY: UNSUPERVISED — uses only the output F, NO labels, so Ω can be estimated on unlabeled/validation/test data (vs EWC's Fisher = mean SQUARED (L2) gradient of the LOG-LIKELIHOOD, supervised). (Eq.3) regularizer L=L_new+λ·Σ Ω_i(θ_i−θ*_i)², θ*=prior-task params — identical to EWC/SI ⇒ Ω feeds nn.EWCPenalty. Function is squared ℓ2 norm ‖F‖₂² (footnote 4, "simplifies math + Hebbian link"). Mirrors nn.EWCFisher (mean g²) with \|g\| (mean abs). Pure-f64 (§V10), not differentiable. matches nn.MASImportance(gradSamples [][]tensor)→[]tensor [gradSamples[n] = per-param grad of ‖F(x_n)‖² for sample n]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/mas_test.go — ImportanceParity(hand mean\|g\|: \|[1,−2]\|,\|[3,4]\| ⇒ [2,3]) + NonNegative(abs ⇒ ≥0) + DistinctFromFisher(g=2 ⇒ MAS 2=\|g\| vs Fisher 4=g² — proves L1≠L2) + ComposesWithEWCPenalty(0 at anchor, hand quadratic 0.62) + MultiParam(2 tensors incl. [2,2]) + Errors(empty, shape mismatch) + ExampleMASImportance | Aljundi et al. 2018 arXiv:1711.09601 Eq.2/Eq.3 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R225 | RSO — Statistical Rejection Sampling Optimization for preference data (Liu, Zhao, Joshi, Khalman, Saleh, Peter J. Liu & Jialu Liu 2024, "Statistical Rejection Sampling Improves Preference Optimization", ICLR, arXiv:2309.06657); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.2 + Algorithm 2, NOT /deep-research. A preference-DATA-GENERATION procedure distinct from all preference LOSSES here (DPO/IPO/KTO/CPO/SimPO/ORPO/SLiC/Bradley-Terry/Plackett-Luce): DPO/SLiC fit a model defined w.r.t. the OPTIMAL policy π*(y\|x) ∝ π_sft(y\|x)·exp(r(x,y)/β) but train on pairs from an arbitrary policy — RSO closes the gap by REJECTION SAMPLING from π* (proposal π_sft, RM-scored candidates), keeping a subset distributed as π* to label into pairs for DPO(sigmoid)/SLiC(hinge). CONFIRMED (§3.2/Alg.2): target π*∝π_sft·exp(r/β); M smallest s.t. π*≤M·π_sft; accept prob = π*/(M·π_sft) = exp((r−r_max)/β); draw u≈U(0,1), accept iff u<exp((r−r_max)/β); best candidate always kept (p=1), rest decay exponentially. β = KL temperature (β→0 keeps only max-reward ≈ best-of-N; β→∞ accepts all ≈ π_sft). NUANCE (research, all 3 agents): Alg.2 recomputes r_max over the NOT-YET-ACCEPTED subset (y∉D_x) iteratively; THIS impl uses the STATIC batch max (the §3.2 acceptance-probability form + a valid rejection sampler, M=exp(r_max/β) bounds the batch) — iterative-M variant parked, documented honestly. Pure reward statistics (no gradient/tensor). matches nn.RSOAcceptProbs(rewards, beta)→[]float64 [p_i=exp((r_i−max r)/β)] + RSOAccept(rewards, beta, rng)→[]int [accepted indices]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/rso_test.go — AcceptProbsParity(hand [e⁻¹,1,e⁻²]) + MaxAlwaysAccepted(argmax p=1, survives 200 passes) + AcceptanceRate(empirical rate ≈ exp((r−r_max)/β) over 40k trials, within 0.02) + BetaEffect(smaller β ⇒ lower sub-max accept: e⁻² vs e^−0.5 exact) + Single(1 candidate ⇒ p=1) + Errors(empty, β≤0) + ExampleRSOAcceptProbs | Liu et al. 2024 arXiv:2309.06657 §3.2/Alg.2 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
