| R226 | WordPiece subword tokenizer — greedy longest-match encoding (BERT: Devlin, Chang, Lee & Toutanova 2019; wordpiece model of Schuster & Nakajima 2012 + Wu et al. 2016 GNMT §4); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs HF WordpieceTokenizer + google-research/bert tokenization.py, NOT /deep-research. Completes the tokenizer set: byte-BPE (GPT-2/Llama-3, nlp.Tokenizer §T37) + Unigram/SentencePiece (Llama/Mistral/T5, nlp.Unigram §R87) + WordPiece (BERT). ENCODING = GREEDY LONGEST-MATCH-FIRST (MaxMatch): per pre-tokenized word, take the LONGEST prefix in vocab, emit, continue from suffix (inner loop shrinks end). (2) '##' CONTINUATION: non-leading pieces (start>0) looked up as "##"+substr, leading bare ("playing"{play,##ing}→[play,##ing]). (3) WHOLE-WORD UNK: if any position matches nothing down to 1 char, the ENTIRE word → one [UNK] (NOT partial). (4) max_input_chars_per_word default 100 (HF/BERT; google-research/bert current uses 200 — chose 100 per research) → over-long word → [UNK] untokenized. (5) DECODE: concat, strip "##" from continuations, join words with spaces. Distinct from BPE (merges) & Unigram (Viterbi): pure greedy maximum-matching. Basic pretok/normalization upstream (documented, like Unigram NFKC). matches nlp.WordPiece{vocab} via NewWordPiece([]string, opts…) + WithWordPieceUnk/Continuation/MaxChars (§C12) + Encode/Decode. §V15 round-trip+FUZZ + §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/wordpiece_test.go — Playing([play,##ing]) + MaxMatch(unaffable→un/##aff/##able) + LongestPreferred(una over un — proves LONGEST not shortest) + WholeWordUnk(fail after ##aff ⇒ [UNK] whole word) + MaxChars(>limit ⇒ [UNK]) + MultiWord + RoundTrip(full char vocab ⇒ Decode∘Encode==space-normalized) + Errors(empty/dup) + FuzzWordPieceRoundTrip(950k execs 0 fail; over-long-word→[UNK] honestly modeled) + ExampleWordPiece. apicheck: nlp.WordPieceOption allowlisted | Devlin 2019 + Wu 2016 GNMT §4 + HF WordpieceTokenizer/google-research-bert (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R227 | DyT — Dynamic Tanh, normalization-free LayerNorm replacement (Zhu, Chen, He, LeCun & Liu 2025, "Transformers without Normalization", CVPR, arXiv:2503.10622); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.2 + official github.com/jiachenzhu/DyT dynamic_tanh.py, NOT /deep-research. A drop-in replacement for LayerNorm/RMSNorm (§norms: layernorm/rmsnorm/groupnorm/deepnorm/qknorm) that uses NO normalization statistics (no mean, no variance, no division) — motivated by the observation that a trained LayerNorm maps inputs through an S-shaped tanh-like curve, DyT reproduces it directly: DyT(x) = γ ⊙ tanh(α·x) + β (Eq.2). α = a SINGLE learnable SCALAR shared across the whole layer (controls the effective input range before tanh saturates); γ (scale), β (shift) = per-channel learnable vectors, exactly LayerNorm's affine. tanh bounds large activations. Cheaper than LayerNorm (no reductions); paper shows it matches/beats LN across transformers. INIT: α₀=0.5 (larger for some LLMs, §7); γ=ones; β=zeros. Fully differentiable via elementwise OpMul(α broadcast)+OpTanh+OpMul(γ)+OpAdd(β) — no fused op. matches nn.DyT{Alpha[1],Gamma[d],Beta[d]} + NewDyT(dtype,d,alphaInit)→(*DyT,err) [alphaInit≤0→default] + Forward + Params()={α,γ,β} + const DyTDefaultAlphaInit=0.5. §V16 tier-1/§V-GRAD/§V2: nn/dyt_test.go — ForwardParity(hand γ⊙tanh(α·x)+β) + NoCrossChannelCoupling(perturb 1 channel ⇒ others unchanged — THE elementwise/no-statistics property vs LayerNorm's mean/var coupling) + Saturation(x=±100 ⇒ tanh→±1 ⇒ γ·sign+β bounded) + GradCheck(finite-diff into α AND γ AND β) + ShapeAndParams(default α=0.5, shape preserved, Params α[1]/γ[d]/β[d]) + Errors(d≤0) + ExampleDyT | Zhu et al. 2025 arXiv:2503.10622 Eq.2 + jiachenzhu/DyT (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R228 | Focal Loss — class-imbalance training loss (Lin, Goyal, Girshick, He & Dollár 2018, "Focal Loss for Dense Object Detection" / RetinaNet, ICCV/TPAMI, arXiv:1708.02002); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs Eq.4/Eq.5 + torchvision.ops.sigmoid_focal_loss, NOT /deep-research. A training loss DISTINCT from the loss family here (CrossEntropy/CrossEntropySmooth/CrossEntropyZLoss/ZLoss/Distill): down-weights well-classified examples so training focuses on hard ones, addressing class imbalance. FL(p_t)=−α·(1−p_t)^γ·log(p_t) (Eq.5 α-balanced; Eq.4 no-α), p_t = prob of ground-truth class, γ≥0 focusing param, α class-balance. (1−p_t)^γ shrinks the loss of confident-correct (p_t→1 ⇒ factor→0), leaves misclassified near-untouched. γ=0 recovers CE −α·log p_t (EXACT). Defaults γ=2, α=0.25 (RetinaNet best). NUANCE (research, all 3 agents): the PAPER defines focal for BINARY/sigmoid p_t; THIS is the MULTI-CLASS SOFTMAX generalization (p_t=softmax(logits)[gt]) — the standard community extension, NOT a verbatim paper eq (documented honestly). Differentiable ex-composition: OpSoftmax→gather p_t via one-hot→OpLog; (1−p_t)^γ = exp(γ·log(1−p_t)) [no OpPow]; −α·mean via AXPY. matches nn.FocalLoss(ctx, logits[batch,classes], targets[batch] class-idx, gamma, alpha)→tensor. §V16 tier-1/§V-GRAD/§V2: nn/focal_test.go — ReducesToCE(γ=0,α=1 == nn.CrossEntropy EXACT — THE defining property) + Parity(hand −α(1−p_t)^γ log p_t) + DownweightsEasy(confident-correct ≪ 0.05× uncertain at γ=2; γ=2 down-weights MORE than γ=0) + AlphaScales(linear in α) + GradCheck(finite-diff into logits) + Errors(rank-1/target-range/batch) + ExampleFocalLoss | Lin et al. 2018 arXiv:1708.02002 Eq.4/5 + torchvision sigmoid_focal_loss (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R229 | BERT Masked Language Modeling (MLM) masking objective (Devlin, Chang, Lee & Toutanova 2019, "BERT: Pre-training of Deep Bidirectional Transformers for Language Understanding", NAACL, arXiv:1810.04805 §3.1); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.1/App.C.2 + HF DataCollatorForLanguageModeling, NOT /deep-research. The ENCODER pretraining objective — DISTINCT from the seq2seq span corruption (T5 §R213, multi-span) and causal FIM (§R209): single-token IN-PLACE masking with the 80/10/10 corruption. (1) 15% of positions selected at random for prediction (maskProb). (2) of SELECTED: 80% → [MASK], 10% → uniform-random vocab token, 10% → UNCHANGED (mitigates pretrain/finetune mismatch since [MASK] never appears downstream). (3) labels = ORIGINAL token at selected positions, MLMIgnoreLabel(−100) elsewhere (loss only on the 15%; HF labels[≈masked]=−100). (4) special tokens excluded upstream. LOSSLESS: original recoverable from (input, labels) — masked positions carry originals in labels, rest unchanged in input. Pure function (like T5/FIM/packing). matches nlp.MLMMask(tokens, maskProb, maskID, vocabSize, rng)→(input,labels) + MLMReconstruct(input,labels)→(doc,ok) [exact inverse] + const MLMIgnoreLabel=−100. §V15 ROUND-TRIP+FUZZ + §V16 tier-1/§V2: nlp/mlm_test.go — SelectionRate(≈15% over 20k, ±0.01) + Split801010(≈80/10/10 over 40k selected, maskID=0 disjoint, ±0.02) + Labels(label=orig at selected, input unchanged at non-selected) + RoundTrip(reconstruct∘mask==doc, 50 random) + Edges(empty/single) + Errors(len mismatch⇒ok=false) + FuzzMLMRoundTrip(1.27M execs 0 fail) + ExampleMLMMask | Devlin et al. 2019 arXiv:1810.04805 §3.1 + HF DataCollatorForLanguageModeling (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R230 | Sinusoidal positional encoding — the original absolute PE (Vaswani, Shazeer, Parmar, Uszkoreit, Jones, Gomez, Kaiser & Polosukhin 2017, "Attention is All You Need", NeurIPS, arXiv:1706.03762 §3.5); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.5 (verbatim) + Jalammar illustrated-transformer, NOT /deep-research. The classic ABSOLUTE positional encoding — complements this lib's RELATIVE schemes (RoPE §R?, ALiBi §R60, xPos §R125): a fixed non-learned [seqLen,dModel] table ADDED to embeddings. (Eq. §3.5) PE(pos,2i)=sin(pos/base^(2i/dModel)), PE(pos,2i+1)=cos(pos/base^(2i/dModel)). CONFIRMED INTERLEAVED: pair (2i,2i+1) shares freq 1/base^(2i/dModel), sin@even/cos@odd — the paper's eq. as WRITTEN (NOT [all-sin\|all-cos] concat; NUANCE: tensor2tensor CONCATENATES the halves — equiv up to channel permutation, documented). base=10000, wavelengths geometric 2π→10000·2π. Rationale: PE(pos+k) is a LINEAR (rotation) function of PE(pos) ⇒ relative-position-friendly. Pure-f64 deterministic table (not learned, not differentiable). matches nn.SinusoidalPositionalEncoding(seqLen, dModel, base, dtype)→(*tensor,err) [base≤0→10000, dModel even]. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/sinusoidal_test.go — Row0(pos=0 ⇒ [0,1,0,1,…] sin0/cos0) + Parity(hand d=4 freqs {1,1/100} at pos=1: sin1/cos1/sin.01/cos.01) + PairingInterleaved(sin²+cos²=1 ∀pos,pair — PROVES 2i/2i+1 share frequency & interleaved) + Bounded([−1,1]) + ShapeAndBaseDefault(base≤0→10000) + Errors(odd dModel, seqLen/dModel≤0) + ExampleSinusoidalPositionalEncoding | Vaswani et al. 2017 arXiv:1706.03762 §3.5 + illustrated-transformer (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R231 | Triplet margin loss (Schroff, Kalenichenko & Philbin 2015, "FaceNet: A Unified Embedding for Face Recognition and Clustering", CVPR, arXiv:1503.03832 §3.1); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs §3.1–3.3 (loss=Eq.3, constraint=Eq.1), NOT /deep-research. The MARGIN-based metric-learning loss — DISTINCT from the contrastive/redundancy-reduction embedding family here (InfoNCE/BarlowTwins/VICReg/SwAV/DINO/SimSiam): trains an embedding so the anchor is closer to a POSITIVE (same class) than a NEGATIVE (different class) by ≥ margin. Loss (Eq.3): L=mean[‖a−p‖²−‖a−n‖²+α]_+, SQUARED Euclidean, [·]_+=max(0,·) hinge. Zero exactly when ‖a−n‖²≥‖a−p‖²+α (negative ≥α farther). FaceNet L2-normalizes embeddings to the unit hypersphere FIRST (‖f‖=1) — done upstream (documented, like other embedding losses). Default α=0.2 (§3.3). Hard/semi-hard triplet MINING is a separate step (not the loss). Differentiable via ex-composition (OpSub/OpMul/OpSum/OpReLU/OpAdd/OpMean). matches nn.TripletLoss(ctx, anchor, positive, negative [batch,dim], margin)→tensor [batch-mean, diff w.r.t. all three]. §V16 tier-1/§V-GRAD/§V-PROP/§V2: nn/triplet_test.go — Parity(hand a=[1,0]/p=[1,1]/n=[0,0]/α=.2 ⇒ dPos=dNeg=1 ⇒ 0.2) + ZeroWhenSeparated(‖a−n‖²≫ ⇒ loss AND grad 0 — the margin property) + BatchMean(active+satisfied ⇒ (0.5+0)/2) + GradCheck(finite-diff into anchor/positive/negative on active triplet) + Trains(GD step ↓ loss, pulls positive in/pushes negative out) + Errors(shape/rank) + ExampleTripletLoss | Schroff et al. 2015 arXiv:1503.03832 §3.1 Eq.3 (PAPER = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R232 | T5 relative position bias — log-spaced bucketing (Raffel, Shazeer, Roberts, Lee, Narang, Matena, Zhou, Li & Liu 2020, "Exploring the Limits of Transfer Learning..." / T5, arXiv:1910.10683 §2.1); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous verbatim vs HF transformers modeling_t5.py::_relative_position_bucket + §2.1, NOT /deep-research. A RELATIVE position scheme DISTINCT from sinusoidal (§R230, absolute), RoPE, ALiBi (§R60, linear bias), xPos: T5 drops absolute PE and adds a LEARNED per-head SCALAR to each pre-softmax attention logit, chosen by a BUCKET of the relative position (key−query), bias params SHARED across all layers. CONFIRMED bucketing: (bidi/encoder) num_buckets//=2, +num_buckets if rel>0, rel=abs(rel); (causal/decoder) rel=−min(rel,0) (future→0). Then max_exact=num_buckets//2: rel<max_exact → EXACT bucket (+=rel); else log-spaced += max_exact + floor(log(rel/max_exact)/log(max_distance/max_exact)·(num_buckets−max_exact)), clamped to num_buckets−1. Defaults 32 buckets / max_distance 128. Content-independent, position-only (no sinusoids/rotation). The bucket indexes a learned [num_buckets, num_heads] table (caller-owned); THIS provides the exact bucketing (the definitional algorithm). matches nn.T5RelativePositionBucket(rel, bidirectional, numBuckets, maxDistance)→int + nn.T5RelativePositionBuckets(qLen, kLen, bidi, numBuckets, maxDistance)→([][]int,err) [entry [i][j]=bucket(j−i)] + const T5DefaultNumBuckets=32/T5DefaultMaxDistance=128. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/t5_relpos_test.go — Bidirectional(hand HF: {−3→3,−8→8,−100→15,3→19,7→23,8→24,100→31}) + Causal({5→0,100→0,−5→5,−16→16,−100→30}) + Clamp(±1e6 → 31 last bucket) + Matrix([i][j]=bucket(j−i): [[0,17,18],[1,0,17],[2,1,0]] 3×3 bidi) + Defaults(≤0→32/128) + Errors(odd numBuckets, len≤0, too-small) + ExampleT5RelativePositionBucket | Raffel et al. 2020 arXiv:1910.10683 §2.1 + HF modeling_t5.py (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R233 | OneCycle / 1cycle LR policy (Leslie N. Smith 2019, "Super-Convergence: Very Fast Training of Neural Networks Using Large Learning Rates", arXiv:1708.07120; the PyTorch torch.optim.lr_scheduler.OneCycleLR); research-lite CONFIRMED 3-agent unanimous vs PyTorch lr_scheduler.py _annealing_cos/_schedule_phases + Smith paper, NOT /deep-research. A LR schedule DISTINCT from the monotone-decay ones here (WarmupCosine, InverseSqrt/Noam, WSD): the 1cycle policy first RAISES the LR from a small initial_lr up to maxLR over the first pctStart of training, then LOWERS it below the start toward min_lr — large mid-training rates act as regularization (super-convergence). CONFIRMED: initial_lr=maxLR/divFactor, min_lr=initial_lr/finalDivFactor=maxLR/(divFactor·finalDivFactor); cosine anneal_cos(a,b,p)=b+(a−b)/2·(1+cos(π·p)); phase1 anneal_cos(initial_lr,maxLR,step/stepUp), phase2 anneal_cos(maxLR,min_lr,(step−stepUp)/(total−stepUp)). Defaults pctStart=0.3, divFactor=25, finalDivFactor=1e4 (+ momentum cycled inversely 0.95↔0.85, coupled — that half is a parked follow-up, this is the LR curve). NUANCE (research, 2/3 agents): PyTorch places the peak at pct_start·total_steps−1 over 0-indexed [0,total_steps−1] (scheduler-init-at-−1 quirk); THIS uses total=#steps, peak at round(pctStart·total), step total→min_lr — same curve/anchors up to a one-step boundary offset (documented). Pure-f64 (like WarmupCosine). matches nn.OneCycleLR(step, total, maxLR, pctStart, divFactor, finalDivFactor)→float64 [non-positive args→defaults] + consts OneCyclePctStart=0.3/OneCycleDivFactor=25/OneCycleFinalDivFactor=1e4. §V16 tier-1/§V2: nn/onecycle_test.go — Anchors(step0=maxLR/25, peak=maxLR, final=maxLR/25/1e4) + Monotonic(phase1↑/phase2↓) + CosineParity(hand midpoints: phase1@15=0.52, phase2@65≈0.5) + PeakIsMax(global max==maxLR) + DefaultsAndClamp(≤0→PyTorch defaults, step<0→initial, step>total→min) + ExampleOneCycleLR | Smith 2019 arXiv:1708.07120 + PyTorch OneCycleLR (PAPER+REF = tier2 final §V16) | high |
| R234 | online gap audit 2026-07-13 (user directive): swept llama.cpp sampler/server docs, vLLM/SGLang feature state, 2026 architecture roundups (Raschka, Qwen3.5/MiniMax-M2.5 hybrid-attention wave), quant-format guides. VERIFIED NON-GAPS: Gated DeltaNet/GLA/KDA/DSA/MLA/RetNet/SSD all present (the 2026 hybrid wave is covered), min-p/mirostat/typical, speculative+Medusa+Jacobi, KV-quant/SnapKV/Self-Extend, RegexGuide, watermark/DoLa/CFG/contrastive, BPE+unigram+wordpiece+tiktoken, all ggml i-quants+MXFP4. CONFIRMED GAPS → T573-T577 (DRY, XTC, CFG-grammar+JSON-schema, chat templates, safetensors FP8/int dtypes). Out of scope per §G: OpenAI-server, continuous batching, PagedAttention (ADR-0020) |  |  |
| R235 | govulncheck reachability (T578, verified 2026-07-13 vs pkg.go.dev x/vuln + x/tools/go/callgraph/vta docs): SYMBOL level = load pkgs via x/tools/go/packages → build SSA (buildssa) → callgraph via VTA (variable type analysis: global type-propagation graph over-approximating which concrete types/function literals flow into each variable → interface/method-value dispatch resolved SOUNDLY, unprovable-unreachable counts as reachable) → reachability walk from main/init entries → match reached symbols vs OSV. PACKAGE level (-scan package) = SKIPS SSA/callgraph entirely, pure import-graph membership — govulncheck itself ships the downscaled mode. TRANSFER DECISION for our CI selector: package granularity, std-lib only. Reasons: (a) x/tools = end of the zero-dep module (go.sum appears, tidy gate + README claim change); (b) the CI selection UNIT is `go test ./pkg` — even a perfect symbol callgraph still selects whole test packages, so symbol precision only prunes a package whose tests import a changed pkg yet provably never touch any changed symbol — rare here (parity/e2e tests exercise whole backends THROUGH dynamic dispatch); (c) soundness argument survives the downscale: dispatch needs linkage, linkage needs import, imports = our edges (tag-union makes register_*.go edges permanent). Import-graph closure = the -scan package mode of govulncheck applied to test selection |  |  |

| R240 | TurboQuant sub-4-bit KV-quant VERIFIED (research-lite, WebFetch paper+ref-impls, §R234 discipline) vs arXiv:2504.19874 (ICLR 2026) + refs github 0xsero/turboquant + scos-lab/turboquant: all 3 components MATCH the sources. (1) ROTATE Π = QR-of-iid-Gaussian (paper §PolarQuant "QR decomposition on a random matrix with i.i.d Normal entries"); repo default = fast RHT (1/√m)HD, an ACCEPTED O(d log d) equivalent (0xsero/rotation.py lists RHT as option 2) — same orthogonal, inner-product-preserving, near-Gaussian coordinate law. (2) PER-COORD Lloyd-Max on the Beta density → N(0,1)/√d; closed forms b=1 ±√(2/(πd)), b=2 ±0.453/√d,±1.51/√d EXACT-MATCH (boundaries = centroid midpoints, centroid = cond-mean (φ(a)−φ(c))/(Φ(c)−Φ(a))); 0xsero/codebook.py = identical solver on the Beta directly. (3) QJL 1-bit residual r = x−DeQ_mse(Q_mse x), qjl = sign(S·r) one-sided, est = ⟨y,x̃_mse⟩ + ‖r‖·(√(π/2)/d)·⟨Sᵀqjl,y⟩ UNBIASED (0xsero/quantizer.py:212 const √(π/2)/d for dense Gaussian S); repo uses the structured (1/√m)HD sketch → const √(π/2)/√m, a CORRECT sketch-normalization re-derivation (empirically verified, repo TestQJLUnbiasedInnerProduct). Data-OBLIVIOUS core, no calibration. OUTLIER 2.5-bit SPLIT (the §T619 follow-up): the paper §4.3 does NOT specify the mechanism (punts to QuaRot[51]/RotateKV[63]); the ref-impl scos-lab/mixed_precision._detect_outliers = per-CHANNEL RMS threshold (channel_rms>3·median, cap 0.25) in the ORIGINAL coordinate space → DATA-DEPENDENT (calibration/streaming), NOT data-oblivious, NOT post-rotation → it BREAKS TurboQuant's core no-calibration property. GoAI DECLINES the split (grounded ADR-style decision): keep the data-oblivious uniform-bit cache; the 2.5-bit split would sacrifice the main value proposition (applies to any model with no calibration) for ≈0.5 bit. | arXiv:2504.19874 §PolarQuant/§QJL/§4.3 + github 0xsero/turboquant quantizer.py:212 + codebook.py + scos-lab/turboquant mixed_precision.py | high |

| R242 | Aux-loss-free MoE load balancing VERIFIED (research-lite, §R234, WebFetch both papers) vs arXiv:2408.15664 "Loss-Free Balancing" (+DeepSeek-V3 2412.19437): per-expert bias b_i added to the score ONLY for TopK selection (argmax over s_i+b_i, Eq.3/Eq.16); the combine GATE weight uses the RAW s_i (no bias) ⇒ no gradient/magnitude distortion — the crux, verbatim in both papers. b_i updated PER-BATCH by a gradient-free control rule b_i ← b_i + u·sign(c̄−c_i) (raise under-loaded, lower over-loaded expert); default u=0.001 (DeepSeek-V3 γ=0.001 then annealed→0 for the last 500B tokens). Gating=SIGMOID (not softmax), scores NOT normalized before the bias. b_i INIT=0, OUTSIDE the gradient graph (a control-loop buffer, stop-grad, no VJP). 2408.15664 = STANDALONE (no aux loss = its premise); DeepSeek-V3 adds a TINY complementary seq-wise aux loss α·Σf_iP_i, α=0.0001 "just to avoid extreme per-sequence imbalance". Integration: a []float32 bias buffer (len=nExperts, not a Parameter) + a one-line fork in the router's top-K index choice + a per-batch b+=u·sign(mean−count) buffer update; reuses existing soft/sparse/DeepSeekMoE routing, no autograd/VJP change. §T648 verify-first satisfied. | arXiv:2408.15664 §3 Eq.3/Alg.1/§4.3 + arXiv:2412.19437 §2 Eq.16-17 | high |

| R247 | RECURSIVE DECOMPOSITION / STEPWISE REFINEMENT (grounds §C26 decompose half): develop a program by recursively DECONSTRUCTING a task into subtasks, and subtasks into sub-subtasks, until each leaf is directly & simply implementable; design decisions deferred to the level where they are resolvable. The canonical top-down structured-programming discipline. | Wirth 1971 CACM "Program Development by Stepwise Refinement" + Dijkstra 1972 "Notes on Structured Programming" (hierarchical decomposition, correctness by construction) | high |
| R248 | MODULAR DECOMPOSITION FOR INDEPENDENT VERIFIABILITY + DIVIDE-AND-CONQUER (grounds §C26 verify-each-leaf-in-isolation half): decompose a system into modules that can be built, understood & TESTED independently (information hiding = each module's correctness checkable without the whole); the divide-and-conquer algorithmic paradigm = split a problem into independent subproblems, solve each, COMBINE. A leaf small+isolated enough to verify eliminates bugs at that level before composition. | Parnas 1972 CACM "On the Criteria To Be Used in Decomposing Systems into Modules" + Cormen/Leiserson/Rivest/Stein CLRS ch.4 (divide-and-conquer) | high |
| R249 | BOTTOM-UP ASSEMBLY + TEST PYRAMID + FAULT-LOCALIZATION (grounds §C26 assemble half): bottom-up integration builds & tests low-level units FIRST, then composes upward, so each new level rests on already-verified parts. The Test Pyramid = many fast, isolated UNIT tests at the base, progressively fewer integration/e2e tests above (base tests are cheap+precise, top tests broad+slow). Verified components SHRINK the defect search space: a failure at a composed level implicates the NEW composition, not the leaves already proven → cheaper fault-localization, only higher-level tests re-checked. Matches the repo pattern: collapse-test pins a base equivalence @1e-10, per-param gradcheck, e2e only checks integration (T654/T655/T660). | Cohn 2009 "Succeeding with Agile" (test pyramid) + Fowler martinfowler.com/bliki/TestPyramid + Beck 2002 "Test-Driven Development" (unit-first) + Myers "The Art of Software Testing" (bottom-up integration) | high |

KERN-BEFUND: global-workspace-verhalten — "denk an X, schreib Y" -> J-space zeigt X obwohl output Y ist. | R251 | LLaDA-style masked discrete diffusion (Nie et al. 2025, arXiv:2502.09992 Eq.3): forward corruption independently replaces each token with one absorbing `[MASK]` state at level t; a bidirectional transformer predicts original tokens; per-sequence objective `1/(L·t)·Σ_masked CE`. Reverse generation starts all-mask and iteratively keeps high-confidence predictions while re-masking the rest. GoAI maps this to Vocab+1, non-causal GPT blocks, differentiable masked-row gather, and a fixed confidence schedule. Validation: hand objective, mask statistics, sampler schedule, full parameter gradcheck and trained grammar e2e. | Nie et al. 2025 arXiv:2502.09992 Eq.3 | high |
| R252 | Cross-Layer Attention / CLA (Brandon et al. 2024, arXiv:2405.12981): adjacent transformer layers form groups; the leader computes K,V once and followers reuse them while retaining their own Q/output/FFN parameters. Thus KV-cache slots fall from Layers to Layers/Share; Share=1 reduces to ordinary per-layer attention. GoAI uses decoupled OpMHA(q,k,v), omits follower Wk/Wv, and stores one decode cache slot per group. Validation: exact Share=1 GPT collapse, fan-out gradient into leader K/V, cache cardinality, Forward/Decode parity and trained e2e. | Brandon et al. 2024 arXiv:2405.12981 | high |
| R253 | Coconut continuous latent reasoning (Hao et al. 2024, arXiv:2412.06769 §3): one continuous thought is the last hidden state fed back directly as the next input embedding; K thoughts create K additional sequential backbone passes and remain differentiable through BPTT. Curriculum stage k replaces the first k textual reasoning steps with k latent thoughts and supervises only the remaining chain/answer tokens; optional begin/end markers delimit latent mode. GoAI validation isolates the mechanism with H-hop pointer chasing: K=0 exact GPT collapse, full feedback gradcheck, and a pause-token equal-position control proving the gain comes from recurrent state rather than added positions. | Hao et al. 2024 arXiv:2412.06769 §3 | high |
| R254 | BitNet b1.58 QAT (Ma et al. 2024 arXiv:2402.17764; Wang et al. 2023 arXiv:2310.11453): a full-precision master weight is fake-quantized each forward by absmean scale gamma=mean(abs(W)) to effective gamma*clamp(round(W/gamma),-1,1), while each activation row uses absmax int8 quantization; STE passes gradients to the masters. RMSNorm precedes activation quantization. Validation pins the three-value grid/gamma, STE gradients, full-precision collapse, convergence and log2(3) bits/weight. | Ma et al. 2024 arXiv:2402.17764 + Wang et al. 2023 arXiv:2310.11453 | high |
| R255 | Process reward models (Lightman et al. 2023 arXiv:2305.20050; Math-Shepherd arXiv:2312.08935; ProcessBench arXiv:2412.06559): score every reasoning step at its end position with a sigmoid scalar head and BCE against hard/soft correctness labels; solution score is the minimum step score, so the weakest step controls best-of-N. Unlike an outcome reward model, step supervision localizes erroneous reasoning and exposes lucky-correct contamination. Validation uses earliest-error F1 and PRM-vs-ORM/random best-of-8 plus full parameter gradcheck. | Lightman et al. 2023 §2/§4.2 + Math-Shepherd Eq.4-5 + ProcessBench | high |
| R256 | EAGLE feature-level speculative decoding (Li et al. 2024 arXiv:2401.15077 §4.1): a small autoregressive head predicts the next base-model feature from current feature plus next-token embedding; the frozen LM head maps drafted features to tokens and a base pass verifies the longest matching prefix, preserving the base distribution/greedy sequence. Training loss is SmoothL1(predicted feature,next feature)+0.1*CE(predicted-feature logits,next token), not MSE. GoAI ships a linear draft chain (candidate tree is follow-up); validation pins the exact loss including abs(d)>1, full gradients and lossless generation for arbitrary heads. | Li et al. 2024 arXiv:2401.15077 §4.1 | high |
| R257 | Byte Latent Transformer / BLT (Pagnoni et al. 2024 arXiv:2412.09871): a separately trained byte LM creates frozen boundaries from next-byte entropy; local byte encoder pools each span into one patch, a causal latent transformer runs over the variable per-forward patch count, and a local decoder cross-attends patches back to byte positions. Dynamic patch count needs no ragged tensor for GoAI's single-sequence rank-2 path. Validation: entropy/patched-length references, span-mask isolation, stride-1 collapse to byte GPT, all-main-parameter gradcheck and trained byte-LM generation. | Pagnoni et al. 2024 arXiv:2412.09871 §2.3/§3 | high |
| R258 | APOLLO (Zhu et al. 2024): project raw full-rank gradient G through a periodically redrawn Gaussian rank-r P, maintain Adam moments on R=PG, derive each output-channel scale as norm(Adam(R) column)/norm(R column), and apply that scale to raw G; norm-growth limiter caps consecutive update-norm growth at gamma=1.01. APOLLO-Mini uses rank 1, one tensor scale, alpha=sqrt(128). Unlike GaLore/Fira it stores no projection matrix: only its random seed, regenerating P when used. | Zhu et al. 2024 arXiv:2412.05270 Eq.4-5 + Algorithm 1 + §4.2 | high |
| R259 | Q-GaLore has four simultaneous storage/scheduling mechanisms, not merely 8-bit Adam state: block-wise INT8 optimizer states; SVD projection stored block-wise INT4; layer-local lazy refresh whose 5-similarity mean at least 0.4 doubles its cadence; model weights persist as block-wise INT8 and are re-quantized after updates with unbiased stochastic rounding. Official defaults include block 256 and gap 200. GoAI's float Tensor remains a dequantized execution mirror, so paper numerics/stores are testable but a full end-to-end weight-memory claim awaits integer-native Tensor storage. | Zhang et al. 2024 arXiv:2407.08296 §3.1-3.5 + github.com/VITA-Group/Q-GaLore q_galore_projector.py/q_galore_adamw8bit.py | high |
| R260 | Softmax-off-by-one adds a fixed zero-logit no-op candidate: weight_i=exp(score_i)/(1+sum_j exp(score_j)); equivalently append 0, softmax, then drop the sink, allowing total real-key mass below one. Zuhri et al.'s later Softpick is distinct: ReLU(exp(x_i)-1) divided by sum_j abs(exp(x_j)-1), numerically max-shifted with epsilon; it is sparse and not sum-to-one. GoAI's SoftpickAttention intentionally implements the former and documents the naming distinction. | Evan Miller 2023 Attention Is Off By One + Zuhri et al. 2025 arXiv:2504.20966 Eq.1-2 | high |
| R261 | Fira keeps GaLore's SVD low-rank optimizer state but estimates full-rank gradient-update magnitude from the low-rank adaptive update and adds a norm-growth limiter. These two value mechanisms overlap APOLLO's shipped channel/tensor scaling plus the same limiter, while Fira retains SVD/storage overhead; skipping a separate near-duplicate is a C3 product decision, not a claim that the equations are identical. | Chen et al. 2024 arXiv:2410.01623 + Zhu et al. 2024 arXiv:2412.05270 Table 1 | high |
| R262 | LayerSkip self-speculative decoding uses ONE early-exit-trained transformer: first E blocks autoregressively draft K tokens through the shared final norm+LM head; the full model verifies/corrects them. Smaller E drafts faster but less accurately; larger K amortizes verification but lowers acceptance. LayerSkip training = progressive layer dropout + shared-head early-exit loss; ordinary checkpoints may run but need not accept enough drafts to speed up. Meta's simple Hugging Face variant deliberately shares weights but NOT KV cache/execution; GoAI's reference path matches that bounded variant. Greedy equivalence is LayerSkip's stated correctness guarantee; sampling remains target-distributed by GoAI's existing Leviathan accept/reject rule R53. | Elhoushi et al. 2024 ACL arXiv:2404.16710 + github.com/facebookresearch/LayerSkip README/self_speculation + Leviathan et al. 2023 arXiv:2211.17192 | high |
| R263 | Official LayerSkip checkpoints are standard Llama-family causal LMs (Llama-2, CodeLlama, Llama-3/3.2), not custom architectures. Meta's native-HF recipe loads AutoModelForCausalLM, deep-copies with shared parameter identities, truncates only `assistant_model.model.layers[:early_exit]`, and reuses the original final norm+LM head; no auxiliary weights/modules. Thus GoAI LlamaFromHF can load the checkpoints and needs only a true bounded Llama.ForwardExit + the R262 verifier loop. Optimized shared-KV/activation execution remains separate. | huggingface.co/facebook/layerskip-llama3-8B model card + github.com/facebookresearch/LayerSkip README | high |
| R264 | LayerSkip TRAINING (§4.1, Eq.1-6): per-sample Bernoulli drops WHOLE residual blocks; `p(l,t)=S(t)D(l)pmax`, `D(l)=2^(l/(L-1))-1`, `S=1` fuer finetune/continual-pretrain, `S(t)=2^(t/T)-1` fuer scratch. Shared final norm+LM-head scores each enabled post-block residual. Paper-exakte raw exit-weights: frueh `escale*sum(i=0..l)i`, letzter `L-1+escale*sum(i=0..L-2)i`; C normalisiert nur enabled layers. Rotationscurriculum: jeden R-ten exit aktiv, Maske circular pro step → jeder layer 1/R steps, ceil(L/R) unembeddings. GoAI rank-2 Llama = batch-size 1, also Bernoulli pro sequence exakt; keine batch-API vortaeuschen. Torchtune v0.5 bestaetigt shared unembed, per-sample layer-dropout, exponential depth scale und rotational/gradual curricula; PAPER bleibt §V16-Tier-2-Formelautoritaet. | Elhoushi et al. ACL 2024 arXiv:2404.16710 §4.1 Eq.1-6 + meta-pytorch/torchtune v0.5 early_exit_loss.py/layer_dropout.py | high |
| R265 | LayerSkip KVQ self-speculation (§4.3/A.6): draft fuehrt nur erste E blocks autoregressiv aus, speichert deren K/V + post-E residual (`exit_query_cache`); verification uebernimmt diese Residuen direkt in blocks E..L, also ⊥ early-block recompute. EIN asymmetrischer per-layer KV-cache: early layers liegen waehrend draft K+1 rows voraus, late layers holen batch-verifiziert auf. Nach accept/reject Cache auf `committed_len-1` croppen; letztes committed token bleibt naechster Input. Backend-OpMHA causal rectangle nutzt offset `sk-sq`, passt exakt zu past+verification-window. Paper CPU-Ablation: KVQ spart 9-20 ms/token. | Elhoushi et al. ACL 2024 arXiv:2404.16710 §4.3/A.6/Table A7 + facebookresearch/LayerSkip self_speculation/llama_model_utils.py,self_speculation_generator.py + backend/cpu/mha.go | high |
| R266 | PREFIX-KV-REUSE: Decoder-K/V an Position t haengt nur von tokens 0..t ab ∴ identischer Tokenpraefix ist ausgabe-exakt wiederverwendbar; nur neuer Suffix braucht prefill. SGLang RadixAttention speichert token→KV in radix tree, longest-prefix-match + LRU leaf eviction; vLLM APC hash-blockt `(parent_hash, block_tokens, extras)`, cached nur volle blocks und betont: Prefill/TTFT gewinnt, Decode ⊥. GoAI-Scope jetzt: contiguous single-request primitive `Llama.PrefillAppend` — cached prefix + batched suffix via rectangular causal MHA (`sk-sq=past`), kein Radix/LRU/Scheduler/PagedAttention vortaeuschen. Dies ist notwendige Compute-Naht fuer spaeteren multi-request prefix manager und direkt nutzbar fuer Multi-Turn/RAG mit behaltenem LlamaCache. | Zheng et al. SGLang arXiv:2312.07104/MLSys 2024 RadixAttention + vLLM stable design/features Automatic Prefix Caching + backend OpMHA rectangular-causal contract | high |
| R267 | MULTI-REQUEST PREFIX POLICY: vLLM APC identifiziert volle KV-blocks durch parent-hash+exact block tokens+extras, haelt freie cached blocks in LRU und injiziert optional `cache_salt` in ersten block-hash: nur dieselbe trust-group reused, Timing-guess ueber fremde prompts verhindert. GoAI hat contiguous KV statt PagedAttention ∴ kleinste ehrliche Stufe = bounded whole-prompt slots: exact LCP scan nur innerhalb salt, clone shared contiguous KV-prefix, T873 suffix append, entry-level LRU. Das spart compute und isoliert reuse, teilt aber KV NICHT physisch zwischen slots und behauptet weder hash-blocks/radix/refcounts/paging noch concurrent scheduler. | vLLM v0.20.1 Automatic Prefix Caching design (Data Structure/Eviction/Cache Isolation for Security) + vLLM v0.9.1 generalized caching policy | high |
| R268 | PREFIX-LOOKUP POLICY: SGLang RadixAttention organisiert token-exakte Prefixe als komprimierte radix edges; `match_prefix` traversiert nur den Request-Pfad, splittet bei edge-divergence, und leaf-LRU verwaltet eviction. Paper Alg.1 nutzt match length fuer cache-aware scheduling; physische memory savings entstehen dort separat durch KV-pool indices. GoAI-GAP nach Code-Crosscheck: `LlamaPrefixPool.Prefill` scannt C whole-prompt slots und bis L tokens/slot, `insert` scannt C erneut fuer LRU ⇒ policy worst-case O(C·L+C). Ehrliche contiguous-Stufe: salt-separater compressed-radix INDEX mit subtree-MRU candidate + intrusive slot-LRU ⇒ match/touch O(L), eviction O(1), waehrend KV weiter vollstaendig pro slot owned/copied bleibt. Auswahlsemantik MUSS alten Scan exakt reproduzieren: groesster LCP; exact vor strict-shorter; sonst MRU tie. Kein RadixAttention/physical sharing/refcount/paging/scheduler claim. | Zheng et al.
